---
title: Mūlamadhyamakakārikā (Root Verses on the Middle Way)
sidebar:
  label: Mūlamadhyamakakārikā
---

import { Tabs, TabItem } from '@astrojs/starlight/components';

The `Mūlamadhyamakakārikā` ("Root Verses on the Middle Way", also called `Prajñā`) is Nāgārjuna's foundational work of the Madhyamaka school, composed in India around the second to third century CE. In 448 verses in twenty-seven chapters it examines, one by one, the categories through which experience is described, causes, motion, the sense bases, the `skandhas` (collections), the `dhātus` (components), arising and ceasing, action and agent, fire and firewood, bondage and release, action and its fruit, the self, time, the `tathāgata` (thus-gone one), the four realisations, `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) and the twelve links of `pratītyasamutpāda` (dependent origination), and shows of each that it cannot be found with a `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) of its own. What is dependently originated is `śūnyatā` (emptiness), and that emptiness is itself a dependent `prajñapti` (designation), the `madhyamā pratipad` (middle way) between the extremes of eternalism and annihilation (24.18). The verses are compressed to the point of riddles, and were meant to be read with a commentary; each chapter below has its own tabbed section so that commentary can follow it.

:::note[Source]
The Sanskrit is the Digital Sanskrit Buddhist Canon's text, *Mūlamadhyamakakārikā prajñā nāma* (input and proofreading by Miroj Shakya, 2023, supplied by the Nagarjuna Institute of Buddhist Studies, sponsored by the University of the West), https://www.dsbcproject.org/canon-text/content/931/3060, reproduced as it stands except that its `ṁ` is written `ṃ` as elsewhere on this site. Where its spelling is a slip of the input (`kecanaḥ` for `kecana`, `ārambaṇam` for `ālambanam`), the translation follows the word intended and the Sanskrit is left as printed. The translation is literal: each word by its dictionary meaning, the grammar recast only where English needs it, one line per half-verse; the terms kept in Sanskrit are glossed at their first occurrence in each chapter. The Interlinear tab is kavi's word-by-word analysis in the textbook's symbols, checked by hand. The footnotes record the DSBC's slips, the readings of Poussin's edition where they matter, and the places where Kalupahana (1986, from the Sanskrit) and Garfield (1995, from the Tibetan) construe a verse differently.
:::


### §0 Maṅgalam (Benediction)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**0.1** Without cessation, without arising, without annihilation, not eternal, \
not one thing, not different things, without coming, without going[^1] —

**0.2** He who taught[^2] `pratītyasamutpāda` (dependent origination), the auspicious calming[^3] of `prapañca` (proliferation), \
the fully awakened one: him I honour, the best of speakers.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**0.1** anirodhamanutpādamanucchedamaśāśvatam | \
anekārthamanānārthamanāgamamanirgamam ||

**0.2** ya pratītyasamutpādaṃ prapañcopaśamaṃ śivam | \
deśayāmāsa saṃbuddhastaṃ vande vadatāṃ varam ||

</TabItem>

the fully awakened one: him I honour, the best of speakers.

### §1 Pratyayaparīkṣā (Examination of causes)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**1.1** Not from itself, not from another, not from both, nor from no primary cause: \
existents, anywhere, of any kind[^4], are never found to have arisen.

**1.2** Four are the causes: the primary cause, the objective support[^5], the immediate antecedent, \
and likewise the dominant cause; there is no fifth cause.

**1.3** For the `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) of existents is not found in the causes and so on; \
when `svabhāva` is not found, `parabhāva` (extrinsic nature) is not found.

**1.4** Activity does not possess causes, nor is activity without causes; \
causes are not without activity, nor indeed are they with activity[^6].

**1.5** 'It arises in dependence on these' — so these are causes, it is said; \
as long as it does not arise[^7], how, for so long, are these not non-causes?

**1.6** Neither of a non-existent nor of an existent thing is a cause fitting; \
of a non-existent, a cause of what? and of an existent, what is the use of a cause?

**1.7** When neither an existent, nor a non-existent, nor a both existent and non-existent `dharma` (phenomenon) is produced, \
how, this being so, is a productive primary cause fitting?

**1.8** This existent `dharma` is taught as simply without objective support[^8]; \
now, when a `dharma` is without objective support, whence, again, an objective support?

**1.9** When `dharmas` have not arisen, cessation is not possible; \
therefore the immediate antecedent is not fitting; and when it has ceased, what cause is there?

**1.10** Since the existence[^9] of existents[^10] without `svabhāva` is not found, \
'when this is, that comes to be' — this is not possible at all.

**1.11** And that fruit does not exist in the causes, separately or together; \
and how could that which is not in the causes come to be from the causes?

**1.12** Now if that, though non-existent, comes forth from those causes, \
why does the fruit not come forth from non-causes as well?

**1.13** And the fruit is made of the causes, and the causes are not made of themselves; \
how is that fruit which is from things not made of themselves made of the causes?[^11]

**1.14** Therefore neither a fruit made of the causes nor one made of non-causes \
is found; because the fruit is non-existent, whence causes and non-causes?

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**1.1** na svato nāpi parato na dvābhyāṃ nāpyahetutaḥ | \
utpannā jātu vidyante bhāvāḥ kvacana kecanaḥ ||

**1.2** catvāraḥ pratyayā heturārambaṇamanantaram | \
tathaivādhipateyaṃ ca pratyayo nāsti pañcamaḥ ||

**1.3** na hi svabhāvo bhāvānāṃ pratyayādiṣu vidyate | \
avidyamāne svabhāve parabhāvo na vidyate ||

**1.4** kriyā na pratyayavatī nāpratyayavatī kriyā | \
pratyayā nākriyāvantaḥ kriyāvantaśca santyuta ||

**1.5** utpadyate pratītyemānitīme pratyayāḥ kila | \
yāvannotpadyata ime tāvannāpratyayāḥ katham ||

**1.6** naivāsato naiva sataḥ pratyayo’rthasya yujyate | \
asataḥ pratyayaḥ kasya sataśca pratyayena kim ||

**1.7** na sannāsanna sadasan dharmo nirvartate yadā | \
kathaṃ nirvartako heturevaṃ sati hi yujyate ||

**1.8** anārambaṇa evāyaṃ san dharma upadiśyate | \
athānārambaṇe dharme kuta ārambaṇaṃ punaḥ ||

**1.9** anutpanneṣu dharmeṣu nirodho nopapadyate | \
nānantaramato yuktaṃ niruddhe pratyayaśca kaḥ ||

**1.10** bhāvanāṃ niḥsvabhāvānāṃ na sattā vidyate yataḥ | \
satīdamasmin bhavatītyetannaivopapadyate ||

**1.11** na ca vyastasamasteṣu pratyayeṣvasti tat phalam | \
pratyayebhyaḥ kathaṃ tacca bhavenna pratyayeṣu yat ||

**1.12** athāsadapi tat tebhyaḥ pratyayebhyaḥ pravartate | \
phalamapratyayebhyo’pi kasmānnābhipravartate ||

**1.13** phalaṃ ca pratyayamayaṃ pratyayāścāsvayaṃmayāḥ | \
phalamasvamayebhyo yat tat pratyayamayaṃ katham ||

**1.14** tasmānna pratyayamayaṃ nāpratyayamayaṃ phalam | \
saṃvidyate phalābhāvāt pratyayāpratyayāḥ kutaḥ ||

### §2 Gatāgatagamyamānaparīkṣā (Examination of the gone, the not gone and the being gone)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**2.1** The gone is not traversed[^12], first of all; the not gone is not traversed at all; \
the being gone, free of the gone and the not gone, is not traversed.

**2.2** Where there is movement, there is going; and since it is in the being gone, \
and movement is not in the gone, not in the not gone, therefore going is in the being gone.

**2.3** How indeed will the motion of the being gone be possible, \
when a being gone without motion[^13] is not possible at all?

**2.4** For whom there is the motion of the being gone, for him it follows \
that the being gone is without going; for the being gone is traversed[^14].

**2.5** When the being gone has motion, a pair of motions follows: \
that by which it is the being gone, and, moreover, that which is the motion here.

**2.6** When a pair of motions follows, two goers follow; \
for, setting aside the goer, motion is not possible.

**2.7** If, setting aside the goer, motion is not possible, \
then, when there is no motion, whence indeed will a goer be?

**2.8** The goer does not go, first of all[^15]; the non-goer does not go at all; \
then what third, other than the goer and the non-goer, goes?

**2.9** 'The goer, first of all, goes' — how indeed will that be possible, \
when a goer without motion is not possible at all?

**2.10** For whom the position is 'the goer goes', for him it follows \
that there is a goer without motion, for the one who wants the motion of the goer[^16].

**2.11** If indeed the goer goes, two motions follow: \
that by which he is made manifest[^17] as 'goer', and that which, being a goer, he goes.

**2.12** In the gone, one does not begin to go; one does not begin to go in the not gone; \
one does not begin in the being gone; where does one begin to go?

**2.13** Before the beginning of motion there is neither a being gone nor a gone[^18] \
where motion might be begun; in the not gone, whence motion?

**2.14** What gone, what being gone, what not gone is conceived, \
when the very beginning of motion is in no way seen?

**2.15** The goer does not stand still, first of all; the non-goer does not stand still at all; \
then what third, other than the goer and the non-goer, stands still?

**2.16** 'The goer, first of all, stands still' — how indeed will that be possible, \
when a goer without motion is not possible at all?

**2.17** One does not stand still from the being gone, nor from the gone, nor even from the not gone; \
the commencing and the ceasing of motion are the same as going[^19].

**2.18** 'The goer is the very same as the motion' — that is not fitting; \
'the goer, again, is quite other than the going' — that is not fitting.

**2.19** If the goer were the very same as the motion, \
oneness of the agent and of the `karman` (action) would follow.

**2.20** If, again, the goer is conceived as quite other than the going, \
there would be motion without a goer, and a goer without motion.

**2.21** Of which two the establishment, whether by oneness or by difference, \
is not found — how indeed is the establishment of those two[^20] found?

**2.22** The going by which the goer is made manifest, that going he does not go, \
since there is no one before the going; for someone goes somewhere[^21].

**2.23** The going by which the goer is made manifest, other than that he does not go, \
since two goings are not possible[^22] in one goer.

**2.24** A real goer does not go a threefold motion; \
nor does an unreal one go a threefold motion.

**2.25** One both real and unreal[^23] does not go a threefold motion; \
therefore going, goer and what is to be traversed are not found.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**2.1** gataṃ na gamyate tāvadagataṃ naiva gamyate | \
gatāgatavinirmuktaṃ gamyamānaṃ na gamyate ||

**2.2** ceṣṭā yatra gatistatra gamyamāne ca sā yataḥ | \
na gate nāgate ceṣṭā gamyamāne gatistataḥ ||

**2.3** gamyamānasya gamanaṃ kathaṃ nāmopapatsyate | \
gamyamānaṃ vigamanaṃ yadā naivopapadyate ||

**2.4** gamyamānasya gamanaṃ yasya tasya prasajyate | \
ṛte gatergamyamānaṃ gamyamānaṃ hi gamyate ||

**2.5** gamyamānasya gamane prasaktaṃ gamanadvayam | \
yena tad gamyamānaṃ ca yaccātra gamanaṃ punaḥ ||

**2.6** dvau gantārau prasajyete prasakte gamanadvaye | \
gantāraṃ hi tiraskṛtya gamanaṃ nopapadyate ||

**2.7** gantāraṃ cet tiraskṛtya gamanaṃ nopapadyate | \
gamane’sati gantātha kuta eva bhaviṣyati ||

**2.8** gantā na gacchate tāvadagantā naiva gacchati | \
anyo ganturagantuśca kastṛtīyo’tha gacchati ||

**2.9** gantā tāvad gacchatīti kathamevopapatsyate | \
gamanena vinā gantā yadā naivopapadyate ||

**2.10** pakṣo gantā gacchatīti yasya tasya prasajyate | \
gamanena vinā gantā ganturgamanamicchataḥ ||

**2.11** gamane dve prasajyete gantā yadyuta gacchati | \
ganteti cājyate yena gantā san yacca gacchati ||

**2.12** gate nārabhyate gantuṃ gantuṃ nārabhyate’gate | \
nārabhyate gamyamāne gantumārabhyate kuha ||

**2.13** prāgasti gamanārambhād gamyamānaṃ na vā gatam | \
yatrārabhyeta gamanamagate gamanaṃ kutaḥ ||

**2.14** gataṃ kiṃ gamyamānaṃ kimagataṃ kiṃ vikalpyate | \
adṛśyamāna ārambhe gamanasyaiva sarvathā ||

**2.15** gantā na tiṣṭhati tāvadagantā naiva tiṣṭhati | \
anyo ganturagantuśca kastṛtīyo’tha tiṣṭhati ||

**2.16** gantā tāvat tiṣṭhatīti kathamevopapatsyate | \
gamanena vinā gantā yadā naivopapadyate ||

**2.17** na tiṣṭhati gamyamānānna gatānnāgatādapi | \
gamanaṃ saṃpravṛttiśca nivṛttiśca gateḥ samā ||

**2.18** yadeva gamanaṃ gantā sa eveti na yujyate | \
anya eva punargantā gateriti na yujyate ||

**2.19** yadeva gamanaṃ gantā sa eva hi bhaved yadi | \
ekībhāvaḥ prasajyeta kartuḥ karmaṇa eva ca ||

**2.20** anya eva punargantā gateryadi vikalpyate | \
gamanaṃ syādṛte ganturgantā syād gamanādṛte ||

**2.21** ekībhāvena vā siddhirnānābhāvena vā yayoḥ | \
na vidyate tayoḥ siddhiṃ kathaṃ nu khalu vidyate ||

**2.22** gatyā yayājyate gantā gatiṃ tāṃ sa na gacchati | \
yasmānna gatipūrvo’sti kaścit kiṃciddhi gacchati ||

**2.23** gatyā yayājyate gantā tato’nyāṃ sa na gacchati | \
gatī dve nopapadyate yasmādeke pragantari ||

**2.24** sadbhūto gamanaṃ gantā triprakāraṃ na gacchati | \
nāsadbhūto’pi gamanaṃ triprakāraṃ sa gacchati ||

**2.25** gamanaṃ sadasadbhūtastriprakāraṃ na gacchati | \
tasmād gatiśca gantā ca gantavyaṃ ca na vidyate ||

2.1 gataṃ na gamyate tāvadagataṃ naiva gamyate gatāgatavinirmuktaṃ gamyamānaṃ na gamyate

the gone | not | is traversed | first of all | the not gone | not | at all | is traversed | free of the gone and the not gone | the being gone | not | is traversed  
The gone is not traversed, first of all; the not gone is not traversed at all;  
the being gone, free of the gone and the not gone, is not traversed.

and movement is not in the gone, not in the not gone, therefore going is in the being gone.

when the very beginning of motion is in no way seen?

⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(māna)(gamyamāna) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(ta)(gata) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(ta)🆎(na+gata) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨀①(gamana) 🚺⨀①(saṃpravṛtti) ⏏️(ca) 🚺⨀①(nivṛtti) ⏏️(ca) 🚺⨀⑤(gati) 🚺⨀①(sama)  
not | stands still | from the being gone | not | from the gone | not | from the not gone | even | motion | the commencing | and | the ceasing | and | as going | are the same  
One does not stand still from the being gone, nor from the gone, nor even from the not gone;  
the commencing and the ceasing of motion are the same as going.

2.18 yadeva gamanaṃ gantā sa eveti na yujyate anya eva punargantā gateriti na yujyate  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀①(gamana) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) 🚹⨀⑤(gati) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate)  
which | indeed | motion | the goer | that | very same | thus | not | is fitting | other | quite | again | the goer | than the going | thus | not | is fitting  
'The goer is the very same as the motion' — that is not fitting;  
'the goer, again, is quite other than the going' — that is not fitting.

2.19 yadeva gamanaṃ gantā sa eva hi bhaved yadi ekībhāvaḥ prasajyeta kartuḥ karmaṇa eva ca  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀①(gamana) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(hi) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀①(ekībhāva) 🔵⨀🤟⏯(prasajyate) 🚹⨀⑥(kartṛ) 🚻⨀⑥(karman) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(ca)  
which | indeed | motion | the goer | that | very same | indeed | would be | if | oneness | would follow | of the agent | of the action | indeed | and  
If the goer were the very same as the motion,  
oneness of the agent and of the `karman` (action) would follow.

2.20 anya eva punargantā gateryadi vikalpyate gamanaṃ syādṛte ganturgantā syād gamanādṛte  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) 🚺⨀⑤(gati) ⏏️(yadi) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vikalpyate) 🚻⨀①(gamana) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) ⏏️(ṛte) 🚹⨀⑥(gantṛ) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀⑤(gamana) ⏏️(ṛte)  
other | quite | again | the goer | than the going | if | is conceived | motion | would be | without | a goer | a goer | would be | motion | without  
If, again, the goer is conceived as quite other than the going,  
there would be motion without a goer, and a goer without motion.

2.21 ekībhāvena vā siddhirnānābhāvena vā yayoḥ na vidyate tayoḥ siddhiṃ kathaṃ nu khalu vidyate  
🚹⨀③(ekībhāva) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀①(siddhi) 🚹⨀③🆎(nānā+bhāva) ⏏️(vā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨁⑥(yad) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨁⑥(tad) 🚺⨀①(siddhi) ⏏️(katham) ⏏️(nu) ⏏️(khalu) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
by oneness | whether | the establishment | by difference | or | of which two | not | is found | of those two | the establishment | how | indeed | at all | is found  
Of which two the establishment, whether by oneness or by difference,  
is not found — how indeed is the establishment of those two found?

2.22 gatyā yayājyate gantā gatiṃ tāṃ sa na gacchati yasmānna gatipūrvo’sti kaścit kiṃciddhi gacchati  
🚺⨀③(gati) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀③(yad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ajyate) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) 🚺⨀②(gati) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀②(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gacchati) ⏏️(yasmāt) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(gati+pūrva) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(kaścit) ⏏️(hi) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gacchati)  
by the going | by which | is made manifest | the goer | the going | that | he | not | goes | since | not | before the going | there is | someone | something | for | goes  
The going by which the goer is made manifest, that going he does not go,  
since there is no one before the going; for someone goes somewhere.

2.23 gatyā yayājyate gantā tato’nyāṃ sa na gacchati gatī dve nopapadyate yasmādeke pragantari  
🚺⨀③(gati) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀③(yad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ajyate) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) ⏏️(tatas) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀②(anya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gacchati) 🚺⨁①(gati) 🚺⨁①(dvi) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) ⏏️(yasmāt) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(eka) 🚹⨀⑦(pragantṛ)  
by the going | by which | is made manifest | the goer | than that | other | he | not | goes | goings | two | not | are possible | since | in one | goer  
The going by which the goer is made manifest, other than that he does not go,  
since two goings are not possible in one goer.

2.24 sadbhūto gamanaṃ gantā triprakāraṃ na gacchati nāsadbhūto’pi gamanaṃ triprakāraṃ sa gacchati  
🚹⨀①🆎(sad+bhūta) 🚻⨀②(gamana) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) 🚻⨀②🆎(tri+prakāra) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gacchati) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+sad+bhūta) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨀②(gamana) 🚻⨀②🆎(tri+prakāra) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gacchati)  
a real | motion | goer | threefold | not | goes | not | an unreal one | also | motion | threefold | he | goes  
A real goer does not go a threefold motion;  
nor does an unreal one go a threefold motion.

2.25 gamanaṃ sadasadbhūtastriprakāraṃ na gacchati tasmād gatiśca gantā ca gantavyaṃ ca na vidyate  
🚻⨀②(gamana) 🚹⨀①🆎(sad+asat+bhūta) 🚻⨀②🆎(tri+prakāra) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gacchati) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚺⨀①(gati) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(gantṛ) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(gantavya) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
motion | one both real and unreal | threefold | not | goes | therefore | going | and | goer | and | what is to be traversed | and | not | are found  
One both real and unreal does not go a threefold motion;  
therefore going, goer and what is to be traversed are not found.

### §3 Āyatanaparīkṣā (Examination of the sense bases)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**3.1** Seeing, hearing, smelling, tasting, touching and mind \
are the six faculties; the range of these is what is to be seen and so on[^24].

**3.2** For that seeing does not see its very own self; \
since it does not see itself, how then will it see others[^25]?

**3.3** The example of fire is not adequate for establishing seeing; \
together with seeing, it has been refuted by the being gone, the gone and the not gone.

**3.4** When there is no seeing whatever that is not seeing, \
how then is this fitting: "seeing sees"?

**3.5** Seeing does not see at all, nor does non-seeing see at all; \
and let the seer too be understood[^26] as explained by seeing itself.

**3.6** There is no seer, whether not setting aside seeing or setting it aside; \
and when the seer does not exist, whence those two, what is to be seen and seeing?[^27]

**3.7** From the non-existence of what is to be seen and seeing, the four beginning with `vijñāna` (consciousness) \
do not exist[^28]; so how, again, will `upādāna` (fuel)[^29] and the rest come to be?

**3.8** Explained are hearing, smelling, tasting, touching and mind \
by seeing itself[^30]; and one should know the hearer, what is to be heard and the rest.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**3.1** darśanaṃ śravaṇaṃ ghrāṇaṃ rasanaṃ sparśanaṃ manaḥ | \
indriyāṇi ṣaḍeteṣāṃ draṣṭavyādini gocaraḥ ||

**3.2** svamātmānaṃ darśanaṃ hi tat tameva na paśyati | \
na paśyati yadātmānaṃ kathaṃ drakṣyati tat parān ||

**3.3** na paryāpto’gnidṛṣṭānto darśanasya prasiddhaye | \
sadarśanaḥ sa pratyukto gamyamānagatāgataiḥ ||

**3.4** nāpaśyamānaṃ bhavati yadā kiṃcana darśanam | \
darśanaṃ paśyatītyevaṃ kathametat tu yujyate ||

**3.5** paśyati darśanaṃ naiva naiva paśyatyadarśanam | \
vyākhyāto darśanenaiva draṣṭā cāpyavagamyatām ||

**3.6** draṣṭā nāstyatiraskṛtya tiraskṛtya ca darśanam | \
draṣṭavyaṃ darśanaṃ caiva draṣṭaryasati te kutaḥ ||

**3.7** draṣṭavyadarśanābhāvād vijñānādicatuṣṭayam | \
nāstītyupādānādīni bhaviṣyanti punaḥ katham ||

**3.8** vyākhyātaṃ śravaṇaṃ ghrāṇaṃ rasanaṃ sparśanaṃ manaḥ | \
darśanenaiva jānīyācchrotṛśrotavyakādi ca ||

### §4 Skandhaparīkṣā (Examination of the collections)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**4.1** Form freed from the cause of form is not found; \
the cause of form, too, is not seen freed from form.

**4.2** When form is freed from the cause of form, it follows that form \
is without a primary cause[^31]; and there is no thing anywhere[^32] without a primary cause.

**4.3** But if the cause of form were freed from form, \
the cause would be without an effect; and there is no cause without an effect.

**4.4** When form does exist[^33], a cause of form is not possible; \
when form does not exist, a cause of form is not possible.

**4.5** And form without a cause is not possible at all, not at all; \
therefore one should not conceive any conceptions about form[^34].

**4.6** That the effect is like the cause[^35] is not possible; \
that the effect is unlike the cause is not possible.

**4.7** For feeling, mind and apperception, and for the `saṃskāras` (mental constructions), in every way, \
for all existents whatever[^36], the procedure is the same as with form.

**4.8** If, when an objection has been made by means of `śūnyatā` (emptiness)[^37], someone should state a rebuttal, \
everything of theirs, being unrebutted, becomes equal to what is to be proved.

**4.9** If, when an explanation has been made by means of `śūnyatā`[^38], someone should state a criticism, \
everything of theirs, being uncriticised, becomes equal to what is to be proved.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**4.1** rūpakāraṇanirmuktaṃ na rūpamupalabhyate | \
rūpeṇāpi na nirmuktaṃ dṛśyate rūpakāraṇam ||

**4.2** rūpakāraṇanirmukte rūpe rūpaṃ prasajyate | \
āhetukaṃ na cāstyarthaḥ kaścidāhetukaḥ kvacit ||

**4.3** rūpeṇa tu vinirmuktaṃ yadi syād rūpakāraṇam | \
akāryakaṃ kāraṇaṃ syānnāstyakāryaṃ ca kāraṇam ||

**4.4** rūpe satveva rūpasya kāraṇaṃ nopapadyate | \
rūpe’satyeva rūpasya kāraṇaṃ nopapadyate ||

**4.5** niṣkāraṇaṃ punā rūpaṃ naiva naivopapadyate | \
tasmād rūpagatān kāṃścinna vikalpān vikalpayet ||

**4.6** na kāraṇasya sadṛśaṃ kāryamityupapadyate | \
na kāraṇasyāsadṛśaṃ kāryamityupapadyate ||

**4.7** vedanācittasaṃjñānāṃ saṃskārāṇāṃ ca sarvaśaḥ | \
sarveṣāmeva bhāvānāṃ rūpeṇaiva samaḥ kramaḥ ||

**4.8** vigrahe yaḥ parīhāraṃ kṛte śūnyatayā vadet | \
sarvaṃ tasyāparihṛtaṃ samaṃ sādhyena jāyate ||

**4.9** vyākhyāne ya upālambhaṃ kṛte śūnyatayā vadet | \
sarvaṃ tasyānupālabdhaṃ samaṃ sādhyena jāyate ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">
4.1 rūpakāraṇanirmuktaṃ na rūpamupalabhyate rūpeṇāpi na nirmuktaṃ dṛśyate rūpakāraṇam

### §5 Dhātuparīkṣā (Examination of the components)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**5.1** No space whatever is found prior to the characteristic of space; \
if it were prior to the characteristic, it would follow that[^39] it is without a characteristic.

**5.2** And no existent without a characteristic is found anywhere; \
when an existent without a characteristic does not exist, where is the characteristic to proceed?

**5.3** There is no application of the characteristic to what is without a characteristic, nor to what has a characteristic; \
nor does it apply elsewhere than[^40] to what has a characteristic and what is without one.

**5.4** And when the characteristic does not apply, the characterised is not possible; \
and when the characterised is not possible, there is no coming to be of the characteristic either.

**5.5** Therefore the characterised is not found, and the characteristic is not found at all; \
and an existent, too, freed from the characterised and the characteristic, is not found at all.

**5.6** And when an existent does not exist, of what will the non-existent be?[^41] \
And who, differing in nature from the existent and the non-existent[^42], understands the existent and the non-existent?

**5.7** Therefore, not an existent, not a non-existent, not the characterised, nor even the characteristic[^43] \
is space[^44]; the five other `dhātus` (components) are the same as space.

**5.8** But those of little intelligence[^45] who see the existence and non-existence \
of existents do not see the auspicious calming of what is to be seen.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**5.1** nākāśaṃ vidyate kiṃcit pūrvamākāśalakṣaṇāt | \
alakṣaṇaṃ prasajyet syāt pūrvaṃ yadi lakṣaṇāt ||

**5.2** alakṣaṇo na kaścicca bhāvaḥ saṃvidyate kvacit | \
asatyalakṣaṇe bhāve kramatāṃ kuha lakṣaṇam ||

**5.3** nālakṣaṇe lakṣaṇasya pravṛttirna salakṣaṇe | \
salakṣaṇālakṣaṇābhyāṃ nāpyanyatra pravartate ||

**5.4** lakṣaṇāsaṃpravṛttau ca na lakṣyamupapadyate | \
lakṣyasyānupapattau ca lakṣaṇasyāpyasaṃbhavaḥ ||

**5.5** tasmānna vidyate lakṣyaṃ lakṣaṇaṃ naiva vidyate | \
lakṣyalakṣaṇanirmukto naiva bhāvo’pi vidyate ||

**5.6** avidyamāne bhāve ca kasyābhāvo bhaviṣyati | \
bhāvābhāvavidharmā ca bhāvābhāvāvavaiti kaḥ ||

**5.7** tasmānna bhāvo nābhāvo na lakṣyaṃ nāpi lakṣaṇam | \
ākāśamākāśasamā dhātavaḥ pañca ye’pare ||

**5.8** astitvaṃ ye tu paśyanti nāstitvaṃ cālpabuddhayaḥ | \
bhāvānāṃ te na paśyanti draṣṭavyopaśamaṃ śivam ||

</TabItem>

### §6 Rāgaraktaparīkṣā (Examination of passion and the impassioned)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**6.1** If the impassioned, with passion set aside, were before passion, \
depending on it passion would be; when the impassioned exists, passion would be.

**6.2** But when the impassioned does not exist, whence at all will passion be? \
Whether passion exists or does not exist, for the impassioned too[^46] this procedure is the same.

**6.3** Again, an arising of passion and the impassioned together is not fitting; \
for passion and the impassioned would be independent of each other.

**6.4** In identity there is no coexistence, for it is not together with its very self[^47]; \
and then in separateness, whence at all will coexistence be?

**6.5** If there were coexistence in identity, it would be even without a companion; \
if there were coexistence in separateness, it would be even without a companion.

**6.6** And if coexistence is in separateness, is it that, of passion and the impassioned, \
a separate existence of each is established[^48], and hence the coexistence of the two?

**6.7** Or if a separate existence of each of passion and the impassioned is established, \
for what purpose, then, do you conceive the coexistence of the two?

**6.8** "It is not established separately" — thus you aim at coexistence; \
for the sake of establishing coexistence[^49], you again wish for separateness.

**6.9** And since separate existence is not established, coexistence is not established[^50]; \
there being which separate existence, do you wish for coexistence?

**6.10** Thus the establishment of passion is neither together with the impassioned nor not together[^51]; \
as with passion, the establishment of all `dharmas` (phenomena) is neither together nor not together.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**6.1** rāgād yadi bhavet pūrvaṃ rakto rāgatiraskṛtaḥ | \
taṃ pratītya bhaved rāgo rakte rāgo bhavet sati ||

**6.2** rakte’sati punā rāgaḥ kuta eva bhaviṣyati | \
sati vāsati vā rāge rakte’pyeṣa samaḥ kramaḥ ||

**6.3** sahaiva punarudbhūtirna yuktā rāgaraktayoḥ | \
bhavetāṃ rāgaraktau hi nirapekṣau parasparam ||

**6.4** naikatve sahabhāvo’sti na tenaiva hi tat saha | \
pṛthaktve sahabhāvo’tha kuta eva bhaviṣyati ||

**6.5** ekatve sahabhāvaścet syāt sahāyaṃ vināpi saḥ | \
pṛthaktve sahabhāvaścet syāt sahāyaṃ vināpi saḥ ||

**6.6** pṛthaktve sahabhāvaśca yadi kiṃ rāgaraktayoḥ | \
siddhaḥ pṛthakpṛthagbhāgaḥ sahabhāvastatastayoḥ ||

**6.7** siddhaḥ pṛthakpṛthagbhāvo yadi vā rāgaraktayoḥ | \
sahabhāvaṃ kimarthaṃ nu parikalpayase tayoḥ ||

**6.8** pṛthag na sidhyatītyevaṃ sahabhāvaṃ vikāṅkṣasi | \
sahabhāvaprasiddhyarthaṃ pṛthaktvaṃ bhūya icchasi ||

**6.9** pṛthagbhāvāprasiddheśca sahabhāvo na sidhyati | \
katamasmin pṛthagbhāve sahabhāvaṃ satīcchasi ||

**6.10** evaṃ raktena rāgasya siddhirna saha nāsaha | \
rāgavat sarvadharmāṇāṃ siddhirna saha nāsaha ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">
6.1 rāgād yadi bhavet pūrvaṃ rakto rāgatiraskṛtaḥ taṃ pratītya bhaved rāgo rakte rāgo bhavet sati  
🚹⨀⑤(rāga) ⏏️(yadi) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) ⏏️(pūrvam) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(rakta) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(rāga+tiraskṛta) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(tad) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚹⨀①(rāga) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(rakta) 🚹⨀①(rāga) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat)  
than passion | if | were | before | the impassioned | with passion set aside | it | depending on | would be | passion | when the impassioned | passion | would be | exists  
If the impassioned, with passion set aside, were before passion,  
depending on it passion would be; when the impassioned exists, passion would be.

6.2 rakte’sati punā rāgaḥ kuta eva bhaviṣyati sati vāsati vā rāge rakte’pyeṣa samaḥ kramaḥ  
🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(rakta) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀①(rāga) ⏏️(kutas) ⏏️(eva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat) ⏏️(vā) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) ⏏️(vā) 🚹⨀⑦(rāga) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(rakta) ⏏️(api) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(etad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(sama) 🚹⨀①(krama)  
when the impassioned | does not exist | but | passion | whence | at all | will be | exists | whether | does not exist | or | passion | for the impassioned | too | this | the same | procedure  
But when the impassioned does not exist, whence at all will passion be?  
Whether passion exists or does not exist, for the impassioned too this procedure is the same.

6.3 sahaiva punarudbhūtirna yuktā rāgaraktayoḥ bhavetāṃ rāgaraktau hi nirapekṣau parasparam  
⏏️(saha) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(punar) 🚺⨀①(udbhūti) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(yukta) 🚹⨁⑥🔽(ta)🆎(rāga+rakta) 🟢⨁🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚹⨁①🔽(ta)🆎(rāga+rakta) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨁①(nirapekṣa) ⏏️(parasparam)  
together | indeed | again | an arising | not | fitting | of passion and the impassioned | would be | passion and the impassioned | for | independent | of each other  
Again, an arising of passion and the impassioned together is not fitting;  
for passion and the impassioned would be independent of each other.

6.4 naikatve sahabhāvo’sti na tenaiva hi tat saha pṛthaktve sahabhāvo’tha kuta eva bhaviṣyati  
⏏️(na) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(tva)(ekatva) 🚹⨀①🆎(saha+bhāva) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) ⏏️(saha) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(tva)(pṛthaktva) 🚹⨀①🆎(saha+bhāva) ⏏️(atha) ⏏️(kutas) ⏏️(eva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
not | in identity | coexistence | there is | not | with that | very | for | it | together | in separateness | coexistence | then | whence | at all | will be  
In identity there is no coexistence, for it is not together with its very self;  
and then in separateness, whence at all will coexistence be?

6.5 ekatve sahabhāvaścet syāt sahāyaṃ vināpi saḥ pṛthaktve sahabhāvaścet syāt sahāyaṃ vināpi saḥ  
🚻⨀⑦🔽(tva)(ekatva) 🚹⨀①🆎(saha+bhāva) ⏏️(cet) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀②(sahāya) ⏏️(vinā) ⏏️(api) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(tva)(pṛthaktva) 🚹⨀①🆎(saha+bhāva) ⏏️(cet) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀②(sahāya) ⏏️(vinā) ⏏️(api) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad)  
in identity | coexistence | if | would be | a companion | without | even | it | in separateness | coexistence | if | would be | a companion | without | even | it  
If there were coexistence in identity, it would be even without a companion;  
if there were coexistence in separateness, it would be even without a companion.

6.6 pṛthaktve sahabhāvaśca yadi kiṃ rāgaraktayoḥ siddhaḥ pṛthakpṛthagbhāgaḥ sahabhāvastatastayoḥ  
🚻⨀⑦🔽(tva)(pṛthaktva) 🚹⨀①🆎(saha+bhāva) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(kim) 🚹⨁⑥🔽(ta)🆎(rāga+rakta) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(siddha) 🚹⨀①🆎(pṛthak+pṛthak+bhāga) 🚹⨀①🆎(saha+bhāva) ⏏️(tatas) *️⃣🤟🚹⨁⑥(tad)  
in separateness | coexistence | and | if | is it that | of passion and the impassioned | is established | a portion for each separately | the coexistence | hence | of the two  
And if coexistence is in separateness, is it that, of passion and the impassioned,  
a separate existence of each is established, and hence the coexistence of the two?

6.7 siddhaḥ pṛthakpṛthagbhāvo yadi vā rāgaraktayoḥ sahabhāvaṃ kimarthaṃ nu parikalpayase tayoḥ  
🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(siddha) 🚹⨀①🆎(pṛthak+pṛthak+bhāva) ⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(vā) 🚹⨁⑥🔽(ta)🆎(rāga+rakta) 🚹⨀②🆎(saha+bhāva) ⏏️(kimartham) ⏏️(nu) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(parikalpayati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨁⑥(tad)  
is established | a separate existence of each | if | or | of passion and the impassioned | the coexistence | for what purpose | then | you conceive | of the two  
Or if a separate existence of each of passion and the impassioned is established,  
for what purpose, then, do you conceive the coexistence of the two?

6.8 pṛthag na sidhyatītyevaṃ sahabhāvaṃ vikāṅkṣasi sahabhāvaprasiddhyarthaṃ pṛthaktvaṃ bhūya icchasi  
⏏️(pṛthak) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(sidhyati) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(evam) 🚹⨀②🆎(saha+bhāva) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(vikāṅkṣati) 🚻⨀②🆎(saha+bhāva+prasiddhi+artha) 🚻⨀②🔽(tva)(pṛthaktva) ⏏️(bhūyas) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(icchati)  
separately | not | is established | thus | in this way | coexistence | you aim at | for the sake of establishing coexistence | separateness | again | you wish for  
"It is not established separately" — thus you aim at coexistence;  
for the sake of establishing coexistence, you again wish for separateness.

6.9 pṛthagbhāvāprasiddheśca sahabhāvo na sidhyati katamasmin pṛthagbhāve sahabhāvaṃ satīcchasi  
🚺⨀⑤🆎(pṛthak+bhāva+na+prasiddhi) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(saha+bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(sidhyati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(katama) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(pṛthak+bhāva) 🚹⨀②🆎(saha+bhāva) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(icchati)  
since separate existence is not established | and | coexistence | not | is established | which | separate existence | coexistence | there being | do you wish for  
And since separate existence is not established, coexistence is not established;  
there being which separate existence, do you wish for coexistence?

6.10 evaṃ raktena rāgasya siddhirna saha nāsaha rāgavat sarvadharmāṇāṃ siddhirna saha nāsaha  
⏏️(evam) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)(rakta) 🚹⨀⑥(rāga) 🚺⨀①(siddhi) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(saha) ⏏️(na) ⏏️🆎(na+saha) ⏏️(rāgavat) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(sarva+dharma) 🚺⨀①(siddhi) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(saha) ⏏️(na) ⏏️🆎(na+saha)  
thus | with the impassioned | of passion | the establishment | not | together | not | not together | as with passion | of all dharmas | the establishment | not | together | not | not together  
Thus the establishment of passion is neither together with the impassioned nor not together;  
as with passion, the establishment of all `dharmas` (phenomena) is neither together nor not together.

### §7 Utpādasthitibhaṅgaparīkṣā (Examination of arising, enduring and dissolution)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**7.1** If arising is `saṃskṛta` (constructed), the triad of characteristics is fitting for it; \
but if arising is `asaṃskṛta` (unconstructed), how is it a characteristic of the `saṃskṛta`[^52]?

**7.2** The three, arising and the rest, separately are not adequate for the work of a characteristic \
of the `saṃskṛta`; combined, how could they be in one place at one time?

**7.3** Of arising, enduring and dissolution, another characteristic of the `saṃskṛta`[^53]: \
if there is one, thus there is infinite regress; if there is not, they are not `saṃskṛta`.

**7.4** The arising of arising is the arising of the root arising alone[^54]; \
the root arising, in turn, generates the arising of arising[^55].

**7.5** If, for you, the arising of arising is the arising of the root arising[^56], \
how, for you, will it, not generated by the root one, generate that?

**7.6** If, for you, it, generated by the root one, generates the root one, \
how does that root one, not generated by it, produce it?

**7.7** This one, while arising, may well produce that one[^57], for you, \
if this one, unborn, could produce that one.

**7.8** Just as a lamp illuminates itself and another, \
so arising would produce both itself and another.

**7.9** There is no darkness in a lamp, nor where it stands[^58]; \
what does the lamp illuminate?[^59] For illumination is the destruction of darkness.

**7.10** How is darkness destroyed by the lamp that is arising, \
when indeed the lamp that is arising does not reach darkness?

**7.11** Or if darkness is destroyed by the lamp without reaching it at all, \
it, standing here, will destroy the darkness standing in the whole world.

**7.12** If a lamp illuminates itself and another, \
darkness too will conceal itself and another, without doubt.

**7.13** How could this arising, not arisen, generate its own self? \
But if, arisen, it generates: when it is born, what is generated again?

**7.14** Neither what is arising, nor what has arisen, nor what has not arisen, in any way \
arises; so it was explained by the being gone, the gone and the not gone.

**7.15** When this, which is arising, does not proceed to origination, \
how then is it said to be arising in dependence on origination[^60]?

**7.16** Whatever comes to be in dependence, that is at peace in respect of `svabhāva`[^61] (intrinsic nature); \
therefore both what is arising and origination itself are at peace.

**7.17** If some existent that has not arisen is found somewhere, \
it might arise; when that existent does not exist, what arises?[^62]

**7.18** And if this arising produces what is arising[^63], \
which arising, again, would produce that arising?

**7.19** If another arising produces this one[^64], there is infinite regress; \
but if it has arisen without an arising, let everything arise in that way[^65].

**7.20** Firstly, the origination of what exists, and of what does not exist, is not fitting, \
nor of what both exists and does not exist: this was already demonstrated before.

**7.21** The origination of an existent that is ceasing is not possible; \
and that existent which is not ceasing[^66], however, is not possible.

**7.22** An existent that has not endured does not endure[^67]; an existent that has endured does not endure; \
what is enduring does not endure; and what, not arisen, endures?

**7.23** The enduring of an existent that is ceasing is not possible; \
and that existent which is not ceasing, however, is not possible.

**7.24** Among all existents[^68], whose nature is always `jarāmaraṇa` (ageing and death), \
which existents endure, that are without `jarāmaraṇa`?

**7.25** The enduring of enduring by another enduring, and by that very one, is not fitting, \
just as the arising of arising is neither by itself nor by another.

**7.26** What has not ceased does not cease[^69]; what has ceased does not cease; \
likewise what is ceasing; and what, unborn, ceases?[^70]

**7.27** Firstly, the cessation of an existent that has endured is not possible; \
nor is the cessation of an existent that has not endured possible.

**7.28** For a state, being that very one, does not cease by that very state; \
nor does a state, being another, cease by another state[^71].

**7.29** Just when the arising of all `dharmas` (phenomena) is not possible, \
then, in this way[^72], the cessation of all `dharmas` is not possible.

**7.30** Firstly, the cessation of an existent that exists is not possible; \
for in oneness, an existent and a non-existent are not both possible.

**7.31** Nor is the cessation of an existent that does not exist possible, \
just as no cutting off of a second head is found.

**7.32** Cessation is not by its own self, nor is cessation by another, \
just as the arising of arising is neither by itself nor by another.

**7.33** Because arising, enduring and dissolution are not established, there is no `saṃskṛta`; \
and when the `saṃskṛta` is not established, how will the `asaṃskṛta` be established?

**7.34** Like an illusion, like a dream, like a city of gandharvas, \
so arising, so enduring, so dissolution have been declared.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**7.1** yadi saṃskṛta utpādastatra yuktā trilakṣaṇī | \
athāsaṃskṛta utpādaḥ kathaṃ saṃskṛtalakṣaṇam ||

**7.2** utpādādyāstrayo vyastā nālaṃ lakṣaṇakarmaṇi | \
saṃskṛtasya samastāḥ syurekatra kathamekadā ||

**7.3** utpādasthitibhaṅgānāmanyat saṃskṛtalakṣaṇam | \
asti cedanavasthaivaṃ nāsti cet te na saṃskṛtāḥ ||

**7.4** utpādotpāda utpādo mulotpādasya kevalam | \
utpādotpādamutpādo maulo janayate punaḥ ||

**7.5** utpādotpāda utpādo mūlotpādasya te yadi | \
maulenājanitastaṃ te sa kathaṃ janayiṣyati ||

**7.6** sa te maulena janito maulaṃ janayate yadi | \
maulaḥ sa tenājanitastamutpādayate katham ||

**7.7** ayamutpadyamānaste kāmamutpādayedimam | \
yadīmamutpādayitumajātaḥ śaknuyādayam ||

**7.8** pradīpaḥ svaparātmānau saṃprakāśayate yathā | \
utpādaḥ svaparātmānāvubhāvutpādayet tathā ||

**7.9** pradīpe nāndhakāro’sti yatra casau pratiṣṭhitaḥ | \
kiṃ prakāśayate dīpaṃ prakāśo hi tamovadhaḥ ||

**7.10** kathamutpadyamānena pradīpena tamo hatam | \
notpadyamāno hi tamaḥ pradīpaḥ prāpnute yadā ||

**7.11** aprāpyaiva pradīpena yadi vā nihataṃ tamaḥ | \
ihasthaḥ sarvalokasthaṃ sa tamo nihaniṣyati ||

**7.12** pradīpaḥ svaparātmānau saṃprakāśayate yadi | \
tamo’pi svaparātmānau chādayiṣyatyasaṃśayam ||

**7.13** anutpanno’yamutpādaḥ svātmānaṃ janayet katham | \
athotpanno janayate jāte kiṃ janyate punaḥ ||

**7.14** notpadyamānaṃ notpannaṃ nānutpannaṃ kathaṃcana | \
utpadyate tathākhyātaṃ gamyamānagatāgataiḥ ||

**7.15** utpadyamānamutpattāvidaṃ na kramate yadā | \
kathamutpadyamānaṃ tu pratītyotpattimucyate ||

**7.16** pratītya yad yad bhavati tat tacchāntaṃ svabhāvataḥ | \
tasmādutpadyamānaṃ ca śāntamutpattireva ca ||

**7.17** yadi kaścidanutpanno bhāvaḥ saṃvidyate kvacit | \
utpadyeta sa kiṃ tasmin bhāva utpadyate’sati ||

**7.18** utpadyamānamutpādo yadi cotpādayatyayam | \
utpādayet tamutpādamutpādaḥ katamaḥ punaḥ ||

**7.19** anya utpādayatyenaṃ yadyutpādo’navasthitiḥ | \
athānutpāda utpannaḥ sarvamutpadyatāṃ tathā ||

**7.20** sataśca tāvadutpattirasataśca na yujyate | \
na sataścāsataśceti pūrvamevopapāditam ||

**7.21** nirudhyamānasyotpattirna bhāvasyopapadyate | \
yaścānirudhyamānastu sa bhāvo nopapadyate ||

**7.22** nāsthitastiṣṭhate bhāvaḥ sthito bhāvo na tiṣṭhati | \
na tiṣṭhate tiṣṭhamānaḥ ko’nutpannaśca tiṣṭhati ||

**7.23** sthitirnirudhyamānasya na bhāvasyopapadyate | \
yaścānirudhyamānastu sa bhāvo nopapadyate ||

**7.24** jarāmaraṇadharmeṣu sarvabhāveṣu sarvadā | \
tiṣṭhanti katame bhāvā ye jarāmaraṇaṃ vinā ||

**7.25** sthityānyayā sthiteḥ sthānaṃ tayaiva ca na yujyate | \
utpādasya yathotpādo nātmanā na parātmanā ||

**7.26** nirudhyate nānirūddhaṃ na nirūddhaṃ nirudhyate | \
tathā nirudhyamānaṃ ca kimajātaṃ nirudhyate ||

**7.27** sthitasya tāvad bhāvasya nirodho nopapadyate | \
nāsthitasyāpi bhāvasya nirodha upapadyate ||

**7.28** tayaivāvasthayāvasthā na hi saiva nirudhyate | \
ananyāvasthayāvasthā na cānyaiva nirudhyate ||

**7.29** yadaiva sarvadharmāṇāmutpādo nopapadyate | \
tadaivaṃ sarvadharmāṇāṃ nirodho nopapadyate ||

**7.30** sataśca tāvad bhāvasya nirodho nopapadyate | \
ekatve na hi bhāvaśca nābhāvaścopapadyate ||

**7.31** asato’pi na bhāvasya nirodha upapadyate | \
na dvitīyasya śirasaśchedanaṃ vidyate yathā ||

**7.32** na svātmanā nirodho’sti nirodho na parātmanā | \
utpādasya yathotpādo nātmanā na parātmanā ||

**7.33** utpādasthitibhaṅgānāmasiddhernāsti saṃskṛtam | \
saṃskṛtasyāprasiddhau ca kathaṃ setsyatyasaṃskṛtam ||

**7.34** yathā māyā yathā svapno gandharvanagaraṃ yathā | \
tathotpādastathā sthānaṃ tathā bhaṅga udāhṛtam ||

7.1 yadi saṃskṛta utpādastatra yuktā trilakṣaṇī athāsaṃskṛta utpādaḥ kathaṃ saṃskṛtalakṣaṇam  
⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(saṃskṛta) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) ⏏️(tatra) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(yukta) 🚺⨀①🆎(tri+lakṣaṇa) ⏏️(atha) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+saṃskṛta) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①🆎(saṃskṛta+lakṣaṇa)  
if | constructed | arising | for it | is fitting | the triad of characteristics | but if | unconstructed | arising | how | a characteristic of the constructed  
If arising is `saṃskṛta` (constructed), the triad of characteristics is fitting for it;  
but if arising is `asaṃskṛta` (unconstructed), how is it a characteristic of the `saṃskṛta`?

7.2 utpādādyāstrayo vyastā nālaṃ lakṣaṇakarmaṇi saṃskṛtasya samastāḥ syurekatra kathamekadā  
🚹⨂①🆎(utpāda+ādya) 🚹⨂①(tri) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(vyasta) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(alam) 🚻⨀⑦🆎(lakṣaṇa+karman) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(saṃskṛta) 🚹⨂①(samasta) 🟢⨂🤟⏯(asti) ⏏️(ekatra) ⏏️(katham) ⏏️(ekadā)  
arising and the rest | the three | separately | not | adequate | for the work of a characteristic | of the constructed | combined | could they be | in one place | how | at one time  
The three, arising and the rest, separately are not adequate for the work of a characteristic  
of the `saṃskṛta`; combined, how could they be in one place at one time?

7.3 utpādasthitibhaṅgānāmanyat saṃskṛtalakṣaṇam asti cedanavasthaivaṃ nāsti cet te na saṃskṛtāḥ  
🚹⨂⑥🆎(utpāda+sthiti+bhaṅga) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(anya) 🚻⨀①🆎(saṃskṛta+lakṣaṇa) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(cet) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+avasthā) ⏏️(evam) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(cet) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(saṃskṛta)  
of arising, enduring and dissolution | another | characteristic of the constructed | there is | if | infinite regress | thus | there is not | if | they | not | constructed  
Of arising, enduring and dissolution, another characteristic of the `saṃskṛta`:  
if there is one, thus there is infinite regress; if there is not, they are not `saṃskṛta`.

7.4 utpādotpāda utpādo mulotpādasya kevalam utpādotpādamutpādo maulo janayate punaḥ  
🚹⨀①🆎(utpāda+utpāda) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(mūla+utpāda) ⏏️(kevalam) 🚹⨀②🆎(utpāda+utpāda) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) 🚹⨀①(maula) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) ⏏️(punar)  
the arising of arising | the arising | of the root arising | alone | the arising of arising | the arising | the root one | generates | in turn  
The arising of arising is the arising of the root arising alone;  
the root arising, in turn, generates the arising of arising.

7.5 utpādotpāda utpādo mūlotpādasya te yadi maulenājanitastaṃ te sa kathaṃ janayiṣyati  
🚹⨀①🆎(utpāda+utpāda) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(mūla+utpāda) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀③(maula) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+janita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(tad) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(katham) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(janayati)  
the arising of arising | the arising | of the root arising | for you | if | by the root one | not generated | that | for you | it | how | will generate  
If, for you, the arising of arising is the arising of the root arising,  
how, for you, will it, not generated by the root one, generate that?

7.6 sa te maulena janito maulaṃ janayate yadi maulaḥ sa tenājanitastamutpādayate katham  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) 🚹⨀③(maula) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(janita) 🚹⨀②(maula) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀①(maula) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+janita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(tad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(utpādayati) ⏏️(katham)  
it | for you | by the root one | generated | the root one | generates | if | that root one | that | by it | not generated | it | does … produce | how  
If, for you, it, generated by the root one, generates the root one,  
how does that root one, not generated by it, produce it?

7.7 ayamutpadyamānaste kāmamutpādayedimam yadīmamutpādayitumajātaḥ śaknuyādayam  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(idam) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) ⏏️(kāmam) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(utpādayati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(idam) ⏏️(yadi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(idam) 🔽(tum)(utpādayati) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+jāta) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(śaknoti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(idam)  
this one | while arising | for you | may well | produce | that one | if | that one | to produce | unborn | could | this one  
This one, while arising, may well produce that one, for you,  
if this one, unborn, could produce that one.

7.8 pradīpaḥ svaparātmānau saṃprakāśayate yathā utpādaḥ svaparātmānāvubhāvutpādayet tathā  
🚹⨀①(pradīpa) 🚹⨁②🆎(sva+para+ātman) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(saṃprakāśayati) ⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) 🚹⨁②🆎(sva+para+ātman) *️⃣🤟🚹⨁②(ubha) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(utpādayati) ⏏️(tathā)  
a lamp | itself and another | illuminates | just as | arising | itself and another | both | would produce | so  
Just as a lamp illuminates itself and another,  
so arising would produce both itself and another.

7.9 pradīpe nāndhakāro’sti yatra casau pratiṣṭhitaḥ kiṃ prakāśayate dīpaṃ prakāśo hi tamovadhaḥ  
🚹⨀⑦(pradīpa) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(andha+kāra) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(yatra) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(adas) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(pratiṣṭhita) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(kim) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prakāśayati) 🚹⨀②(dīpa) 🚹⨀①(prakāśa) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀①🆎(tamas+vadha)  
in a lamp | not | darkness | there is | where | and | it | stands | what | does … illuminate | the lamp | illumination | for | the destruction of darkness  
There is no darkness in a lamp, nor where it stands;  
what does the lamp illuminate? For illumination is the destruction of darkness.

7.10 kathamutpadyamānena pradīpena tamo hatam notpadyamāno hi tamaḥ pradīpaḥ prāpnute yadā  
⏏️(katham) 🚹⨀③🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) 🚹⨀③(pradīpa) 🚻⨀①(tamas) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(hata) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) ⏏️(hi) 🚻⨀②(tamas) 🚹⨀①(pradīpa) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prāpnoti) ⏏️(yadā)  
how | that is arising | by the lamp | darkness | is destroyed | not | that is arising | indeed | darkness | the lamp | reaches | when  
How is darkness destroyed by the lamp that is arising,  
when indeed the lamp that is arising does not reach darkness?

7.11 aprāpyaiva pradīpena yadi vā nihataṃ tamaḥ ihasthaḥ sarvalokasthaṃ sa tamo nihaniṣyati  
🔽(ya)🆎(na+prāpnoti) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀③(pradīpa) ⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(vā) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(nihata) 🚻⨀①(tamas) 🚹⨀①🆎(iha+stha) 🚻⨀②🆎(sarva+loka+stha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀②(tamas) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(nihanti)  
without reaching | at all | by the lamp | if | or | is destroyed | darkness | standing here | standing in the whole world | it | the darkness | will destroy  
Or if darkness is destroyed by the lamp without reaching it at all,  
it, standing here, will destroy the darkness standing in the whole world.

7.12 pradīpaḥ svaparātmānau saṃprakāśayate yadi tamo’pi svaparātmānau chādayiṣyatyasaṃśayam  
🚹⨀①(pradīpa) 🚹⨁②🆎(sva+para+ātman) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(saṃprakāśayati) ⏏️(yadi) 🚻⨀①(tamas) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨁②🆎(sva+para+ātman) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(chādayati) ⏏️(asaṃśayam)  
a lamp | itself and another | illuminates | if | darkness | too | itself and another | will conceal | without doubt  
If a lamp illuminates itself and another,  
darkness too will conceal itself and another, without doubt.

7.13 anutpanno’yamutpādaḥ svātmānaṃ janayet katham athotpanno janayate jāte kiṃ janyate punaḥ  
🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(idam) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) 🚹⨀②🆎(sva+ātman) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(janayati) ⏏️(katham) ⏏️(atha) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(utpanna) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(jāta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janyate) ⏏️(punar)  
not arisen | this | arising | its own self | could … generate | how | but | arisen | it generates | when it is born | what | is generated | again  
How could this arising, not arisen, generate its own self?  
But if, arisen, it generates: when it is born, what is generated again?

7.14 notpadyamānaṃ notpannaṃ nānutpannaṃ kathaṃcana utpadyate tathākhyātaṃ gamyamānagatāgataiḥ  
⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(utpanna) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) ⏏️(kathaṃcana) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(utpadyate) ⏏️(tathā) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(ākhyāta) 🚻⨂③🆎(gamyamāna+gata+na+gata)  
neither | what is arising | nor | what has arisen | nor | what has not arisen | in any way | arises | so | it was explained | by the being gone, the gone and the not gone  
Neither what is arising, nor what has arisen, nor what has not arisen, in any way  
arises; so it was explained by the being gone, the gone and the not gone.

7.15 utpadyamānamutpattāvidaṃ na kramate yadā kathamutpadyamānaṃ tu pratītyotpattimucyate  
🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) 🚺⨀⑦(utpatti) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(krāmati) ⏏️(yadā) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) ⏏️(tu) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) 🚺⨀②(utpatti) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(ucyate)  
which is arising | to origination | this | not | proceeds | when | how | arising | then | in dependence on | origination | is it said  
When this, which is arising, does not proceed to origination,  
how then is it said to be arising in dependence on origination?

7.16 pratītya yad yad bhavati tat tacchāntaṃ svabhāvataḥ tasmādutpadyamānaṃ ca śāntamutpattireva ca  
🔽(ya)(pratyeti) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(śānta) ⏏️(svabhāvatas) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(śānta) 🚺⨀①(utpatti) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(ca)  
in dependence | whatever | whatever | comes to be | that | that | is at peace | in respect of intrinsic nature | therefore | what is arising | and | at peace | origination | itself | and  
Whatever comes to be in dependence, that is at peace in respect of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature);  
therefore both what is arising and origination itself are at peace.

7.17 yadi kaścidanutpanno bhāvaḥ saṃvidyate kvacit utpadyeta sa kiṃ tasmin bhāva utpadyate’sati  
⏏️(yadi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(saṃvidyate) ⏏️(kvacit) 🔵⨀🤟⏯(utpadyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(tad) 🚹⨀⑦(bhāva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(utpadyate) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)🆎(na+sat)  
if | some | that has not arisen | existent | is found | somewhere | it might arise | it | what | when that | existent | arises | does not exist  
If some existent that has not arisen is found somewhere,  
it might arise; when that existent does not exist, what arises?

7.18 utpadyamānamutpādo yadi cotpādayatyayam utpādayet tamutpādamutpādaḥ katamaḥ punaḥ  
🚹⨀②🔽(māna)(utpadyamāna) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) ⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(utpādayati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(idam) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(utpādayati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(tad) 🚹⨀②(utpāda) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(katama) ⏏️(punar)  
what is arising | arising | if | and | produces | this | would produce | that | arising | arising | which | again  
And if this arising produces what is arising,  
which arising, again, would produce that arising?

7.19 anya utpādayatyenaṃ yadyutpādo’navasthitiḥ athānutpāda utpannaḥ sarvamutpadyatāṃ tathā  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(utpādayati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(ena) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+avasthiti) ⏏️(atha) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+utpāda) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(utpanna) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(sarva) 🔵⨀🤟⏹(utpadyate) ⏏️(tathā)  
another | produces | this one | if | arising | infinite regress | but | without an arising | it has arisen | everything | let … arise | in that way  
If another arising produces this one, there is infinite regress;  
but if it has arisen without an arising, let everything arise in that way.

7.20 sataśca tāvadutpattirasataśca na yujyate na sataścāsataśceti pūrvamevopapāditam  
🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)(sat) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(tāvat) 🚺⨀①(utpatti) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)(sat) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(pūrvam) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(upapādita)  
of what exists | and | firstly | the origination | of what does not exist | and | not | is fitting | nor | of what exists | and | of what does not exist | and | this | before | already | was demonstrated  
Firstly, the origination of what exists, and of what does not exist, is not fitting,  
nor of what both exists and does not exist: this was already demonstrated before.

7.21 nirudhyamānasyotpattirna bhāvasyopapadyate yaścānirudhyamānastu sa bhāvo nopapadyate  
🚹⨀⑥🔽(māna)(nirudhyamāna) 🚺⨀①(utpatti) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)🆎(na+nirudhyamāna) ⏏️(tu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
that is ceasing | the origination | not | of an existent | is possible | which | and | is not ceasing | however | that | existent | not | is possible  
The origination of an existent that is ceasing is not possible;  
and that existent which is not ceasing, however, is not possible.

7.22 nāsthitastiṣṭhate bhāvaḥ sthito bhāvo na tiṣṭhati na tiṣṭhate tiṣṭhamānaḥ ko’nutpannaśca tiṣṭhati  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+sthita) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(sthita) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(tiṣṭhamāna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kim) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati)  
not | that has not endured | endures | an existent | that has endured | an existent | not | endures | not | endures | what is enduring | what | not arisen | and | endures  
An existent that has not endured does not endure; an existent that has endured does not endure;  
what is enduring does not endure; and what, not arisen, endures?

7.23 sthitirnirudhyamānasya na bhāvasyopapadyate yaścānirudhyamānastu sa bhāvo nopapadyate  
🚺⨀①(sthiti) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(māna)(nirudhyamāna) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)🆎(na+nirudhyamāna) ⏏️(tu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
the enduring | that is ceasing | not | of an existent | is possible | which | and | is not ceasing | however | that | existent | not | is possible  
The enduring of an existent that is ceasing is not possible;  
and that existent which is not ceasing, however, is not possible.

7.24 jarāmaraṇadharmeṣu sarvabhāveṣu sarvadā tiṣṭhanti katame bhāvā ye jarāmaraṇaṃ vinā  
🚹⨂⑦🆎(jarā+maraṇa+dharma) 🚹⨂⑦🆎(sarva+bhāva) ⏏️(sarvadā) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(katama) 🚹⨂①(bhāva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) 🚻⨀②🆎(jarā+maraṇa) ⏏️(vinā)  
whose nature is ageing and death | among all existents | always | endure | which | existents | that | ageing and death | without  
Among all existents, whose nature is always `jarāmaraṇa` (ageing and death),  
which existents endure, that are without `jarāmaraṇa`?

7.25 sthityānyayā sthiteḥ sthānaṃ tayaiva ca na yujyate utpādasya yathotpādo nātmanā na parātmanā  
🚺⨀③(sthiti) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀③(anya) 🚺⨀⑥(sthiti) 🚻⨀①(sthāna) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀③(tad) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) 🚹⨀⑥(utpāda) ⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③(ātman) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🆎(para+ātman)  
by enduring | by another | of enduring | the enduring | by that | very | and | not | is fitting | of arising | just as | arising | not | by itself | by another | by another  
The enduring of enduring by another enduring, and by that very one, is not fitting,  
just as the arising of arising is neither by itself nor by another.

7.26 nirudhyate nānirūddhaṃ na nirūddhaṃ nirudhyate tathā nirudhyamānaṃ ca kimajātaṃ nirudhyate  
🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+niruddha) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(niruddha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate) ⏏️(tathā) 🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(nirudhyamāna) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+jāta) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate)  
ceases | not | what has not ceased | not | what has ceased | ceases | likewise | what is ceasing | and | what | unborn | ceases  
What has not ceased does not cease; what has ceased does not cease;  
likewise what is ceasing; and what, unborn, ceases?

7.27 sthitasya tāvad bhāvasya nirodho nopapadyate nāsthitasyāpi bhāvasya nirodha upapadyate  
🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(sthita) ⏏️(tāvat) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)🆎(na+sthita) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
of what has endured | firstly | of an existent | cessation | not | is possible | not | of what has not endured | even | of an existent | cessation | is possible  
Firstly, the cessation of an existent that has endured is not possible;  
nor is the cessation of an existent that has not endured possible.

7.28 tayaivāvasthayāvasthā na hi saiva nirudhyate ananyāvasthayāvasthā na cānyaiva nirudhyate  
*️⃣🤟🚺⨀③(tad) ⏏️(eva) 🚺⨀③(avasthā) 🚺⨀①(avasthā) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(tad) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀③(anya) 🚺⨀③(avasthā) 🚺⨀①(avasthā) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(anya) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate)  
by that | very | state | a state | not | for | that | very | ceases | by another | state | a state | not | and | another | indeed | ceases  
For a state, being that very one, does not cease by that very state;  
nor does a state, being another, cease by another state.

7.29 yadaiva sarvadharmāṇāmutpādo nopapadyate tadaivaṃ sarvadharmāṇāṃ nirodho nopapadyate  
⏏️(yadā) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(sarva+dharma) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) ⏏️(tadā) ⏏️(evam) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(sarva+dharma) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
when | just | of all dharmas | arising | not | is possible | then | thus | of all dharmas | cessation | not | is possible  
Just when the arising of all `dharmas` (phenomena) is not possible,  
then, in this way, the cessation of all `dharmas` is not possible.

7.30 sataśca tāvad bhāvasya nirodho nopapadyate ekatve na hi bhāvaśca nābhāvaścopapadyate  
🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)(sat) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(tāvat) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) 🚻⨀⑦(ekatva) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(abhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
of what exists | and | firstly | of an existent | cessation | not | is possible | in oneness | not | for | an existent | and | not | a non-existent | and | is possible  
Firstly, the cessation of an existent that exists is not possible;  
for in oneness, an existent and a non-existent are not both possible.

7.31 asato’pi na bhāvasya nirodha upapadyate na dvitīyasya śirasaśchedanaṃ vidyate yathā  
🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥(dvitīya) 🚻⨀⑥(śiras) 🚻⨀①(chedana) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) ⏏️(yathā)  
of what does not exist | even | not | of an existent | cessation | is possible | not | of a second | head | cutting off | is found | just as  
Nor is the cessation of an existent that does not exist possible,  
just as no cutting off of a second head is found.

7.32 na svātmanā nirodho’sti nirodho na parātmanā utpādasya yathotpādo nātmanā na parātmanā  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🆎(sva+ātman) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🆎(para+ātman) 🚹⨀⑥(utpāda) ⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③(ātman) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🆎(para+ātman)  
not | by its own self | cessation | is | cessation | not | by another | of arising | just as | arising | not | by itself | not | by another  
Cessation is not by its own self, nor is cessation by another,  
just as the arising of arising is neither by itself nor by another.

7.33 utpādasthitibhaṅgānāmasiddhernāsti saṃskṛtam saṃskṛtasyāprasiddhau ca kathaṃ setsyatyasaṃskṛtam  
🚹⨂⑥🆎(utpāda+sthiti+bhaṅga) 🚺⨀⑤🆎(na+siddhi) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(saṃskṛta) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(saṃskṛta) 🚺⨀⑦🆎(na+prasiddhi) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(katham) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(sidhyati) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+saṃskṛta)  
of arising, enduring and dissolution | because of the non-establishment | there is not | the constructed | of the constructed | in the non-establishment | and | how | will be established | the unconstructed  
Because arising, enduring and dissolution are not established, there is no `saṃskṛta`;  
and when the `saṃskṛta` is not established, how will the `asaṃskṛta` be established?

7.34 yathā māyā yathā svapno gandharvanagaraṃ yathā tathotpādastathā sthānaṃ tathā bhaṅga udāhṛtam  
⏏️(yathā) 🚺⨀①(māyā) ⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀①(svapna) 🚻⨀①🆎(gandharva+nagara) ⏏️(yathā) ⏏️(tathā) 🚹⨀①(utpāda) ⏏️(tathā) 🚻⨀①(sthāna) ⏏️(tathā) 🚹⨀①(bhaṅga) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(udāhṛta)  
as | an illusion | as | a dream | a city of gandharvas | as | so | arising | so | enduring | so | dissolution | have been declared  
Like an illusion, like a dream, like a city of gandharvas,  
so arising, so enduring, so dissolution have been declared.

</TabItem>
</Tabs>

### §8 Karmakārakaparīkṣā (Examination of action and agent)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**8.1** This real agent does not perform a real `karman` (action); \
nor does an unreal agent attempt an unreal `karman`[^73].

**8.2** There is no activity of the real, and the `karman` would be without an agent; \
there is no activity of the real, and the agent would be without a `karman`.

**8.3** If an unreal agent performs an unreal `karman`, \
the `karman` would be without a primary cause, and the agent would be without a primary cause.

**8.4** When there is no primary cause, neither effect nor cause is found; \
in the non-existence of that, activity, agent and instrument[^74] are not found.

**8.5** `dharma` (merit)[^75] and `adharma` (demerit)[^76] are not found when activity and the rest do not come to be; \
and when there is no `dharma` and no `adharma`, the fruit born of them is not found.

**8.6** When there is no fruit, neither for release nor for heaven is there possible \
a path; and the uselessness of all activities follows.

**8.7** An agent both real and unreal does not perform that which is both real and unreal; \
for how could the real and the unreal, mutually contradictory, be together?

**8.8** And the unreal is not performed by a real, nor is the real performed by an unreal, \
agent; for all those very faults follow there.

**8.9** Neither an unreal one nor one that is both real and unreal does the real \
agent perform as `karman`, for the very reasons stated before.

**8.10** Nor a real one, nor one that is both real and unreal, does the unreal \
agent perform as `karman`, for the very reasons stated before.

**8.11** An agent both real and unreal does not perform a real, nor an unreal[^77], \
`karman`; but that one should understand from the very reasons stated before.

**8.12** The agent proceeds in dependence on the `karman`, and in dependence on that agent \
the `karman` proceeds; we see no other cause of establishment.

**8.13** Thus one should understand `upādāna` (fuel)[^78], by the dismissal of `karman` \
and of agent; and by `karman` and agent one should discern the remaining existents.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**8.1** sadbhūtaḥ kārakaḥ karma sadbhūtaṃ na karotyayam | \
kārako nāpyasadbhūtaḥ karmāsadbhūtamīhate ||

**8.2** sadbhūtasya kriyā nāsti karma ca syādakartṛkam | \
sadbhūtasya kriyā nāsti kartā ca syādakarmakaḥ ||

**8.3** karoti yadyasadbhūto’sadbhūtaṃ karma kārakaḥ | \
ahetukaṃ bhavet karma kartā cāhetuko bhavet ||

**8.4** hetāvasati kāryaṃ ca kāraṇaṃ ca na vidyate | \
tadabhāve kriyā kartā kāraṇaṃ ca na vidyate ||

**8.5** dharmādharmo na vidyete kriyādīnāmasaṃbhave | \
dharme cāsatyadharme ca phalaṃ tajjaṃ na vidyate ||

**8.6** phale’sati na mokṣāya na svargāyopapadyate | \
mārgaḥ sarvakriyāṇāṃ ca nairarthakyaṃ prasajyate ||

**8.7** kārakaḥ sadasadbhūtaḥ sadasat kurute na tat | \
parasparaviruddhaṃ hi saccāsaccaikataḥ kutaḥ ||

**8.8** satā ca kriyate nāsan nāsatā kriyate ca sat | \
kartrā sarve prasajyante doṣāstatra ta eva hi ||

**8.9** nāsadbhūtaṃ na sadbhūtaḥ sadasadbhūtameva vā | \
karoti kārakaḥ karma pūrvoktaireva hetubhiḥ ||

**8.10** nāsadbhūto’pi sadbhūtaṃ sadasadbhūtameva vā | \
karoti kārakaḥ karma pūrvoktaireva hetubhiḥ ||

**8.11** karoti sadasadbhūto na sannāsacca kārakaḥ | \
karma tat tu vijānīyāt pūrvoktaireva hetubhiḥ ||

**8.12** pratītya kārakaḥ karma taṃ pratītya ca kārakam | \
karma pravartate nānyat paśyāmaḥ siddhikāraṇam ||

**8.13** evaṃ vidyādupādānaṃ vyutsargāditi karmaṇaḥ | \
kartuśca karmakartṛbhyāṃ śeṣān bhāvān vibhāvayet ||

</TabItem>

`karman`; but that one should understand from the very reasons stated before.

8.12 pratītya kārakaḥ karma taṃ pratītya ca kārakam karma pravartate nānyat paśyāmaḥ siddhikāraṇam  
🔽(ya)(pratyeti) 🚹⨀①(kāraka) 🚻⨀②(karman) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(tad) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀②(kāraka) 🚻⨀①(karman) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(pravartate) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(anya) 🟢⨂👆▶️(paśyati) 🚻⨀②🆎(siddhi+kāraṇa)  
in dependence on | the agent | the action | that | in dependence on | and | the agent | the action | proceeds | not | other | we see | cause of establishment  
The agent proceeds in dependence on the `karman`, and in dependence on that agent  
the `karman` proceeds; we see no other cause of establishment.

8.13 evaṃ vidyādupādānaṃ vyutsargāditi karmaṇaḥ kartuśca karmakartṛbhyāṃ śeṣān bhāvān vibhāvayet  
⏏️(evam) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(vetti) 🚻⨀②(upādāna) 🚹⨀⑤(vyutsarga) ⏏️(iti) 🚻⨀⑥(karman) 🚹⨀⑥(kartṛ) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨁③🆎(karman+kartṛ) 🚹⨂②(śeṣa) 🚹⨂②(bhāva) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(vibhāvayati)  
thus | one should understand | fuel | by the dismissal | thus | of action | of the agent | and | by action and agent | the remaining | existents | one should discern  
Thus one should understand `upādāna` (fuel), by the dismissal of `karman`  
and of agent; and by `karman` and agent one should discern the remaining existents.

</TabItem>
</Tabs>

### §9 Upadātrupādānaparīkṣā (Examination of the taker of fuel and the fuel)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**9.1** Now seeing, hearing and the rest, and feeling and the rest too: \
he whose they are[^79] exists prior to these — so indeed some say.[^80]

**9.2** For how will seeing and the rest come to be of an existent that is not found? \
Therefore that existent exists, established prior to these.

**9.3** Seeing, hearing and the rest, and feeling and the rest as well: \
the existent that is established prior to them — by what, then, is it designated?

**9.4** And if that one is established even without seeing and the rest, \
those too will exist without that one; there is no doubt.

**9.5** Someone is made manifest by something; something is made manifest by someone: \
whence someone without something? Whence something without someone?[^81]

**9.6** Someone prior to all of seeing and the rest is not found; \
rather, he is made manifest by another of seeing and the rest at another time[^82].

**9.7** If one prior to all of seeing and the rest is not found, \
how is he found prior to each one of seeing and the rest?

**9.8** If that very one is the seer, that one the hearer, that very one the feeler, \
he would be prior to each one; and so this is not fitting.

**9.9** If, again, the seer is quite different, the hearer different, the feeler different, \
there would be a hearer when there is a seer, and there would be a multiplicity of selves.

**9.10** Now seeing, hearing and the rest, and feeling and the rest too: \
in those elements from which they come to be, this one is not found either[^83].

**9.11** Now seeing, hearing and the rest, and feeling and the rest too: \
if he whose they are is not found, these are not found either.

**9.12** And he who, before seeing and the rest, and at present, and after as well, \
is not found — with regard to him[^84] the conceptions "he exists", "he does not exist" have ceased.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**9.1** darśanaśravaṇādīni vedanādīni cāpyatha | \
bhavanti yasya prāgebhyaḥ so’stītyeke vadantyuta ||

**9.2** kathaṃ hyavidyamānasya darśanādi bhaviṣyati | \
bhāvasya tasmāt prāgebhyaḥ so’sti bhāvo vyavasthitaḥ ||

**9.3** darśanaśravaṇādibhyo vedanādibhya eva ca | \
yaḥ prāg vyavasthito bhāvaḥ kena prajñapyate’tha saḥ ||

**9.4** vināpi darśanādīni yadi cāsau vyavasthitaḥ | \
amūnyapi bhaviṣyanti vinā tena na saṃśayaḥ ||

**9.5** ajyate kenacit kaścit kiṃcit kenacidajyate | \
kutaḥ kiṃcidvinā kaścit kiṃcit kiṃcidvinā kutaḥ ||

**9.6** sarvebhyo darśanādibhyaḥ kaścit pūrvo na vidyate | \
ajyate darśanādīnāmanyena punaranyadā ||

**9.7** sarvebhyo darśanādibhyo yadi pūrvo na vidyate | \
ekaikasmāt kathaṃ pūrvo darśanādeḥ sa vidyate ||

**9.8** draṣṭā sa eva sa śrotā sa eva yadi vedakaḥ | \
ekaikasmād bhavet pūrvamevaṃ caitanna yujyate ||

**9.9** draṣṭānya eva śrotānyo vedako’nyaḥ punaryadi | \
sati syād draṣṭari śrotā bahutvaṃ cātmanāṃ bhavet ||

**9.10** darśanaśravaṇādīni vedanādīni cāpyatha | \
bhavanti yebhyasteṣveṣa bhūteṣvapi na vidyate ||

**9.11** darśanaśravaṇādīni vedanādīni cāpyatha | \
na vidyate ced yasya sa na vidyanta imānyapi ||

**9.12** prāk ca yo darśanādibhyaḥ sāṃprataṃ cordhvameva ca | \
na vidyate’stināstīti nivṛttāstatra kalpanāḥ ||

### §10 Agnīndhanaparīkṣā (Examination of fire and firewood)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**10.1** If the fire is that which is the firewood, there is oneness of agent and `karman` (object)[^85]; \
if the fire is other than the firewood, it would exist even without the firewood.

**10.2** It would simply be permanently ablaze, without a primary cause of kindling; \
beginning again is pointless[^86]; and, this being so, it is without `karman`.

**10.3** Because of not depending on another, it is without a primary cause of kindling; \
it follows that it is permanently ablaze[^87] and that beginning again is pointless.

**10.4** If here this should be so[^88]: "the firewood is that which is being kindled", \
by what is that firewood to be kindled, when this is only that much?

**10.5** Being other, it will not reach[^89]; not having reached, it will not burn; not burning, moreover, \
it will not be extinguished; or, not extinguished, it will remain, bearing its own mark.

**10.6** If the fire were wholly other than the firewood, it could reach the firewood, \
just as a woman reaches a man, and a man a woman.

**10.7** The fire, wholly other than the firewood, may well reach the firewood, \
if the fire and the firewood were set apart from one another.

**10.8** If the fire depends on the firewood, and if the firewood depends on the fire, \
which of the two is accomplished first, that depending on which there is fire or firewood[^90]?

**10.9** If the fire depends on the firewood, there is the establishing of a fire that is established; \
this being so, the firewood too will be without fire.

**10.10** The existent that is established depending on another — depending on that very existent the other is established: \
if that which is to be depended on is to be established, which, depending on which?

**10.11** The existent that is established depending — how does it, not established, depend? \
Or if it depends once established, then its dependence is not fitting.

**10.12** There is no fire depending on the firewood[^91], nor fire not depending on the firewood; \
there is no firewood depending on the fire, nor firewood not depending on the fire.

**10.13** The fire does not come from elsewhere; the fire is not found in the firewood; \
here, as to the firewood, the rest has been stated by means of the being gone, the gone and the not gone[^92].

**10.14** Again, the fire is not the firewood; nor is the fire elsewhere than the firewood[^93]; \
the fire does not possess firewood; the firewood is not in the fire, nor is the fire in it.

**10.15** By fire and firewood the sequence of the self and the `upādāna` (fuel)[^94] is explained, \
all of it without remainder, together with pot, cloth and the rest[^95].

**10.16** And those who declare the sameness of the self and of existents, and their separateness[^96], \
I do not think them skilled in the meaning of the teaching.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**10.1** yadindhanaṃ sa cedagnirekatvaṃ kartṛkarmaṇoḥ | \
anyaścedindhanādagnirindhanādapyṛte bhavet ||

**10.2** nityapradīpta eva syādapradīpanahetukaḥ | \
punarārambhavaiyarthyamevaṃ cākarmakaḥ sati ||

**10.3** paratra nirapekṣatvādapradīpanahetukaḥ | \
punarārambhavaiyarthyaṃ nityadīptaḥ prasajyate ||

**10.4** tatraitat syādidhyamānamindhanaṃ bhavatīti cet | \
kenedhyatāmindhanaṃ tat tāvanmātramidaṃ yadā ||

**10.5** anyo na prāpsyate’prāpto na dhakṣyatyadahan punaḥ | \
na nirvāsyatyanirvāṇaḥ sthāsyate vā svaliṅgavān ||

**10.6** anya evendhanādagnirindhanaṃ prāpnuyād yadi | \
strī saṃprāpnoti puruṣaṃ puruṣaśca striyaṃ yathā ||

**10.7** anya evendhanādagnirindhanaṃ kāmamāpnuyāt | \
agnīndhane yadi syātāmanyonyena tiraskṛte ||

**10.8** yadīndhanamapekṣyāgnirapekṣyāgniṃ yadīndhanam | \
katarat pūrvaniṣpannaṃ yadapekṣyāgnirindhanam ||

**10.9** yadīndhanamapekṣyāgniragneḥ siddhasya sādhanam | \
evaṃ satīndhanaṃ cāpi bhaviṣyati niragnikam ||

**10.10** yo’pekṣya sidhyate bhāvastamevāpekṣya sidhyati | \
yadi yo’pekṣitavyaḥ sa sidhyatāṃ kamapekṣya kaḥ ||

**10.11** yo’pekṣya sidhyate bhāvaḥ so’siddho’pekṣate katham | \
athāpyapekṣate siddhastvapekṣāsya na yujyate ||

**10.12** apekṣyendhanamagnirna nānapekṣyāgnirindhanam | \
apekṣyendhanamagniṃ na nānapekṣyāgnimindhanam ||

**10.13** āgacchatyanyato nāgnirindhane’gnirna vidyate | \
atrendhane śeṣamuktaṃ gamyamānagatāgataiḥ ||

**10.14** indhanaṃ punaragnirna nāgniranyatra cendhanāt | \
nāgnirindhanavān nāgnāvindhanāni na teṣu saḥ ||

**10.15** agnīndhanābhyāṃ vyākhyāta ātmopādānayoḥ kramaḥ | \
sarvo niravaśeṣeṇa sārdhaṃ ghaṭapaṭādibhiḥ ||

**10.16** ātmanaśca satattvaṃ ye bhāvānāṃ ca pṛthak pṛthak | \
nirdiśanti na tān manye śāsanasyārthakovidān ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">
10.1 yadindhanaṃ sa cedagnirekatvaṃ kartṛkarmaṇoḥ anyaścedindhanādagnirindhanādapyṛte bhavet  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) 🚻⨀①(indhana) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(cet) 🚹⨀①(agni) 🚻⨀①(ekatva) 🚻⨁⑥🆎(kartṛ+karman) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) ⏏️(cet) 🚻⨀⑤(indhana) 🚹⨀①(agni) 🚻⨀⑤(indhana) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(ṛte) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati)  
which | the firewood | that | if | the fire | oneness | of agent and object | other | if | than the firewood | the fire | the firewood | even | without | it would exist  
If the fire is that which is the firewood, there is oneness of agent and `karman` (object);  
if the fire is other than the firewood, it would exist even without the firewood.

10.2 nityapradīpta eva syādapradīpanahetukaḥ punarārambhavaiyarthyamevaṃ cākarmakaḥ sati  
🚹⨀①🆎(nitya+pradīpta) ⏏️(eva) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+pradīpana+hetuka) 🚻⨀①🆎(punar+ārambha+vaiyarthya) ⏏️(evam) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+karmaka) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat)  
permanently ablaze | simply | it would be | without a primary cause of kindling | the pointlessness of beginning again | thus | and | without an object | being so  
It would simply be permanently ablaze, without a primary cause of kindling;  
beginning again is pointless; and, this being so, it is without `karman`.

10.3 paratra nirapekṣatvādapradīpanahetukaḥ punarārambhavaiyarthyaṃ nityadīptaḥ prasajyate  
⏏️(paratra) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(tva)(nirapekṣatva) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+pradīpana+hetuka) 🚻⨀①🆎(punar+ārambha+vaiyarthya) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(nitya+dīpta) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate)  
on another | because of not depending | without a primary cause of kindling | the pointlessness of beginning again | permanently ablaze | it follows  
Because of not depending on another, it is without a primary cause of kindling;  
it follows that it is permanently ablaze and that beginning again is pointless.

10.4 tatraitat syādidhyamānamindhanaṃ bhavatīti cet kenedhyatāmindhanaṃ tat tāvanmātramidaṃ yadā  
⏏️(tatra) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(etad) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(idhyamāna) 🚻⨀①(indhana) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(cet) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(kim) 🔴⨀🤟⏹(idhyate) 🚻⨀①(indhana) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①🆎(tāvat+mātra) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) ⏏️(yadā)  
here | this | should be | being kindled | the firewood | is | thus | if | by what | is to be kindled | the firewood | that | only that much | this | when  
If here this should be so: "the firewood is that which is being kindled",  
by what is that firewood to be kindled, when this is only that much?

10.5 anyo na prāpsyate’prāpto na dhakṣyatyadahan punaḥ na nirvāsyatyanirvāṇaḥ sthāsyate vā svaliṅgavān  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(prāpnoti) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+prāpta) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(dahati) 🚹⨀①🔽(at)🆎(na+dahat) ⏏️(punar) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(nirvāti) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+nirvāṇa) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(tiṣṭhati) ⏏️(vā) 🚹⨀①🆎(sva+liṅgavat)

Being other, it will not reach; not having reached, it will not burn; not burning, moreover,  
it will not be extinguished; or, not extinguished, it will remain, bearing its own mark.

10.6 anya evendhanādagnirindhanaṃ prāpnuyād yadi strī saṃprāpnoti puruṣaṃ puruṣaśca striyaṃ yathā

If the fire were wholly other than the firewood, it could reach the firewood,  
just as a woman reaches a man, and a man a woman.

10.7 anya evendhanādagnirindhanaṃ kāmamāpnuyāt agnīndhane yadi syātāmanyonyena tiraskṛte  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀⑤(indhana) 🚹⨀①(agni) 🚻⨀②(indhana) ⏏️(kāmam) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(āpnoti) 🚻⨁①🆎(agni+indhana) ⏏️(yadi) 🟢⨁🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀③(anyonya) 🚻⨁①🔽(ta)(tiraskṛta)

The fire, wholly other than the firewood, may well reach the firewood,  
if the fire and the firewood were set apart from one another.

10.8 yadīndhanamapekṣyāgnirapekṣyāgniṃ yadīndhanam katarat pūrvaniṣpannaṃ yadapekṣyāgnirindhanam

If the fire depends on the firewood, and if the firewood depends on the fire,  
which of the two is accomplished first, that depending on which there is fire or firewood?

10.9 yadīndhanamapekṣyāgniragneḥ siddhasya sādhanam evaṃ satīndhanaṃ cāpi bhaviṣyati niragnikam
