If the fire depends on the firewood, there is the establishing of a fire that is established;  
this being so, the firewood too will be without fire.

10.10 yo’pekṣya sidhyate bhāvastamevāpekṣya sidhyati yadi yo’pekṣitavyaḥ sa sidhyatāṃ kamapekṣya kaḥ

The existent that is established depending on another — depending on that very existent the other is established:  
if that which is to be depended on is to be established, which, depending on which?

10.11 yo’pekṣya sidhyate bhāvaḥ so’siddho’pekṣate katham athāpyapekṣate siddhastvapekṣāsya na yujyate

The existent that is established depending — how does it, not established, depend?  
Or if it depends once established, then its dependence is not fitting.

10.12 apekṣyendhanamagnirna nānapekṣyāgnirindhanam apekṣyendhanamagniṃ na nānapekṣyāgnimindhanam  
🔽(ya)(apekṣate) 🚻⨀②(indhana) 🚹⨀①(agni) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(na) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+apekṣate) 🚹⨀①(agni) 🚻⨀②(indhana) 🔽(ya)(apekṣate) 🚻⨀②(indhana) 🚹⨀②(agni) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(na) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+apekṣate) 🚹⨀②(agni) 🚻⨀①(indhana)

There is no fire depending on the firewood, nor fire not depending on the firewood;  
there is no firewood depending on the fire, nor firewood not depending on the fire.

10.13 āgacchatyanyato nāgnirindhane’gnirna vidyate atrendhane śeṣamuktaṃ gamyamānagatāgataiḥ  
🟢⨀🤟▶️(āgacchati) ⏏️(anyatas) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(agni) 🚻⨀⑦(indhana) 🚹⨀①(agni) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) ⏏️(atra) 🚻⨀⑦(indhana) 🚻⨀①(śeṣa) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(ukta) 🚻⨂③🆎(gamyamāna+gata+agata)

The fire does not come from elsewhere; the fire is not found in the firewood;  
here, as to the firewood, the rest has been stated by means of the being gone, the gone and the not gone.

10.14 indhanaṃ punaragnirna nāgniranyatra cendhanāt nāgnirindhanavān nāgnāvindhanāni na teṣu saḥ  
🚻⨀①(indhana) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀①(agni) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(agni) ⏏️(anyatra) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀⑤(indhana) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(agni) 🚹⨀①(indhanavat) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑦(agni) 🚻⨂①(indhana) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚻⨂⑦(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad)  
the firewood | again | the fire | not | not | the fire | elsewhere | and | than the firewood | not | the fire | possessing firewood | not | in the fire | the firewood | not | in them | it  
Again, the fire is not the firewood; nor is the fire elsewhere than the firewood;  
the fire does not possess firewood; the firewood is not in the fire, nor is the fire in it.

10.15 agnīndhanābhyāṃ vyākhyāta ātmopādānayoḥ kramaḥ sarvo niravaśeṣeṇa sārdhaṃ ghaṭapaṭādibhiḥ  
🚻⨁③🆎(agni+indhana) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(vyākhyāta) 🚻⨁⑥🆎(ātman+upādāna) 🚹⨀①(krama) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(sarva) ⏏️(niravaśeṣeṇa) ⏏️(sārdham) 🚹⨂③🆎(ghaṭa+paṭa+ādi)  
by fire and firewood | is explained | of the self and the fuel | the sequence | all of it | without remainder | together | with pot, cloth and the rest  
By fire and firewood the sequence of the self and the `upādāna` (fuel) is explained,  
all of it without remainder, together with pot, cloth and the rest.

10.16 ātmanaśca satattvaṃ ye bhāvānāṃ ca pṛthak pṛthak nirdiśanti na tān manye śāsanasyārthakovidān  
🚹⨀⑥(ātman) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀②🆎(sa+tattva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) 🚹⨂⑥(bhāva) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(pṛthak) ⏏️(pṛthak) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(nirdiśati) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(tad) 🟢⨀👆▶️(manyate) 🚻⨀⑥(śāsana) 🚹⨂②🆎(artha+kovida)  
of the self | and | the sameness | those who | of existents | and | separately | separately | declare | not | them | I think | of the teaching | experts in the meaning  
And those who declare the sameness of the self and of existents, and their separateness,  
I do not think them skilled in the meaning of the teaching.

</Tabs>

### §11 Saṃsāraparīkṣā (Examination of the cycle of rebirth)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**11.1** "The prior limit is not discerned", said the great sage; \
for `saṃsāra` (the cycle of rebirth) is without beginning or end: it has no beginning, nor any end.[^97]

**11.2** That which has neither beginning nor end — whence would it have a middle? \
Therefore the sequences of before, after and together are not possible here.

**11.3** If `jāti` (birth) were before and `jarāmaraṇa` (ageing and death) after, \
there would be `jāti` without `jarāmaraṇa`, and one who has not died would be born[^98].

**11.4** If `jāti` were after and `jarāmaraṇa` from the start, \
how would there be `jarāmaraṇa`, without primary cause, of one unborn?

**11.5** Nor is it fitting that `jarāmaraṇa` and `jāti` are together[^99]; \
one being born would die, and both would be without primary cause.

**11.6** Where these sequences of before, after and together do not arise, \
why do they proliferate[^100] that `jāti` and that `jarāmaraṇa`?

**11.7** Effect and cause, and the characterised and the characteristic, \
feeling and the feeler, and whatever things there are[^101] —

**11.8** Not only is the prior limit of `saṃsāra` not found; \
of all existents too[^102] the prior limit is not found.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**11.1** pūrvā prajñāyate koṭirnetyuvāca mahāmuniḥ | \
saṃsāro’navarāgro hi nāsyādirnāpi paścimam ||

**11.2** naivāgraṃ nāvaraṃ yasya tasya madhyaṃ kuto bhavet | \
tasmānnātropapadyante pūrvāparasahakramāḥ ||

**11.3** pūrvaṃ jātiryadi bhavejjarāmaraṇamuttaram | \
nirjarāmaraṇā jātirbhavejjāyeta cāmṛtaḥ ||

**11.4** paścājjātiryadi bhavejjarāmaraṇamāditaḥ | \
ahetukamajātasya syājjarāmaraṇaṃ katham ||

**11.5** na jarāmaraṇaṃ caiva jātiśca saha yujyate | \
mriyeta jāyamānaśca syāccāhetukatobhayoḥ ||

**11.6** yatra na prabhavantyete pūrvāparasahakramāḥ | \
prapañcayanti tāṃ jātiṃ tajjarāmaraṇaṃ ca kim ||

**11.7** kāryaṃ ca kāraṇaṃ caiva lakṣyaṃ lakṣaṇameva ca | \
vedanā vedakaścaiva santyarthā ye ca kecana ||

**11.8** pūrvā na vidyate koṭiḥ saṃsārasya na kevalam | \
sarveṣāmapi bhāvanāṃ pūrvā koṭī na vidyate ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">
11.1 pūrvā prajñāyate koṭirnetyuvāca mahāmuniḥ saṃsāro’navarāgro hi nāsyādirnāpi paścimam  
*️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(pūrva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prajñāyate) 🚺⨀①(koṭi) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(iti) 🟢⨀🤟🔄(vakti) 🚹⨀①🆎(mahā+muni) 🚹⨀①(saṃsāra) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+avara+agra) ⏏️(hi) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(idam) 🚹⨀①(ādi) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨀①(paścima)  
the prior | is discerned | limit | not | thus | said | the great sage | the cycle of rebirth | without beginning or end | for | not | of it | beginning | nor | even | end  
"The prior limit is not discerned", said the great sage;  
for `saṃsāra` (the cycle of rebirth) is without beginning or end: it has no beginning, nor any end.

11.2 naivāgraṃ nāvaraṃ yasya tasya madhyaṃ kuto bhavet tasmānnātropapadyante pūrvāparasahakramāḥ  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀①(agra) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①(avara) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(tad) 🚻⨀①(madhya) ⏏️(kutas) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) ⏏️(tasmāt) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(atra) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(upapadyate) 🚹⨂①🆎(pūrva+apara+saha+krama)  
not | at all | beginning | nor | end | of which | of that | middle | whence | would be | therefore | not | here | are possible | the sequences of before, after and together  
That which has neither beginning nor end — whence would it have a middle?  
Therefore the sequences of before, after and together are not possible here.

11.3 pūrvaṃ jātiryadi bhavejjarāmaraṇamuttaram nirjarāmaraṇā jātirbhavejjāyeta cāmṛtaḥ  
⏏️(pūrvam) 🚺⨀①(jāti) ⏏️(yadi) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚻⨀①🆎(jarā+maraṇa) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(uttara) 🚺⨀①🆎(niḥ+jarā+maraṇa) 🚺⨀①(jāti) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🔴⨀🤟⏯(jāyate) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+mṛta)  
before | birth | if | should be | ageing and death | after | without ageing and death | birth | would be | would be born | and | one who has not died  
If `jāti` (birth) were before and `jarāmaraṇa` (ageing and death) after,  
there would be `jāti` without `jarāmaraṇa`, and one who has not died would be born.

11.4 paścājjātiryadi bhavejjarāmaraṇamāditaḥ ahetukamajātasya syājjarāmaraṇaṃ katham  
⏏️(paścāt) 🚺⨀①(jāti) ⏏️(yadi) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚻⨀①🆎(jarā+maraṇa) ⏏️(āditas) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+hetuka) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)🆎(na+jāta) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀①🆎(jarā+maraṇa) ⏏️(katham)  
after | birth | if | should be | ageing and death | from the start | without primary cause | of the unborn | would be | ageing and death | how  
If `jāti` were after and `jarāmaraṇa` from the start,  
how would there be `jarāmaraṇa`, without primary cause, of one unborn?

11.5 na jarāmaraṇaṃ caiva jātiśca saha yujyate mriyeta jāyamānaśca syāccāhetukatobhayoḥ  
⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🆎(jarā+maraṇa) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) 🚺⨀①(jāti) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(saha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) 🔴⨀🤟⏯(mriyate) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(jāyamāna) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) ⏏️(ca) 🚺⨀①🔽(tā)(ahetukatā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨁⑥(ubha)  
not | ageing and death | and | indeed | birth | and | together | is fitting | would die | being born | and | would be | and | absence of primary cause | of both  
Nor is it fitting that `jarāmaraṇa` and `jāti` are together;  
one being born would die, and both would be without primary cause.

11.6 yatra na prabhavantyete pūrvāparasahakramāḥ prapañcayanti tāṃ jātiṃ tajjarāmaraṇaṃ ca kim  
⏏️(yatra) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(prabhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(etad) 🚹⨂①🆎(pūrva+apara+saha+krama) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(prapañcayati) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀②(tad) 🚺⨀②(jāti) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(tad) 🚻⨀②🆎(jarā+maraṇa) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(kim)  
where | not | arise | these | the sequences of before, after and together | proliferate | that | birth | that | ageing and death | and | why  
Where these sequences of before, after and together do not arise,  
why do they proliferate that `jāti` and that `jarāmaraṇa`?

effect | and | cause | and | indeed | the characterised | the characteristic | indeed | and | feeling | the feeler | and | indeed | there are | things | which | and | any  
Effect and cause, and the characterised and the characteristic,

*️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(pūrva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚺⨀①(koṭi) 🚹⨀⑥(saṃsāra) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(kevalam) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑥(sarva) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨂⑥(bhāva) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(pūrva) 🚺⨀①(koṭi) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
the prior | not | is found | limit | of the cycle of rebirth | not | only | of all | too | of existents | the prior | limit | not | is found  
Not only is the prior limit of `saṃsāra` not found;  
of all existents too the prior limit is not found.

### §12 Duḥkhaparīkṣā (Examination of suffering)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**12.1** Made by itself, made by another, made by both, without primary cause: \
so some hold `duḥkha` (suffering) to be; and that is not fit to be made[^103].

**12.2** If it were made by itself, it would not then come to be dependently; \
for those `skandhas` (collections) come to be in dependence on these `skandhas`.

**12.3** If these were other than those, or if those were other than these, \
`duḥkha` would be made by another, those made by these others[^104].

**12.4** If `duḥkha` is made by its own person, then, apart from `duḥkha`, \
which is that own person[^105] by whom `duḥkha` is made by itself?

**12.5** If `duḥkha` is born of another person, he to whom it is given[^106] — \
that `duḥkha` having been made by another — whence is he, apart from `duḥkha`?

**12.6** If `duḥkha` is born of another person, who is the other person \
who, apart from `duḥkha`, having made it, sends it to another?[^107]

**12.7** Since the self-made is not established, whence is `duḥkha` made by another? \
For whatever `duḥkha` another would make[^108], that would be self-made for him[^109].

**12.8** First, `duḥkha` is not self-made, for it is not made by that very one[^110]; \
if the other is not made by itself, how would `duḥkha` be made by another?

**12.9** `duḥkha` would be made by both, if it could be made by each one[^111]; \
whence `duḥkha` without primary cause, not made by another and not made by itself?

**12.10** For not only is the fourfoldness of `duḥkha` not found; \
the fourfoldness of external existents[^112] too is not found.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**12.1** svayaṃkṛtaṃ parakṛtaṃ dvābhyāṃ kṛtamahetukam | \
duḥkhamityeka icchanti tacca kāryaṃ na yujyate ||

**12.2** svayaṃkṛtaṃ yadi bhavet pratītya na tato bhavet | \
skandhānimānamī skandhāḥ saṃbhavanti pratītya hi ||

**12.3** yadyamībhya ime’nye syurebhyo vāmī pare yadi | \
bhavet parakṛtaṃ duḥkhaṃ parairebhiramī kṛtāḥ ||

**12.4** svapudgalakṛtaṃ duḥkhaṃ yadi duḥkhaṃ punarvinā | \
svapudgalaḥ sa katamo yena duḥkhaṃ svayaṃkṛtam ||

**12.5** parapudgalajaṃ duḥkhaṃ yadi yasmai pradīyate | \
pareṇa kṛtvā tadduḥkhaṃ sa duḥkhena vinā kutaḥ ||

**12.6** [ parapudgalajaṃ duḥkhaṃ yadi kaḥ parapudgalaḥ | \
vinā duḥkhena yaḥ kṛtvā parasmai prahiṇoti tat ||

**12.7** ] svayaṃ kṛtasyāprasiddherduḥkhaṃ parakṛtaṃ kutaḥ | \
paro hi duḥkhaṃ yad kuryāt tat tasya syāt svayaṃkṛtam ||

**12.8** na tāvat svakṛtaṃ duḥkhaṃ na hi tenaiva tat kṛtam | \
paro nātmakṛtaścet syād duḥkhaṃ parakṛtaṃ katham ||

**12.9** syādubhābhyāṃ kṛtaṃ duḥkhaṃ syādekaikakṛtaṃ yadi | \
parākārāsvayaṃkāraṃ duḥkhamahetukaṃ kutaḥ ||

**12.10** na kevalaṃ hi duḥkhasya cāturvidhyaṃ na vidyate | \
bāhyānāmapi bhāvānāṃ cāturvidhyaṃ na vidyate ||

### §13 Tattvaparīkṣā (Examination of reality)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**13.1** 'That is false which is of deceptive quality' — thus the Bhagavān spoke; \
and all `saṃskāras` (mental constructions)[^113] are of deceptive quality; therefore they are false[^114].

**13.2** If that is false which is of deceptive quality, what is deceived there? \
But this was spoken by the Bhagavān as illuminating `śūnyatā` (emptiness).[^115]

**13.3** The lack of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) in existents is from the seeing of alteration; \
and there is no existent without `svabhāva`, since there is the `śūnyatā` of existents.

**13.4** Of what would there be alteration, if `svabhāva` is not found? \
Of what would there be alteration, if `svabhāva` is found?

**13.5** Alteration is not fitting of that very thing, nor indeed of another; \
since a youth does not age, since the aged does not age.

**13.6** If there were alteration of that, milk itself would be curd; \
or else, of what other than milk[^116] will there be the state of curd?

**13.7** If there were anything not empty, there would be something empty too[^117]; \
and there is nothing not empty; whence will the empty be?

**13.8** `Śūnyatā` was proclaimed by the Conquerors as the escape from all `dṛṣṭis` (views); \
but those whose `dṛṣṭi` is `śūnyatā`, those they declared incurable[^118].

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**13.1** tanmṛṣā moṣadharma yad bhagavānityabhāṣata | \
sarve ca moṣadharmāṇaḥ saṃskārāstena te mṛṣā ||

**13.2** tanmṛṣā moṣadharma yad yadi kiṃ tatra muṣyate | \
etat tūktaṃ bhagavatā śūnyatāparidīpakam ||

**13.3** bhāvānāṃ niḥsvabhāvatvamanyathābhāvadarśanāt | \
nāsvabhāvaśca bhāvo’sti bhāvānāṃ śūnyatā yataḥ ||

**13.4** kasya syādanyathābhāvaḥ svabhāvaścenna vidyate | \
kasya syādanyathābhāvaḥ svabhāvo yadi vidyate ||

**13.5** tasyaiva nānyathābhāvo nāpyanyasyaiva yujyate | \
yuvā na jīryate yasmād yasmājjīrṇo na jīryate ||

**13.6** tasya cedanyathābhāvaḥ kṣīrameva bhaved dadhi | \
kṣīrādanyasya kasyātha dadhibhāvo bhaviṣyati ||

**13.7** yadyaśūnyaṃ bhavet kiṃcit syācchūnyamapi kiṃcana | \
na kiṃcidastyaśūnyaṃ ca kutaḥ śūnyaṃ bhaviṣyati ||

**13.8** śūnyatā sarvadṛṣṭīnāṃ proktā niḥsaraṇaṃ jinaiḥ | \
yeṣāṃ tu śūnyatādṛṣṭistānasādhyān babhāṣire ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">
13.1 tanmṛṣā moṣadharma yad bhagavānityabhāṣata sarve ca moṣadharmāṇaḥ saṃskārāstena te mṛṣā

that | false | of deceptive quality | which | the Bhagavān | thus | spoke | all | and | of deceptive quality | mental constructions | therefore | they | false  
'That is false which is of deceptive quality' — thus the Bhagavān spoke;  
and all `saṃskāras` (mental constructions) are of deceptive quality; therefore they are false.

13.2 tanmṛṣā moṣadharma yad yadi kiṃ tatra muṣyate etat tūktaṃ bhagavatā śūnyatāparidīpakam

If that is false which is of deceptive quality, what is deceived there?

13.3 bhāvānāṃ niḥsvabhāvatvamanyathābhāvadarśanāt nāsvabhāvaśca bhāvo’sti bhāvānāṃ śūnyatā yataḥ  
🚹⨂⑥(bhāva) 🚻⨀①🔽(tva)(niḥsvabhāvatva) 🚻⨀⑤🆎(anyathā+bhāva+darśana) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+sva+bhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) 🚹⨂⑥(bhāva) 🚺⨀①(śūnyatā) ⏏️(yatas)  
of existents | the lack of intrinsic nature | from the seeing of alteration | not | without intrinsic nature | and | an existent | exists | of existents | emptiness | since  
The lack of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) in existents is from the seeing of alteration;  
and there is no existent without `svabhāva`, since there is the `śūnyatā` of existents.

If there were anything not empty, there would be something empty too;

🚺⨀①(śūnyatā) 🚺⨂⑥🆎(sarva+dṛṣṭi) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(prokta) 🚻⨀①(niḥsaraṇa) 🚹⨂③(jina) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑥(yad) ⏏️(tu) 🚺⨀①🆎(śūnyatā+dṛṣṭi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(tad) 🚹⨂②🔽(ya)🆎(na+sādhya) 🔵⨂🤟🔄(bhāṣate)  
emptiness | of all views | proclaimed | the escape | by the Conquerors | of whom | but | the view of emptiness | those | incurable | they declared  
`Śūnyatā` was proclaimed by the Conquerors as the escape from all `dṛṣṭis` (views);  
but those whose `dṛṣṭi` is `śūnyatā`, those they declared incurable.

### §14 Saṃsargaparīkṣā (Examination of conjunction)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**14.1** The visible, the seeing, the seer: these three, two by two \
and altogether, do not indeed go into conjunction with one another.

**14.2** Thus let passion, the impassioned and what is to be impassioned be seen; \
in the threefold way[^119], the remaining `kleśas` (defilements) too, and the remaining `āyatanas` (sense bases).

**14.3** Conjunction is of another with another, and that otherness is not found[^120] \
of the visible and the rest; hence they do not go into conjunction.

**14.4** And not only is the otherness of the visible and the rest not found; \
the otherness of anything together with anything[^121] is not possible.

**14.5** Another is another in dependence on another[^122]; another is not another apart from another; \
and for what depends on something to be other than it is not possible[^123].

**14.6** If another were other than another, it would be so even apart from the other[^124]; \
that other is not other apart from the other; and hence it does not exist.

**14.7** Otherness is not found in another; it is not found in a non-other; \
and otherness being not found, there is neither another nor that very thing.

**14.8** The conjunction of that with that is not fitting, nor of another with another; \
the being conjoined, the conjoined and the conjoiner are not found.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**14.1** draṣṭavyaṃ darśanaṃ draṣṭā trīṇyetāni dviśo dviśaḥ | \
sarvaśaśca na saṃsargamanyonyena vrajantyuta ||

**14.2** evaṃ rāgaśca raktaśca rañjanīyaṃ ca dṛśyatām | \
traidhena śeṣāḥ kleśāśca śeṣāṇyāyatanāni ca ||

**14.3** anyenānyasya saṃsargastaccānyatvaṃ na vidhate | \
draṣṭavyaprabhṛtīnāṃ yanna saṃsargaṃ vrajantyataḥ ||

**14.4** na ca kevalamanyatvaṃ draṣṭavyāderna vidyate | \
kasyacit kenacit sārdhaṃ nānyatvamupapadyate ||

**14.5** anyadanyat pratītyānyān nānyadanyadṛte’nyataḥ | \
yat pratītya ca yat tasmāt tadanyannopapadyate ||

**14.6** yadyanyadanyadanyasmādanyasmādapyṛte bhavet | \
tadanyadanyadanyasmādṛte nāsti ca nāstyataḥ ||

**14.7** nānyasmin vidyate’nyatvamananyasminna vidyate | \
avidyamāne cānyatve nāstyanyad vā tadeva vā ||

**14.8** na tena tasya saṃsargo nānyenānyasya yujyate | \
saṃsṛjyamānaṃ saṃsṛṣṭaṃ saṃsraṣṭā ca na vidyate ||

</TabItem>

### §15 Bhāvābhāvaparīkṣā (Examination of the existent and the non-existent)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**15.1** The coming to be of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) through causes and primary causes is not fitting; \
a `svabhāva` come to be from primary causes and causes would be artificial.

**15.2** How, again, will a `svabhāva` be called artificial[^125]? \
For `svabhāva` is not artificial and is independent of another[^126].

**15.3** In the non-existence of `svabhāva`, whence will `parabhāva` (extrinsic nature) be? \
For the `svabhāva` of another existent[^127] is called `parabhāva`.

**15.4** Apart from `svabhāva` and `parabhāva`, whence, again, an existent? \
For when there is `svabhāva` and `parabhāva`[^128], an existent is established.

**15.5** If the existent is unestablished, the non-existent is not established at all; \
for people call the alteration of an existent the non-existent.

**15.6** `Svabhāva` and `parabhāva`, and the existent and the non-existent too: \
those who see these do not see `tattva` (reality) in the Buddha's teaching.

**15.7** And in the Instruction to Kātyāyana both 'it is' and 'it is not' \
were denied by the Bhagavān, who discerns the existent and the non-existent.

**15.8** If existence were by nature, there would be no non-existence of it; \
for the alteration of a nature is never possible.

**15.9** Or, when the nature does not exist[^129], of what will there be change? \
Or, when the nature exists, of what will there be change?

**15.10** 'It is' is grasping at the eternal; 'it is not' is the view of annihilation; \
therefore the clear-sighted one should not rely on[^130] existence and non-existence.

**15.11** For what exists by `svabhāva`, that is not non-existent — thus the eternal; \
'it is not now, it was before' — thus annihilation follows.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**15.1** na saṃbhavaḥ svabhāvasya yuktaḥ pratyayahetubhiḥ | \
hetupratyayasaṃbhūtaḥ svabhāvaḥ kṛtako bhavet ||

**15.2** svabhāvaḥ kṛtako nāma bhaviṣyati punaḥ katham | \
akṛtrimaḥ svabhāvo hi nirapekṣaḥ paratra ca ||

**15.3** kutaḥ svabhāvasyābhāve parabhāvo bhaviṣyati | \
svabhāvaḥ parabhāvasya parabhāvo hi kathyate ||

**15.4** svabhāvaparabhāvābhyāmṛte bhāvaḥ kutaḥ punaḥ | \
svabhāve parabhāve ca sati bhāvo hi sidhyati ||

**15.5** bhāvasya cedaprasiddhirabhāvo naiva sidhyati | \
bhāvasya hyanyathābhāvamabhāvaṃ bruvate janāḥ ||

**15.6** svabhāvaṃ parabhāvaṃ ca bhāvaṃ cābhāvameva ca | \
ye paśyanti na paśyanti te tattvaṃ buddhaśāsane ||

**15.7** kātyāyanāvavāde cāstīti nāstīti cobhayam | \
pratiṣiddhaṃ bhagavatā bhāvābhāvavibhāvinā ||

**15.8** yadyastitvaṃ prakṛtyā syānna bhavedasya nāstitā | \
prakṛteranyathābhāvo na hi jātūpapadyate ||

**15.9** prakṛtau kasya vāsatyāmanyathātvaṃ bhaviṣyati | \
prakṛtau kasya vā satyāmanyathātvaṃ bhaviṣyati ||

**15.10** astīti śāśvatagrāho nāstītyucchedadarśanam | \
tasmādastitvanāstitve nāśrīyet vicakṣaṇaḥ ||

**15.11** asti yaddhi svabhāvena na tannāstīti śāśvatam | \
nāstīdānīmabhūt pūrvamityucchedaḥ prasajyate ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">
15.1 na saṃbhavaḥ svabhāvasya yuktaḥ pratyayahetubhiḥ hetupratyayasaṃbhūtaḥ svabhāvaḥ kṛtako bhavet  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(yukta) 🚹⨂③🆎(pratyaya+hetu) 🚹⨀①🆎(hetu+pratyaya+saṃbhūta) 🚹⨀①🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀①(kṛtaka) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati)  
not | coming to be | of intrinsic nature | fitting | through causes and primary causes | come to be from primary causes and causes | intrinsic nature | artificial | would be  
The coming to be of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) through causes and primary causes is not fitting;  
a `svabhāva` come to be from primary causes and causes would be artificial.

15.2 svabhāvaḥ kṛtako nāma bhaviṣyati punaḥ katham akṛtrimaḥ svabhāvo hi nirapekṣaḥ paratra ca  
🚹⨀①🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀①(kṛtaka) ⏏️(nāma) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) ⏏️(punar) ⏏️(katham) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+kṛtrima) 🚹⨀①🆎(sva+bhāva) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀①(nirapekṣa) ⏏️(paratra) ⏏️(ca)  
intrinsic nature | artificial | called | will be | again | how | not artificial | intrinsic nature | for | independent | of another | and  
How, again, will a `svabhāva` be called artificial?  
For `svabhāva` is not artificial and is independent of another.

15.3 kutaḥ svabhāvasyābhāve parabhāvo bhaviṣyati svabhāvaḥ parabhāvasya parabhāvo hi kathyate  
⏏️(kutas) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀⑦(abhāva) 🚹⨀①🆎(para+bhāva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚹⨀①🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(para+bhāva) 🚹⨀①🆎(para+bhāva) ⏏️(hi) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(kathyate)  
whence | of intrinsic nature | in the non-existence | extrinsic nature | will be | intrinsic nature | of another existent | extrinsic nature | for | is called  
In the non-existence of `svabhāva`, whence will `parabhāva` (extrinsic nature) be?  
For the `svabhāva` of another existent is called `parabhāva`.

15.4 svabhāvaparabhāvābhyāmṛte bhāvaḥ kutaḥ punaḥ svabhāve parabhāve ca sati bhāvo hi sidhyati  
🚹⨁⑤🆎(sva+bhāva+para+bhāva) ⏏️(ṛte) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(kutas) ⏏️(punar) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(para+bhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(hi) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(sidhyati)  
from intrinsic nature and extrinsic nature | apart from | an existent | whence | again | in intrinsic nature | in extrinsic nature | and | there being | an existent | for | is established  
Apart from `svabhāva` and `parabhāva`, whence, again, an existent?  
For when there is `svabhāva` and `parabhāva`, an existent is established.

15.5 bhāvasya cedaprasiddhirabhāvo naiva sidhyati bhāvasya hyanyathābhāvamabhāvaṃ bruvate janāḥ  
🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) ⏏️(cet) 🚺⨀①🆎(na+prasiddhi) 🚹⨀①(abhāva) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(sidhyati) 🚹⨀⑥(bhāva) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀②🆎(anyathā+bhāva) 🚹⨀②(abhāva) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(bravīti) 🚹⨂①(jana)  
of the existent | if | non-establishment | the non-existent | not | at all | is established | of an existent | for | the alteration | the non-existent | call | people  
If the existent is unestablished, the non-existent is not established at all;  
for people call the alteration of an existent the non-existent.

15.6 svabhāvaṃ parabhāvaṃ ca bhāvaṃ cābhāvameva ca ye paśyanti na paśyanti te tattvaṃ buddhaśāsane  
🚹⨀②🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀②🆎(para+bhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀②(bhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀②(abhāva) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(paśyati) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(paśyati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚻⨀②🔽(tva)(tattva) 🚻⨀⑦🆎(buddha+śāsana)  
intrinsic nature | extrinsic nature | and | the existent | and | the non-existent | indeed | and | those who | see | not | see | they | reality | in the Buddha's teaching  
`Svabhāva` and `parabhāva`, and the existent and the non-existent too:  
those who see these do not see `tattva` (reality) in the Buddha's teaching.

15.7 kātyāyanāvavāde cāstīti nāstīti cobhayam pratiṣiddhaṃ bhagavatā bhāvābhāvavibhāvinā  
🚹⨀⑦🆎(kātyāyana+avavāda) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ubhaya) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(pratiṣiddha) 🚹⨀③(bhagavat) 🚹⨀③🆎(bhāva+abhāva+vibhāvin)  
in the Instruction to Kātyāyana | and | it is | thus | it is not | thus | and | both | denied | by the Bhagavān | discerning the existent and the non-existent  
And in the Instruction to Kātyāyana both 'it is' and 'it is not'  
were denied by the Bhagavān, who discerns the existent and the non-existent.

15.8 yadyastitvaṃ prakṛtyā syānna bhavedasya nāstitā prakṛteranyathābhāvo na hi jātūpapadyate  
⏏️(yadi) 🚻⨀①🔽(tva)(astitva) 🚺⨀③(prakṛti) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(idam) 🚺⨀①🔽(tā)(nāstitā) 🚺⨀⑥(prakṛti) 🚹⨀①🆎(anyathā+bhāva) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi) ⏏️(jātu) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
if | existence | by nature | were | not | would be | of it | non-existence | of a nature | alteration | not | for | ever | is possible  
If existence were by nature, there would be no non-existence of it;  
for the alteration of a nature is never possible.

15.9 prakṛtau kasya vāsatyāmanyathātvaṃ bhaviṣyati prakṛtau kasya vā satyāmanyathātvaṃ bhaviṣyati  
🚺⨀⑦(prakṛti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀⑦🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) 🚻⨀①🔽(tva)(anyathātva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚺⨀⑦(prakṛti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat) 🚻⨀①🔽(tva)(anyathātva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
the nature | of what | or | not existing | change | will there be | the nature | of what | or | existing | change | will there be  
Or, when the nature does not exist, of what will there be change?  
Or, when the nature exists, of what will there be change?

15.10 astīti śāśvatagrāho nāstītyucchedadarśanam tasmādastitvanāstitve nāśrīyet vicakṣaṇaḥ  
🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀①🆎(śāśvata+grāha) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(iti) 🚻⨀①🆎(uccheda+darśana) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚻⨁②🆎(astitva+nāstitva) ⏏️(na) 🔴⨀🤟⏯(āśrīyate) 🚹⨀①(vicakṣaṇa)  
it is | thus | grasping at the eternal | it is not | thus | the view of annihilation | therefore | existence and non-existence | not | should rely on | the clear-sighted one  
'It is' is grasping at the eternal; 'it is not' is the view of annihilation;  
therefore the clear-sighted one should not rely on existence and non-existence.

15.11 asti yaddhi svabhāvena na tannāstīti śāśvatam nāstīdānīmabhūt pūrvamityucchedaḥ prasajyate  
🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(iti) 🚻⨀①(śāśvata) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(idānīm) 🟢⨀🤟⏮(bhavati) ⏏️(pūrvam) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀①(uccheda) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate)  
exists | what | for | by intrinsic nature | not | that | is not | thus | the eternal | it is not | now | it was | before | thus | annihilation | follows  
For what exists by `svabhāva`, that is not non-existent — thus the eternal;  
'it is not now, it was before' — thus annihilation follows.

### §16 Bandhanamokṣaparīkṣā (Examination of bondage and release)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**16.1** If `saṃskāras` (mental constructions)[^131] transmigrate, they do not transmigrate as permanent ones; \
nor do they transmigrate as impermanent ones; for a being too, this is the same procedure.

**16.2** If a person transmigrates — in the `skandhas` (collections), the `āyatanas` (sense bases) and the `dhātus` (components) \
sought in five ways, that one does not exist; who will transmigrate?

**16.3** Transmigrating from `upādāna` (fuel)[^132] to `upādāna`, that one would be free from existence[^133]; \
and free from existence and without `upādāna`, who is that one, what will transmigrate?[^134]

**16.4** The `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) of `saṃskāras` is not possible in any way; \
the `nirvāṇa` of a being, too, is not possible in any way.

**16.5** Those whose nature is to rise and pass away are not bound, are not released — \
the `saṃskāras`; as before, a being is not bound, is not released.

**16.6** If bondage is `upādāna`, one with `upādāna` is not bound; \
one without `upādāna` is not bound; then in what state is one bound?

**16.7** Bondage would bind, granted[^135], if it existed[^136] before the one to be bound; \
and it does not exist; the rest has been said through the being gone, the gone and the not gone.

**16.8** The bound, to begin with, is not released; the unbound is not released at all; \
if the bound were being released, binding and releasing would be simultaneous.

**16.9** 'Without `upādāna` I shall be extinguished, `nirvāṇa` will be mine' — \
those whose grasping is thus, theirs is the great grasping of `upādāna`[^137].

**16.10** Where there is no superimposing of `nirvāṇa`, no removing of `saṃsāra` (cycle of rebirth), \
there what `saṃsāra`, what `nirvāṇa`, is imagined?[^138]

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**16.1** saṃskārāḥ saṃsaranti cenna nityāḥ saṃsaranti te | \
saṃsaranti ca nānityāḥ sattve’pyeṣa samaḥ kramaḥ ||

**16.2** pudgalaḥ saṃsarati cet skandhāyatanadhātuṣu | \
pañcadhā mṛgyamāṇo’sau nāsti kaḥ saṃsariṣyati ||

**16.3** upādānādupādānaṃ saṃsaran vibhavo bhavet | \
vibhavaścānupādānaḥ kaḥ sa kiṃ saṃsarriṣyati ||

**16.4** saṃskārāṇāṃ na nirvāṇaṃ kathaṃcidupapadyate | \
sattvasyāpi na nirvāṇaṃ kathaṃcidupapadyate ||

**16.5** na badhyante na mucyanta udayavyayadharmiṇaḥ | \
saṃskārāḥ pūrvavat sattvo badhyate na na mucyate ||

**16.6** bandhanaṃ cedupādānaṃ sopādāno na badhyate | \
badhyate nānupādānaḥ kimavastho’tha badhyate ||

**16.7** badhnīyād bandhanaṃ kāmaṃ bandhyāt pūrvaṃ bhavet yadi | \
na cāsti taccheṣamuktaṃ gamyamānagatāgataiḥ ||

**16.8** baddho na mucyate tāvadabaddho naiva mucyate | \
syātāṃ baddhe mucyamāne yugapad bandhamokṣaṇe ||

**16.9** nirvāsyāmyanupādāno nirvāṇaṃ me bhaviṣyati | \
iti yeṣāṃ grahasteṣāmupādānamahāgrahaḥ ||

**16.10** na nirvāṇasamāropo na saṃsārāpakarṣaṇam | \
yatra kastatra saṃsāro nirvāṇaṃ kiṃ vikalpyate ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">

If `saṃskāras` (mental constructions) transmigrate, they do not transmigrate as permanent ones;  
nor do they transmigrate as impermanent ones; for a being too, this is the same procedure.

16.2 pudgalaḥ saṃsarati cet skandhāyatanadhātuṣu pañcadhā mṛgyamāṇo’sau nāsti kaḥ saṃsariṣyati  
🚹⨀①(pudgala) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(saṃsarati) ⏏️(cet) 🚹⨂⑦🆎(skandha+āyatana+dhātu) ⏏️(pañcadhā) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(mṛgyamāṇa) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(adas) ⏏️(nāsti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kim) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(saṃsarati)  
a person | transmigrates | if | in the collections, sense bases and components | in five ways | sought | that one | does not exist | who | will transmigrate

sought in five ways, that one does not exist; who will transmigrate?

16.3 upādānādupādānaṃ saṃsaran vibhavo bhavet vibhavaścānupādānaḥ kaḥ sa kiṃ saṃsarriṣyati  
🚻⨀⑤(upādāna) 🚻⨀②(upādāna) 🚹⨀①🔽(at)(saṃsarat) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+upādāna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kim) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(saṃsarati)

Transmigrating from `upādāna` (fuel) to `upādāna`, that one would be free from existence;  
and free from existence and without `upādāna`, who is that one, what will transmigrate?

16.4 saṃskārāṇāṃ na nirvāṇaṃ kathaṃcidupapadyate sattvasyāpi na nirvāṇaṃ kathaṃcidupapadyate  
🚹⨂⑥(saṃskāra) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①(nirvāṇa) ⏏️(kathaṃcit) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) 🚹⨀⑥(sattva) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①(nirvāṇa) ⏏️(kathaṃcit) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
of the mental constructions | not | extinguishment | in any way | is possible | of a being | also | not | extinguishment | in any way | is possible  
The `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) of `saṃskāras` is not possible in any way;  
the `nirvāṇa` of a being, too, is not possible in any way.

16.5 na badhyante na mucyanta udayavyayadharmiṇaḥ saṃskārāḥ pūrvavat sattvo badhyate na na mucyate  
⏏️(na) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(badhyate) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(mucyate) 🚹⨂①🆎(udaya+vyaya+dharmin) 🚹⨂①(saṃskāra) ⏏️(pūrvavat) 🚹⨀①(sattva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(badhyate) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(mucyate)

Those whose nature is to rise and pass away are not bound, are not released —  
the `saṃskāras`; as before, a being is not bound, is not released.

16.6 bandhanaṃ cedupādānaṃ sopādāno na badhyate badhyate nānupādānaḥ kimavastho’tha badhyate  
🚻⨀①(bandhana) ⏏️(cet) 🚻⨀①(upādāna) 🚹⨀①🆎(sa+upādāna) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(badhyate) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(badhyate) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+upādāna) 🚹⨀①🆎(kim+avastha) ⏏️(atha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(badhyate)

If bondage is `upādāna`, one with `upādāna` is not bound;  
one without `upādāna` is not bound; then in what state is one bound?

16.7 badhnīyād bandhanaṃ kāmaṃ bandhyāt pūrvaṃ bhavet yadi na cāsti taccheṣamuktaṃ gamyamānagatāgataiḥ  
🟢⨀🤟⏯(badhnāti) 🚻⨀①(bandhana) ⏏️(kāmam) 🚹⨀⑤🔽(ya)(bandhya) ⏏️(pūrvam) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) ⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①(śeṣa) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(ukta) 🚻⨂③🆎(gamyamāna+gata+agata)  
would bind | bondage | granted | the one to be bound | before | it were | if | not | and | it exists | that | the rest | has been said | through the being gone, the gone and the not gone  
Bondage would bind, granted, if it existed before the one to be bound;  
and it does not exist; the rest has been said through the being gone, the gone and the not gone.

16.8 baddho na mucyate tāvadabaddho naiva mucyate syātāṃ baddhe mucyamāne yugapad bandhamokṣaṇe  
🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(baddha) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(mucyate) ⏏️(tāvat) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+baddha) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(mucyate) 🟢⨁🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(baddha) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(māna)(mucyamāna) ⏏️(yugapad) 🚻⨁①🆎(bandha+mokṣaṇa)  
the bound | not | is released | to begin with | the unbound | not | at all | is released | would be | if the bound | were being released | simultaneously | binding and releasing  
The bound, to begin with, is not released; the unbound is not released at all;  
if the bound were being released, binding and releasing would be simultaneous.

16.9 nirvāsyāmyanupādāno nirvāṇaṃ me bhaviṣyati iti yeṣāṃ grahasteṣāmupādānamahāgrahaḥ  
🟢⨀👆⏭(nirvāti) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+upādāna) 🚻⨀①(nirvāṇa) *️⃣👆⚧⨀⑥(asmad) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) ⏏️(iti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑥(yad) 🚹⨀①(graha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑥(tad) 🚹⨀①🆎(upādāna+mahā+graha)  
I shall be extinguished | without fuel | extinguishment | mine | will be | thus | whose | grasping | theirs | the great grasping of fuel  
'Without `upādāna` I shall be extinguished, `nirvāṇa` will be mine' —  
those whose grasping is thus, theirs is the great grasping of `upādāna`.

16.10 na nirvāṇasamāropo na saṃsārāpakarṣaṇam yatra kastatra saṃsāro nirvāṇaṃ kiṃ vikalpyate  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(nirvāṇa+samāropa) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🆎(saṃsāra+apakarṣaṇa) ⏏️(yatra) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kim) ⏏️(tatra) 🚹⨀①(saṃsāra) 🚻⨀①(nirvāṇa) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vikalpyate)  
not | superimposing of extinguishment | not | removing of the cycle of rebirth | where | what | there | cycle of rebirth | extinguishment | what | is imagined  
Where there is no superimposing of `nirvāṇa`, no removing of `saṃsāra` (cycle of rebirth),  
there what `saṃsāra`, what `nirvāṇa`, is imagined?

</TabItem>
</Tabs>

### §17 Karmaphalaparīkṣā (Examination of action and fruit)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**17.1** The mind that restrains oneself and that favours others, \
of goodwill[^139]: that is `dharma` (righteousness); that is the seed of fruit after death and here.

**17.2** Intention, and having intended, were declared to be `karman` (action) by the supreme seer; \
of that `karman` a division of many kinds has been proclaimed.

**17.3** There, the `karman` that is called "intention" is held to be mental; \
but that which is called "having intended" is bodily and verbal.

**17.4** Speech and movement, and the non-abstentions[^140] designated "non-intimation", \
and likewise the other non-intimations, held to be abstentions,

**17.5** merit consequent on enjoyment, and demerit of the same kind, \
and intention: these seven `dharmas`[^141] are held to be the manifestations of `karman`.

**17.6** If `karman` endures until the time of ripening[^142], it would go to permanence; \
if it has ceased, being ceased, what fruit will it generate?

**17.7** The continuum beginning with the sprout, which proceeds from the seed, \
from that is the fruit; and without the seed it does not proceed.

**17.8** And since from the seed is the continuum, and from the continuum the coming forth of the fruit, \
the fruit, preceded by the seed, is therefore neither annihilated nor eternal.

**17.9** The continuum of mind which proceeds from that mind[^143], \
from that is the fruit; and without the mind it does not proceed.

**17.10** And since from the mind is the continuum, and from the continuum the coming forth of the fruit, \
the fruit, preceded by `karman`, is therefore neither annihilated nor eternal.

**17.11** The means of accomplishing `dharma`[^144] are the ten white paths of `karman`; \
the fruit of `dharma`, after death and here, is the five qualities of desire.

**17.12** Many and great would be the faults, if the conception \
were this; therefore this conception is not at all possible here.

**17.13** But I shall declare this conception, which is applied here[^145], \
described by the Buddhas, by the solitary Buddhas and by the disciples.

**17.14** The non-perishing is like a document[^146], and so `karman` is like a debt; \
it is fourfold by `dhātu` (component)[^147], and by nature it is indeterminate.

**17.15** It is not to be abandoned by abandonment, or else it is to be abandoned by cultivation alone; \
therefore the fruit of `karmans` is born through the non-perishing.

**17.16** If it were to be abandoned by abandonment, or by the transference of `karman`[^148], \
faults would follow there, the destruction of `karman` and so on.

**17.17** Of all the `karmans`, the dissimilar and the similar, \
of the same `dhātu`, at rebirth[^149], however, it arises as one[^150].

**17.18** But it arises for each and every `karman` in the present life[^151], \
for every one of the two kinds; and it endures even when ripened[^152].

**17.19** It ceases[^153] either through the passing over of the fruit or through death; \
there one should mark the division into the undefiled and the defiled[^154].

**17.20** `Śūnyatā` (emptiness), and not annihilation[^155]; `saṃsāra` (cycle of rebirth), and not the eternal; \
and the non-perishing of `karman`: the `dharma` taught by the Buddha.

**17.21** Why does `karman` not arise? Since it is without `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature), therefore[^156]; \
and since it is unarisen, therefore it does not perish.

**17.22** If `karman` were by `svabhāva`, it would be eternal, without doubt; \
and `karman` would be not done, for the eternal is not done.

**17.23** There would be the fear of the arrival of what was not done, if `karman` were not done; \
and the fault of dwelling in what is not the optimal life[^157] follows there.

**17.24** All the `vyavahāras` (conventions) indeed are contradicted, no doubt; \
and the distinction between the doer of merit and the doer of evil[^158] is not at all fitting.

**17.25** And that whose ripening has ripened would, for that reason, ripen yet again, \
since `karman` is fixed, if it has `svabhāva`[^159].

**17.26** And this `karman` consists of `kleśa` (defilement)[^160], and those `kleśas` are not in `tattva` (reality); \
if those `kleśas` are not in `tattva`, how would `karman` be in `tattva`?

**17.27** `Karman` and the `kleśas` are declared the causes of bodies; \
if that `karman` and those `kleśas` are empty, what talk is there of bodies?

**17.28** The creature is enveloped by `avidyā` (ignorance), and he has `tṛṣṇā` (craving) as his fetter; \
he is the experiencer, and he is not other than the agent, nor is he the very same as it.

**17.29** Since neither arisen from causes nor risen from non-causes \
is this `karman`, therefore the agent too does not exist, indeed.

**17.30** If there is no `karman` and no agent, whence would there be the fruit born of `karman`? \
And when there is no fruit, whence indeed will there be an experiencer?

**17.31** As the Teacher conjures a conjured one by the perfection of supernormal power, \
and the conjured one conjures another, and that one in turn is a conjured one,

**17.32** so the agent has the form of a conjured one, and the `karman` which he does, that is done \
just as another conjured one is conjured by a conjured one; so it is.

**17.33** `Kleśas`, `karmans`, bodies, and agents and fruits \
have the form of a city of gandharvas, resembling a mirage and a dream.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**17.1** ātmasaṃyamakaṃ cetaḥ parānugrāhakaṃ ca yat | \
maitraṃ sa dharmastad bījaṃ phalasya pretya ceha ca ||

**17.2** cetanā cetayitvā ca karmoktaṃ paramarṣiṇā | \
tasyānekavidho bhedaḥ karmaṇaḥ parikīrtitaḥ ||

**17.3** tatra yaccetanetyuktaṃ karma tanmānasaṃ smṛtam | \
cetayitvā ca yat tūktaṃ tat tu kāyikavācikam ||

**17.4** vāgviṣpando’viratayo yāścāvijñaptisaṃjñitāḥ | \
avijñaptaya evānyāḥ smṛtā viratayastathā ||

**17.5** paribhogānvayaṃ puṇyamapuṇyaṃ ca tathāvidham | \
cetanā ceti saptaite dharmāḥ karmāñjanāḥ smṛtāḥ ||

**17.6** tiṣṭhatyā pākakālāccet karma tannityatāmiyāt | \
niruddhaṃ cenniruddhaṃ sat kiṃ phalaṃ janayiṣyati ||

**17.7** yo’ṅkuraprabhṛtirbījāt saṃtāno’bhipravartate | \
tataḥ phalamṛte bījāt sa ca nābhipravartate ||

**17.8** bījācca yasmāt saṃtānaḥ saṃtānācca phalodbhavaḥ | \
bījapūrvaṃ phalaṃ tasmānnocchinnaṃ nāpi śāśvatam ||

**17.9** yastasmāccittasaṃtānaścetaso’bhipravartate | \
tataḥ phalamṛte cittāt sa ca nābhipravartate ||

**17.10** cittācca yasmāt saṃtānaḥ saṃtānācca phalodbhavaḥ | \
karmapūrvaṃ phalaṃ tasmānnocchinnaṃ nāpi śāśvatam ||

**17.11** dharmasya sādhanopāyāḥ śuklāḥ karmapathā daśa | \
phalaṃ kāmaguṇāḥ pañca dharmasya pretya ceha ca ||

**17.12** bahavaśca mahāntaśca doṣāḥ syuryadi kalpanā | \
syādeṣā tena naivaiṣā kalpanātropapadyate ||

**17.13** imāṃ punaḥ pravakṣyāmi kalpanāṃ yātra yojyate | \
buddhaiḥ pratyekabuddhaiśca śrāvakaiścānuvarṇitām ||

**17.14** pattraṃ yathāvipraṇāśastatharṇamiva karma ca | \
caturvidho dhātutaḥ sa prakṛtyāvyākṛtaśca saḥ ||

**17.15** prahāṇato na praheyo bhāvanāheya eva vā | \
tasmādavipraṇāśena jāyate karmaṇāṃ phalam ||

**17.16** prahāṇataḥ praheyaḥ syāt karmaṇaḥ saṃkrameṇa vā | \
yadi doṣāḥ prasajyeraṃstatra karmavadhādayaḥ ||

**17.17** sarveṣāṃ visabhāgānāṃ sabhāgānāṃ ca karmaṇām | \
pratisaṃghau sadhātūnāmeka utpadyate tu saḥ ||

**17.18** karmaṇaḥ karmaṇo dṛṣṭe dharma utpadyate tu saḥ | \
dviprakārasya sarvasya vipakve’pi ca tiṣṭhati ||

**17.19** phalavyatikramād vā sa maraṇād vā nirudhyate | \
anāsravaṃ sāsravaṃ ca vibhāgaṃ tatra lakṣayet ||

**17.20** śūnyatā ca na cocchedaḥ saṃsāraśca na śāśvatam | \
karmaṇo’vipraṇāśaśca dharmo buddhena deśitaḥ ||

**17.21** karma notpadyate kasmān niḥsvabhāvaṃ yatastataḥ | \
yasmācca tadanutpannaṃ na tasmād vipraṇaśyati ||

**17.22** karma svabhāvataścet syācchāśvataṃ syādasaṃśayam | \
akṛtaṃ ca bhavet karma kriyate na hi śāśvatam ||

**17.23** akṛtābhyāgamabhayaṃ syāt karmākṛtakaṃ yadi | \
abrahmacaryavāsaśca doṣastatra prasajyate ||

**17.24** vyavahārā virudhyante sarva eva na saṃśayaḥ | \
puṇyapāpakṛtornaiva pravibhāgaśca yujyate ||

**17.25** tad vipakvavipākaṃ ca punareva vipakṣyati | \
karma vyavasthitaṃ yasmāt tasmāt svābhāvikaṃ yadi ||

**17.26** karma kleśātmakaṃ cedaṃ te ca kleśā na tattvataḥ | \
na cet te tattvataḥ kleśāḥ karma syāt tattvataḥ katham ||

**17.27** karma kleśāśca dehānāṃ pratyayāḥ samudāhṛtāḥ | \
karma kleśāśca te śūnyā yadi deheṣu kā kathā ||

**17.28** avidyānivṛto jantustṛṣṇāsaṃyojanaśca saḥ | \
sa bhoktā sa ca na karturanyo na ca sa eva saḥ ||

**17.29** na pratyayasamutpannaṃ nāpratyayasamutthitam | \
asti yasmādidaṃ karma tasmāt kartāpi nāstyuta ||

**17.30** karma cennāsti kartā ca kutaḥ syāt karmajaṃ phalam | \
asatyatha phale bhoktā kuta eva bhaviṣyati ||

**17.31** yathā nirmitakaṃ śāstā nirmimītarddhisaṃpadā | \
nirmito nirmimītānyaṃ sa ca nirmitakaḥ punaḥ ||

**17.32** tathā nirmitakākāraḥ kartā yat karma tat kṛtam | \
tadyathā nirmitenānyo nirmito nirmitastathā ||

**17.33** kleśāḥ karmāṇi dehāśca kartāraśca phalāni ca | \
gandharvanagarākārā marīcisvapnasaṃnibhāḥ ||

The continuum of mind which proceeds from that mind,  
from that is the fruit; and without the mind it does not proceed.

17.10 cittācca yasmāt saṃtānaḥ saṃtānācca phalodbhavaḥ karmapūrvaṃ phalaṃ tasmānnocchinnaṃ nāpi śāśvatam  
🚻⨀⑤(citta) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(yasmāt) 🚹⨀①(saṃtāna) 🚹⨀⑤(saṃtāna) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(phala+udbhava) 🚻⨀①🆎(karman+pūrva) 🚻⨀①(phala) ⏏️(tasmāt) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(ucchinna) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨀①(śāśvata)  
from the mind | and | since | the continuum | from the continuum | and | the coming forth of the fruit | preceded by action | the fruit | therefore | not | annihilated | not | even | eternal  
And since from the mind is the continuum, and from the continuum the coming forth of the fruit,  
the fruit, preceded by `karman`, is therefore neither annihilated nor eternal.

17.11 dharmasya sādhanopāyāḥ śuklāḥ karmapathā daśa phalaṃ kāmaguṇāḥ pañca dharmasya pretya ceha ca  
🚹⨀⑥(dharma) 🚹⨂①🆎(sādhana+upāya) 🚹⨂①(śukla) 🚹⨂①🆎(karman+patha) 🚹⨂①(daśan) 🚻⨀①(phala) 🚹⨂①🆎(kāma+guṇa) 🚹⨂①(pañcan) 🚹⨀⑥(dharma) 🔽(ya)(praiti) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(iha) ⏏️(ca)  
of righteousness | the means of accomplishing | white | paths of action | ten | the fruit | the qualities of desire | five | of righteousness | after death | and | here | and  
The means of accomplishing `dharma` are the ten white paths of `karman`;  
the fruit of `dharma`, after death and here, is the five qualities of desire.

17.12 bahavaśca mahāntaśca doṣāḥ syuryadi kalpanā syādeṣā tena naivaiṣā kalpanātropapadyate  
🚹⨂①(bahu) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂①(mahat) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂①(doṣa) 🟢⨂🤟⏯(asti) ⏏️(yadi) 🚺⨀①(kalpanā) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(etad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(tad) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(etad) 🚺⨀①(kalpanā) ⏏️(atra) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
many | and | great | and | the faults | would be | if | the conception | were | this | therefore | not | at all | this | conception | here | is possible  
Many and great would be the faults, if the conception  
were this; therefore this conception is not at all possible here.

17.13 imāṃ punaḥ pravakṣyāmi kalpanāṃ yātra yojyate buddhaiḥ pratyekabuddhaiśca śrāvakaiścānuvarṇitām  
*️⃣🤟🚺⨀②(idam) ⏏️(punar) 🟢⨀👆⏭(prabravīti) 🚺⨀②(kalpanā) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(yad) ⏏️(atra) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) 🚹⨂③(buddha) 🚹⨂③🆎(pratyeka+buddha) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂③(śrāvaka) ⏏️(ca) 🚺⨀②🔽(ta)(anuvarṇita)  
this | but | I shall declare | conception | which | here | is applied | by the Buddhas | by the solitary Buddhas | and | by the disciples | and | described  
But I shall declare this conception, which is applied here,  
described by the Buddhas, by the solitary Buddhas and by the disciples.

17.14 pattraṃ yathāvipraṇāśastatharṇamiva karma ca caturvidho dhātutaḥ sa prakṛtyāvyākṛtaśca saḥ  
🚻⨀①(pattra) ⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+vipraṇāśa) ⏏️(tathā) 🚻⨀①(ṛṇa) ⏏️(iva) 🚻⨀①(karman) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(catur+vidha) ⏏️(dhātutas) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚺⨀③(prakṛti) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+vyākṛta) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad)  
a document | as | the non-perishing | so | a debt | like | action | and | fourfold | by component | it | by nature | indeterminate | and | it  
The non-perishing is like a document, and so `karman` is like a debt;  
it is fourfold by `dhātu` (component), and by nature it is indeterminate.

17.15 prahāṇato na praheyo bhāvanāheya eva vā tasmādavipraṇāśena jāyate karmaṇāṃ phalam  
⏏️(prahāṇatas) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🔽(ya)(praheya) 🚹⨀①🔽(ya)🆎(bhāvanā+heya) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚹⨀③🆎(na+vipraṇāśa) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) 🚻⨂⑥(karman) 🚻⨀①(phala)  
by abandonment | not | to be abandoned | to be abandoned by cultivation | alone | or else | therefore | by the non-perishing | is born | of actions | the fruit  
It is not to be abandoned by abandonment, or else it is to be abandoned by cultivation alone;  
therefore the fruit of `karmans` is born through the non-perishing.

17.16 prahāṇataḥ praheyaḥ syāt karmaṇaḥ saṃkrameṇa vā yadi doṣāḥ prasajyeraṃstatra karmavadhādayaḥ  
⏏️(prahāṇatas) 🚹⨀①🔽(ya)(praheya) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀⑥(karman) 🚹⨀③(saṃkrama) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨂①(doṣa) 🔵⨂🤟⏯(prasajyate) ⏏️(tatra) 🚹⨂①🆎(karman+vadha+ādi)  
by abandonment | to be abandoned | it would be | of action | by transference | or | if | faults | would follow | there | the destruction of action and so on  
If it were to be abandoned by abandonment, or by the transference of `karman`,  
faults would follow there, the destruction of `karman` and so on.

17.17 sarveṣāṃ visabhāgānāṃ sabhāgānāṃ ca karmaṇām pratisaṃghau sadhātūnāmeka utpadyate tu saḥ  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑥(sarva) 🚹⨂⑥(visabhāga) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(sa+bhāga) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨂⑥(karman) 🚹⨀⑦(pratisaṃdhi) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(sa+dhātu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(eka) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(utpadyate) ⏏️(tu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad)

Of all the `karmans`, the dissimilar and the similar,  
of the same `dhātu`, at rebirth, however, it arises as one.

17.18 karmaṇaḥ karmaṇo dṛṣṭe dharma utpadyate tu saḥ dviprakārasya sarvasya vipakve’pi ca tiṣṭhati  
🚻⨀⑥(karman) 🚻⨀⑥(karman) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(dṛṣṭa) 🚹⨀⑦(dharma) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(utpadyate) ⏏️(tu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(dvi+prakāra) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(sarva) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(vipakva) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(tiṣṭhati)

But it arises for each and every `karman` in the present life,  
for every one of the two kinds; and it endures even when ripened.

17.19 phalavyatikramād vā sa maraṇād vā nirudhyate anāsravaṃ sāsravaṃ ca vibhāgaṃ tatra lakṣayet  
🚹⨀⑤🆎(phala+vyatikrama) ⏏️(vā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀⑤(maraṇa) ⏏️(vā) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate) 🚹⨀②🆎(na+āsrava) 🚹⨀②🆎(sa+āsrava) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀②(vibhāga) ⏏️(tatra) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(lakṣayati)  
through the passing over of the fruit | or | it | through death | or | it ceases | the undefiled | the defiled | and | the division | there | one should mark  
It ceases either through the passing over of the fruit or through death;  
there one should mark the division into the undefiled and the defiled.

17.20 śūnyatā ca na cocchedaḥ saṃsāraśca na śāśvatam karmaṇo’vipraṇāśaśca dharmo buddhena deśitaḥ  
🚺⨀①(śūnyatā) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(uccheda) 🚹⨀①(saṃsāra) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①(śāśvata) 🚻⨀⑥(karman) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+vipraṇāśa) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(dharma) 🚹⨀③(buddha) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(deśita)

`Śūnyatā` (emptiness), and not annihilation; `saṃsāra` (cycle of rebirth), and not the eternal;  
and the non-perishing of `karman`: the `dharma` taught by the Buddha.

17.21 karma notpadyate kasmān niḥsvabhāvaṃ yatastataḥ yasmācca tadanutpannaṃ na tasmād vipraṇaśyati  
🚻⨀①(karman) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(utpadyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑤(kim) 🚻⨀①(niḥsvabhāva) ⏏️(yatas) ⏏️(tatas) ⏏️(yasmāt) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(vipraṇaśyati)

Why does `karman` not arise? Since it is without `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature), therefore;  
and since it is unarisen, therefore it does not perish.

17.22 karma svabhāvataścet syācchāśvataṃ syādasaṃśayam akṛtaṃ ca bhavet karma kriyate na hi śāśvatam  
🚻⨀①(karman) ⏏️(svabhāvatas) ⏏️(cet) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀①(śāśvata) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀②🆎(na+saṃśaya) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+kṛta) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚻⨀①(karman) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(kriyate) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi) 🚻⨀①(śāśvata)  
action | by intrinsic nature | if | it were | eternal | it would be | without doubt | not done | and | it would be | action | is done | not | for | the eternal  
If `karman` were by `svabhāva`, it would be eternal, without doubt;  
and `karman` would be not done, for the eternal is not done.

17.23 akṛtābhyāgamabhayaṃ syāt karmākṛtakaṃ yadi abrahmacaryavāsaśca doṣastatra prasajyate  
🚻⨀①🆎(na+kṛta+abhyāgama+bhaya) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀①(karman) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+kṛtaka) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+brahmacarya+vāsa) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(doṣa) ⏏️(tatra) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate)  
the fear of the arrival of what was not done | there would be | action | not done | if | dwelling in what is not the optimal life | and | the fault | there | follows  
There would be the fear of the arrival of what was not done, if `karman` were not done;  
and the fault of dwelling in what is not the optimal life follows there.

17.24 vyavahārā virudhyante sarva eva na saṃśayaḥ puṇyapāpakṛtornaiva pravibhāgaśca yujyate  
🚹⨂①(vyavahāra) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(virudhyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(sarva) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(saṃśaya) 🚹⨁⑥🆎(puṇya+pāpa+kṛt) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀①(pravibhāga) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate)

All the `vyavahāras` (conventions) indeed are contradicted, no doubt;  
and the distinction between the doer of merit and the doer of evil is not at all fitting.

17.25 tad vipakvavipākaṃ ca punareva vipakṣyati karma vyavasthitaṃ yasmāt tasmāt svābhāvikaṃ yadi  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①🆎(vipakva+vipāka) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(punar) ⏏️(eva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(vipacati) 🚻⨀①(karman) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(vyavasthita) ⏏️(yasmāt) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚻⨀①(svābhāvika) ⏏️(yadi)  
that | whose ripening has ripened | and | again | indeed | will ripen | action | fixed | since | for that reason | having intrinsic nature | if  
And that whose ripening has ripened would, for that reason, ripen yet again,  
since `karman` is fixed, if it has `svabhāva`.

17.26 karma kleśātmakaṃ cedaṃ te ca kleśā na tattvataḥ na cet te tattvataḥ kleśāḥ karma syāt tattvataḥ katham

And this `karman` consists of `kleśa` (defilement), and those `kleśas` are not in `tattva` (reality);  
if those `kleśas` are not in `tattva`, how would `karman` be in `tattva`?

17.27 karma kleśāśca dehānāṃ pratyayāḥ samudāhṛtāḥ karma kleśāśca te śūnyā yadi deheṣu kā kathā

action | defilements | and | of bodies | causes | are declared | action | defilements | and | those | empty | if | about bodies | what | talk  
`Karman` and the `kleśas` are declared the causes of bodies;  
if that `karman` and those `kleśas` are empty, what talk is there of bodies?

17.28 avidyānivṛto jantustṛṣṇāsaṃyojanaśca saḥ sa bhoktā sa ca na karturanyo na ca sa eva saḥ  
🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(avidyā+nivṛta) 🚹⨀①(jantu) 🚹⨀①🆎(tṛṣṇā+saṃyojana) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①(bhoktṛ) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑤(kartṛ) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad)

The creature is enveloped by `avidyā` (ignorance), and he has `tṛṣṇā` (craving) as his fetter;  
he is the experiencer, and he is not other than the agent, nor is he the very same as it.

17.29 na pratyayasamutpannaṃ nāpratyayasamutthitam asti yasmādidaṃ karma tasmāt kartāpi nāstyuta  
⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(pratyaya+samutpanna) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+pratyaya+samutthita) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(yasmāt) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) 🚻⨀①(karman) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚹⨀①(kartṛ) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(uta)  
not | arisen from causes | not | risen from non-causes | is | since | this | action | therefore | the agent | also | does not exist | indeed  
Since neither arisen from causes nor risen from non-causes  
is this `karman`, therefore the agent too does not exist, indeed.

17.30 karma cennāsti kartā ca kutaḥ syāt karmajaṃ phalam asatyatha phale bhoktā kuta eva bhaviṣyati  
🚻⨀①(karman) ⏏️(cet) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚹⨀①(kartṛ) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(kutas) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀①(karmaja) 🚻⨀①(phala) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) ⏏️(atha) 🚻⨀⑦(phala) 🚹⨀①(bhoktṛ) ⏏️(kutas) ⏏️(eva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
action | if | is not | the agent | and | whence | would there be | born of action | the fruit | when there is no | and | fruit | an experiencer | whence | indeed | will there be  
If there is no `karman` and no agent, whence would there be the fruit born of `karman`?  
And when there is no fruit, whence indeed will there be an experiencer?

17.31 yathā nirmitakaṃ śāstā nirmimītarddhisaṃpadā nirmito nirmimītānyaṃ sa ca nirmitakaḥ punaḥ  
⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀②🔽(ka)(nirmita) 🚹⨀①(śāstṛ) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirmimīte) 🚺⨀③🆎(ṛddhi+sampad) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(nirmita) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirmimīte) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(anya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🔽(ka)(nirmita) ⏏️(punar)  
as | a conjured one | the Teacher | conjures | by the perfection of supernormal power | the conjured one | conjures | another | that one | and | a conjured one | in turn  
As the Teacher conjures a conjured one by the perfection of supernormal power,  
and the conjured one conjures another, and that one in turn is a conjured one,

17.32 tathā nirmitakākāraḥ kartā yat karma tat kṛtam tadyathā nirmitenānyo nirmito nirmitastathā  
⏏️(tathā) 🚹⨀①🆎(nirmitaka+ākāra) 🚹⨀①(kartṛ) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) 🚻⨀①(karman) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(kṛta) ⏏️(tadyathā) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)(nirmita) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(nirmita) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(nirmita) ⏏️(tathā)  
so | having the form of a conjured one | the agent | which | action | that | is done | just as | by a conjured one | another | a conjured one | is conjured | so it is  
so the agent has the form of a conjured one, and the `karman` which he does, that is done  
just as another conjured one is conjured by a conjured one; so it is.

17.33 kleśāḥ karmāṇi dehāśca kartāraśca phalāni ca gandharvanagarākārā marīcisvapnasaṃnibhāḥ  
🚹⨂①(kleśa) 🚻⨂①(karman) 🚹⨂①(deha) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂①(kartṛ) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨂①(phala) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂①🆎(gandharva+nagara+ākāra) 🚹⨂①🆎(marīci+svapna+saṃnibha)  
defilements | actions | bodies | and | agents | and | fruits | and | having the form of a city of gandharvas | resembling a mirage and a dream  
`Kleśas`, `karmans`, bodies, and agents and fruits  
have the form of a city of gandharvas, resembling a mirage and a dream.

### §18 Ātmadharmaparīkṣā (Examination of the self and phenomena)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**18.1** If the self were the `skandhas` (collections), it would partake of arising and passing away; \
if it were other than the `skandhas`, it would be without the characteristic of the `skandhas`[^161].

**18.2** And when the self does not exist, whence indeed will there be what belongs to the self? \
One is without "mine" and without the making of "I", through the quieting of the self and of what pertains to the self.

**18.3** And he who is without "mine" and without the making of "I", he too is not found; \
he who sees one[^162] without "mine" and without the making of "I" does not see.

**18.4** When "mine" and "I" are exhausted, outwardly and inwardly too, \
`upādāna` (fuel)[^163] ceases; from its exhaustion, the exhaustion of birth.

**18.5** Release is from the exhaustion of `karman` (action) and the `kleśas` (defilements); `karman` and the `kleśas` are from discrimination[^164]; \
those are from `prapañca` (proliferation), but `prapañca` ceases in `śūnyatā` (emptiness).

**18.6** "Self" has also been designated; "not-self" has also been taught; \
by the Buddhas it has also been taught that there is neither self nor any not-self[^165].

**18.7** What is to be expressed has turned back[^166]; the range of mind has turned back[^167]; \
for the nature of `dharmas` (phenomena), unarisen and unceased, is like `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment).

**18.8** Everything is real, or not real, and both real and not real, \
neither not real nor real: this is the instruction of the Buddhas.

**18.9** Not dependent on another[^168], at peace, not proliferated by `prapañcas`, \
without discrimination, not different things[^169]: this is the characteristic of `tattva` (reality).

**18.10** Whatever comes to be depending on whatever, that, to begin with, is indeed not that very thing, \
nor is it other than it either; therefore it is neither annihilated nor eternal.

**18.11** Not one thing, not different things[^170], not annihilation, not eternal: \
this is that deathless teaching of the Buddhas, the lords of the world.

**18.12** When the fully awakened have not arisen, and again when the disciples have perished, \
the knowledge of the solitary Buddhas proceeds from non-association[^171].

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**18.1** ātmā skandhā yadi bhavedudayavyayabhāg bhavet | \
skandhebhyo’nyo yadi bhaved bhavedaskandhalakṣaṇaḥ ||

**18.2** ātmanyasati cātmīyaṃ kuta eva bhaviṣyati | \
nirmamo nirahaṃkāraḥ śamādātmātmanīnayoḥ ||

**18.3** nirmamo nirahaṃkāro yaśca so’pi na vidyate | \
nirmamaṃ nirahaṃkāraṃ yaḥ paśyati na paśyati ||

**18.4** mametyahamiti kṣīṇe bahirdhādhyātmameva ca | \
nirudhyata upādānaṃ tatkṣayājjanmanaḥ kṣayaḥ ||

**18.5** karmakleśakṣayānmokṣaḥ karmakleśā vikalpataḥ | \
te prapañcāt prapañcastu śūnyatāyāṃ nirudhyate ||

**18.6** ātmetyapi prajñapitamanātmetyapi deśitam | \
buddhairnātmā na cānātmā kaścidityapi deśitam ||

**18.7** nirvṛttamabhidhātavyaṃ nivṛttaścittagocaraḥ | \
anutpannāniruddhā hi nirvāṇamiva dharmatā ||

**18.8** sarvaṃ tathyaṃ na vā tathyaṃ tathyaṃ cātathyameva ca | \
naivātathyaṃ naiva tathyametad buddhānuśāsanam ||

**18.9** aparapratyayaṃ śāntaṃ prapañcairaprapañcitam | \
nirvikalpamanānārthametat tattvasya lakṣaṇam ||

**18.10** pratītya yad yad bhavati na hi tāvat tadeva tat | \
na cānyadapi tat tasmānnocchinnaṃ nāpi śāśvatam ||

**18.11** anekārthamanānārthamanucchedamaśāśvatam | \
etat tallokanāthānāṃ buddhānāṃ śāsanāmṛtam ||

**18.12** saṃbuddhānāmanutpāde śrāvakāṇāṃ punaḥ kṣaye | \
jñānaṃ pratyekabuddhānāmasaṃsargāt pravartate ||

### §19 Kālaparīkṣā (Examination of time)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**19.1** If the present and the future are indeed relative to the past, \
the present and the future will be in the past time.

**19.2** If, again, the present and the future are not there, \
how would the present and the future be relative to it?

**19.3** Again, without being relative to the past, the establishment of those two is not found; \
therefore the present and the future time[^172] is not found.

**19.4** By just this, one should mark the remaining two, rotated in turn,[^173] \
and the highest, the lowest, the middle and so on, and oneness and so on.

**19.5** A non-enduring time is not grasped; an enduring time is not found \
which might be grasped;[^174] and how is an ungrasped time designated?

**19.6** If time is dependent on an existent, whence is time without an existent? \
And no existent at all exists; whence will time be?

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**19.1** pratyutpanno’nāgataśca yadyatītamapekṣya hi | \
pratyutpanno’nāgataśca kāle’tīte bhaviṣyataḥ ||

**19.2** pratyutpanno’nāgataśca na stastatra punaryadi | \
pratyutpanno’nāgataśca syātāṃ kathamapekṣya tam ||

**19.3** anapekṣya punaḥ siddhirnātītaṃ vidyate tayoḥ | \
pratyutpanna’nāgataśca tasmāt kālo na vidyate ||

**19.4** etenaivāvaśiṣṭau dvau krameṇa parivartakau | \
uttamādhamamadhyādīnekatvādīṃśca lakṣayet ||

**19.5** nāsthito gṛhyate kālaḥ sthitaḥ kālo na vidyate | \
yo gṛhyetāgṛhītaśca kālaḥ prajñapyate katham ||

**19.6** bhāvaṃ pratītya kālaścet kālo bhāvādṛte kutaḥ | \
na ca kaścana bhāvo’sti kutaḥ kālo bhaviṣyati ||

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Interlinear">

19.3 anapekṣya punaḥ siddhirnātītaṃ vidyate tayoḥ pratyutpanna’nāgataśca tasmāt kālo na vidyate  
🔽(ya)🆎(na+apekṣate) ⏏️(punar) 🚺⨀①(siddhi) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀②🔽(ta)(atīta) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨁⑥(tad) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(pratyutpanna) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+āgata) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚹⨀①(kāla) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)

Again, without being relative to the past, the establishment of those two is not found;  
therefore the present and the future time is not found.

19.4 etenaivāvaśiṣṭau dvau krameṇa parivartakau uttamādhamamadhyādīnekatvādīṃśca lakṣayet  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(etad) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨁①🔽(ta)(avaśiṣṭa) 🚹⨁①(dvi) ⏏️(krameṇa) 🚹⨁①(parivartaka) 🚹⨂②🆎(uttama+adhama+madhya+ādi) 🚹⨂②🆎(ekatva+ādi) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(lakṣayati)  
by this | just | the remaining | two | in turn | rotated | the highest, the lowest, the middle and so on | oneness and so on | and | one should mark  
By just this, one should mark the remaining two, rotated in turn,  
and the highest, the lowest, the middle and so on, and oneness and so on.

19.5 nāsthito gṛhyate kālaḥ sthitaḥ kālo na vidyate yo gṛhyetāgṛhītaśca kālaḥ prajñapyate katham  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+sthita) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(gṛhyate) 🚹⨀①(kāla) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(sthita) 🚹⨀①(kāla) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🔴⨀🤟⏯(gṛhyate) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+gṛhīta) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(kāla) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prajñapyate) ⏏️(katham)  
not | non-enduring | is grasped | time | enduring | time | not | is found | which | might be grasped | ungrasped | and | time | is designated | how  
A non-enduring time is not grasped; an enduring time is not found  
which might be grasped; and how is an ungrasped time designated?

19.6 bhāvaṃ pratītya kālaścet kālo bhāvādṛte kutaḥ na ca kaścana bhāvo’sti kutaḥ kālo bhaviṣyati  
🚹⨀②(bhāva) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) 🚹⨀①(kāla) ⏏️(cet) 🚹⨀①(kāla) 🚹⨀⑤(bhāva) ⏏️(ṛte) ⏏️(kutas) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścana) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(kutas) 🚹⨀①(kāla) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
an existent | dependent on | time | if | time | an existent | without | whence | not | and | any | existent | exists | whence | time | will be  
If time is dependent on an existent, whence is time without an existent?  
And no existent at all exists; whence will time be?

</TabItem>
</Tabs>

### §20 Hetuparīkṣā (Examination of primary cause and fruit)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**20.1** If it is born from the assemblage of the primary cause and of the causes, \
and the fruit exists in the assemblage, how is it born from the assemblage?

**20.2** If it is born from the assemblage of the primary cause and of the causes, \
and the fruit does not exist in the assemblage, how is it born from the assemblage?

**20.3** If the fruit exists in the assemblage of the primary cause and of the causes, \
surely it would be grasped in the assemblage; and in the assemblage it is not grasped.

**20.4** If the fruit does not exist in the assemblage of the primary cause and of the causes, \
the primary causes and the causes would be equal to what are not primary causes and not causes.

**20.5** And if the primary cause, having given[^175] the primary cause to the fruit,[^176] ceases, \
what is given and what has ceased would be two selves of the primary cause.

**20.6** And if the primary cause ceases without having given the primary cause to the fruit, \
that fruit, born when the primary cause has ceased, would be without a primary cause.

**20.7** If, again, the fruit should appear just together with the assemblage, \
it follows that the generator and what is generated are simultaneous.

**20.8** And if the fruit should appear even before the assemblage, \
the fruit, freed of primary cause and causes, would be without a primary cause.

**20.9** If, when the primary cause has ceased, the fruit were a transference of the primary cause, \
then a second birth of the primary cause born before follows.

**20.10** How could a ceased, vanished [primary cause] generate the arisen fruit? \
How could the primary cause, even while standing, enveloped by the fruit,[^177] generate [it]?

**20.11** But if that one is not enveloped by the fruit, which fruit would it generate?[^178] \
For the primary cause generates the fruit neither without having seen nor having seen [it].

**20.12** For of a past fruit, with a past primary cause, \
with an unborn one, with a born one, no coming together is ever found.

**20.13** For of a born fruit, with an unborn primary cause, \
with a past one, with a born one, no coming together is ever found.

**20.14** For of an unborn fruit, with a born primary cause, \
with an unborn one, with a perished one, no coming together is ever found.

**20.15** When there is no coming together, how does the primary cause generate the fruit? \
Or when there is a coming together, how does the primary cause generate the fruit?

**20.16** If the primary cause is empty of the fruit, how does it generate the fruit? \
If the primary cause is not empty of the fruit, how does it generate the fruit?

**20.17** A non-empty fruit will not arise; a non-empty one will not cease; \
that non-empty one will be unceased, unarisen.[^179]

**20.18** How will an empty one arise? How will an empty one cease? \
It follows that that empty one too is unceased, unarisen.

**20.19** For oneness of the primary cause and the fruit is never possible; \
for otherness of the primary cause and the fruit is never possible.

**20.20** Given oneness of fruit and primary cause, there would be identity of generator and generated; \
given separateness of fruit and primary cause, the primary cause would be equal to what is not a primary cause.[^180]

**20.21** What fruit that truly exists by `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) will a primary cause generate?[^181] \
What fruit that does not truly exist by `svabhāva` will a primary cause generate?

**20.22** And being a primary cause is not possible for one that is not generating; \
and when being a primary cause is not possible, whose will the fruit be?

**20.23** And this assemblage of the causes and primary causes does not [generate] itself by itself;[^182] \
the assemblage that does not generate [itself]: how would that generate the fruit?

**20.24** Therefore the fruit is neither made by an assemblage nor made by a non-assemblage; \
how at all is there an assemblage of causes without a fruit?

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**20.1** hetośca pratyayānāṃ ca sāmagryā jāyate yadi | \
phalamasti ca sāmagryāṃ sāmagryā jāyate katham ||

**20.2** hetośca pratyayānāṃ ca sāmagryā jāyate yadi | \
phalaṃ nāsti ca sāmagryāṃ sāmagryā jāyate katham ||

**20.3** hetośca pratyayānāṃ ca sāmagryāmasti cet phalam | \
gṛhyeta nanu sāmagryāṃ sāmagryāṃ ca na gṛhyate ||

**20.4** hetośca pratyayānāṃ ca sāmagryāṃ nāsti cet phalam | \
hetavaḥ pratyayāśca syurahetupratyayaiḥ samāḥ ||

**20.5** hetuṃ phalasya datvā ca yadi heturnirudhyate | \
yad dattaṃ yanniruddhaṃ ca hetorātmadvayaṃ bhavet ||

**20.6** hetuṃ phalasyādattvā ca yadi heturnirudhyate | \
hetau niruddhe jātaṃ tat phalamāhetukaṃ bhavet ||

**20.7** phalaṃ sahaiva sāmagryā yadi prādurbhavet punaḥ | \
ekakālau prasajyete janako yacca janyate ||

**20.8** pūrvameva ca sāmagryāḥ phalaṃ prādurbhaved yadi | \
hetupratyayanirmuktaṃ phalamāhetukaṃ bhavet ||

**20.9** niruddhe cet phalaṃ hetau hetoḥ saṃkramaṇaṃ bhavet | \
pūrvajātasya hetośca punarjanma prasajyate ||

**20.10** janayet phalamutpannaṃ niruddho’staṃgataḥ katham | \
hetustiṣṭhannapi kathaṃ phalena janayed vṛtaḥ ||

**20.11** athāvṛtaḥ phalenāsau katamajjanayet phalam | \
na hyadṛṣṭvā na dṛṣṭvāpi heturjanayate phalam ||

**20.12** nātītasya hyatītena phalasya saha hetunā | \
nājātena na jātena saṃgatirjātu vidyate ||

**20.13** na jātasya hyajātena phalasya saha hetunā | \
nātītena na jātena saṃgatirjātu vidyate ||

**20.14** nājātasya hi jātena phalasya saha hetunā | \
nājātena na naṣṭena saṃgatirjātu vidyate ||

**20.15** asatyāṃ saṃgatau hetuḥ kathaṃ janayate phalam | \
satyāṃ vā saṃgatau hetuḥ kathaṃ janayate phalam ||

**20.16** hetuḥ phalena śūnyaścet kathaṃ janayate phalam | \
hetuḥ phalenāśūnyaścet kathaṃ janayate phalam ||

**20.17** phalaṃ notpatsyate’śūnyamaśūnyaṃ na nirotsyate | \
aniruddhamanutpannamaśūnyaṃ tad bhaviṣyati ||

**20.18** kathamutpatsyate śūnyaṃ kathaṃ śūnyaṃ nirotsyate | \
śūnyamapyaniruddhaṃ tadanutpannaṃ prasajyate ||

**20.19** hetoḥ phalasya caikatvaṃ na hi jātūpapadyate | \
hetoḥ phalasya cānyatvaṃ na hi jātūpapadyate ||

**20.20** ekatve phalahetvoḥ syādaikyaṃ janakajanyayoḥ | \
pṛthaktve phalahetvoḥ syāt tulyo heturahetunāḥ ||

**20.21** phalaṃ svabhāvasadbhūtaṃ kiṃ heturjanayiṣyati | \
phalaṃ svabhāvāsadbhūtaṃ kiṃ heturjanayiṣyati ||

**20.22** na cājanayamānasya hetutvamupapadyate | \
hetutvānupapattau ca phalaṃ kasya bhaviṣyati ||

**20.23** na ca pratyayahetūnāmiyamātmānamātmanā | \
yā sāmagrī janayate sā kathaṃ janayet phalam ||

**20.24** tasmānna sāmagrīkṛtaṃ nāsāmagrīkṛtaṃ phalam | \
asti pratyayasāmagrī kuta eva phalaṃ vinā ||

could generate | the fruit | arisen | ceased | vanished | how | the primary cause | while standing | even | how | by the fruit | could generate | enveloped

How could the primary cause, even while standing, enveloped by the fruit, generate [it]?

For the primary cause generates the fruit neither without having seen nor having seen [it].

not | of a past | for | with a past | of the fruit | together | with the primary cause | not | with an unborn one | not | with a born one | coming together | ever | is found  
For of a past fruit, with a past primary cause,  
with an unborn one, with a born one, no coming together is ever found.

20.13 na jātasya hyajātena phalasya saha hetunā nātītena na jātena saṃgatirjātu vidyate  
⏏️(na) 🚻⨀⑥🔽(ta)(jāta) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)🆎(na+jāta) 🚻⨀⑥(phala) ⏏️(saha) 🚹⨀③(hetu) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)(atīta) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)(jāta) 🚺⨀①(saṃgati) ⏏️(jātu) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
not | of a born | for | with an unborn | of the fruit | together | with the primary cause | not | with a past one | not | with a born one | coming together | ever | is found  
For of a born fruit, with an unborn primary cause,  
with a past one, with a born one, no coming together is ever found.

20.14 nājātasya hi jātena phalasya saha hetunā nājātena na naṣṭena saṃgatirjātu vidyate  
⏏️(na) 🚻⨀⑥🔽(ta)🆎(na+jāta) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)(jāta) 🚻⨀⑥(phala) ⏏️(saha) 🚹⨀③(hetu) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)🆎(na+jāta) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀③🔽(ta)(naṣṭa) 🚺⨀①(saṃgati) ⏏️(jātu) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
not | of an unborn | for | with a born | of the fruit | together | with the primary cause | not | with an unborn one | not | with a perished one | coming together | ever | is found  
For of an unborn fruit, with a born primary cause,  
with an unborn one, with a perished one, no coming together is ever found.

20.15 asatyāṃ saṃgatau hetuḥ kathaṃ janayate phalam satyāṃ vā saṃgatau hetuḥ kathaṃ janayate phalam  
🚺⨀⑦🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) 🚺⨀⑦(saṃgati) 🚹⨀①(hetu) ⏏️(katham) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) 🚻⨀②(phala) 🚺⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀⑦(saṃgati) 🚹⨀①(hetu) ⏏️(katham) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) 🚻⨀②(phala)  
when there is no | coming together | the primary cause | how | generates | the fruit | when there is | or | coming together | the primary cause | how | generates | the fruit  
When there is no coming together, how does the primary cause generate the fruit?  
Or when there is a coming together, how does the primary cause generate the fruit?

20.16 hetuḥ phalena śūnyaścet kathaṃ janayate phalam hetuḥ phalenāśūnyaścet kathaṃ janayate phalam  
🚹⨀①(hetu) 🚻⨀③(phala) 🚹⨀①(śūnya) ⏏️(cet) ⏏️(katham) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) 🚻⨀②(phala) 🚹⨀①(hetu) 🚻⨀③(phala) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) ⏏️(cet) ⏏️(katham) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) 🚻⨀②(phala)  
the primary cause | of the fruit | empty | if | how | generates | the fruit | the primary cause | of the fruit | not empty | if | how | generates | the fruit  
If the primary cause is empty of the fruit, how does it generate the fruit?  
If the primary cause is not empty of the fruit, how does it generate the fruit?

20.17 phalaṃ notpatsyate’śūnyamaśūnyaṃ na nirotsyate aniruddhamanutpannamaśūnyaṃ tad bhaviṣyati  
🚻⨀①(phala) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(utpadyate) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(nirudhyate) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+niruddha) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
fruit | not | will arise | non-empty | non-empty | not | will cease | unceased | unarisen | non-empty | that | will be  
A non-empty fruit will not arise; a non-empty one will not cease;  
that non-empty one will be unceased, unarisen.

20.18 kathamutpatsyate śūnyaṃ kathaṃ śūnyaṃ nirotsyate śūnyamapyaniruddhaṃ tadanutpannaṃ prasajyate  
⏏️(katham) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(utpadyate) 🚻⨀①(śūnya) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①(śūnya) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(nirudhyate) 🚻⨀①(śūnya) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+niruddha) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate)  
how | will arise | an empty one | how | an empty one | will cease | an empty one | too | unceased | that | unarisen | follows  
How will an empty one arise? How will an empty one cease?  
It follows that that empty one too is unceased, unarisen.

20.19 hetoḥ phalasya caikatvaṃ na hi jātūpapadyate hetoḥ phalasya cānyatvaṃ na hi jātūpapadyate  
🚹⨀⑥(hetu) 🚻⨀⑥(phala) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①🔽(tva)(ekatva) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi) ⏏️(jātu) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) 🚹⨀⑥(hetu) 🚻⨀⑥(phala) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①🔽(tva)(anyatva) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi) ⏏️(jātu) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
of the primary cause | of the fruit | and | oneness | not | for | ever | is possible | of the primary cause | of the fruit | and | otherness | not | for | ever | is possible  
For oneness of the primary cause and the fruit is never possible;  
for otherness of the primary cause and the fruit is never possible.

20.20 ekatve phalahetvoḥ syādaikyaṃ janakajanyayoḥ pṛthaktve phalahetvoḥ syāt tulyo heturahetunāḥ  
🚻⨀⑦🔽(tva)(ekatva) 🚹⨁⑥🆎(phala+hetu) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚻⨀①(aikya) 🚹⨁⑥🔽(ya)🆎(janaka+janya) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(tva)(pṛthaktva) 🚹⨁⑥🆎(phala+hetu) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀①(tulya) 🚹⨀①(hetu) 🚹⨀③🆎(na+hetu)  
given oneness | of the fruit and the primary cause | there would be | identity | of generator and generated | given separateness | of the fruit and the primary cause | there would be | equal | the primary cause | to what is not a primary cause  
Given oneness of fruit and primary cause, there would be identity of generator and generated;  
given separateness of fruit and primary cause, the primary cause would be equal to what is not a primary cause.

20.21 phalaṃ svabhāvasadbhūtaṃ kiṃ heturjanayiṣyati phalaṃ svabhāvāsadbhūtaṃ kiṃ heturjanayiṣyati  
🚻⨀②(phala) 🚻⨀②🔽(ta)🆎(sva+bhāva+sad+bhūta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(kim) 🚹⨀①(hetu) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(janayati) 🚻⨀②(phala) 🚻⨀②🔽(ta)🆎(sva+bhāva+na+sad+bhūta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(kim) 🚹⨀①(hetu) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(janayati)  
fruit | truly existing by intrinsic nature | what | a primary cause | will generate | fruit | not truly existing by intrinsic nature | what | a primary cause | will generate  
What fruit that truly exists by `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature) will a primary cause generate?  
What fruit that does not truly exist by `svabhāva` will a primary cause generate?

20.22 na cājanayamānasya hetutvamupapadyate hetutvānupapattau ca phalaṃ kasya bhaviṣyati  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(māna)🆎(na+janayamāna) 🚻⨀①🔽(tva)(hetutva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) 🚺⨀⑦🆎(hetutva+na+upapatti) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①(phala) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
not | and | of one not generating | being a primary cause | is possible | when being a primary cause is not possible | and | the fruit | whose | will be  
And being a primary cause is not possible for one that is not generating;  
and when being a primary cause is not possible, whose will the fruit be?

20.23 na ca pratyayahetūnāmiyamātmānamātmanā yā sāmagrī janayate sā kathaṃ janayet phalam  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂⑥🆎(pratyaya+hetu) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(idam) 🚹⨀②(ātman) 🚹⨀③(ātman) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(yad) 🚺⨀①(sāmagrī) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(janayati) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(tad) ⏏️(katham) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(janayati) 🚻⨀②(phala)  
not | and | of the causes and primary causes | this | itself | by itself | which | assemblage | generates | that | how | would generate | the fruit  
And this assemblage of the causes and primary causes does not [generate] itself by itself;  
the assemblage that does not generate [itself]: how would that generate the fruit?

20.24 tasmānna sāmagrīkṛtaṃ nāsāmagrīkṛtaṃ phalam asti pratyayasāmagrī kuta eva phalaṃ vinā  
⏏️(tasmāt) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(sāmagrī+kṛta) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+sāmagrī+kṛta) 🚻⨀①(phala) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) 🚺⨀①🆎(pratyaya+sāmagrī) ⏏️(kutas) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀②(phala) ⏏️(vinā)  
therefore | not | made by an assemblage | not | made by a non-assemblage | the fruit | is | an assemblage of causes | how | at all | a fruit | without  
Therefore the fruit is neither made by an assemblage nor made by a non-assemblage;  
how at all is there an assemblage of causes without a fruit?
