### §21 Saṃbhavavibhavaparīkṣā (Examination of coming to be and passing away)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**21.1** Whether without or together with coming to be, there is indeed no passing away; \
whether without or together with passing away, there is indeed no coming to be.

**21.2** How indeed will passing away be without coming to be? \
Death without birth indeed;[^183] passing away is not without origination.

**21.3** How will passing away be together with coming to be itself? \
For birth and death are not found at the same time.[^184]

**21.4** How indeed will coming to be be without passing away? \
For impermanence is at no time not found in existents.

**21.5** How will coming to be be together with passing away itself? \
For birth and death are not found at the same time.

**21.6** Those two whose establishment, whether together with each other or without each other, \
is not found: how then indeed is the establishment of those two found?

**21.7** There is no coming to be of what decays;[^185] there is no coming to be of what does not decay; \
there is no passing away of what decays, and no passing away of what does not decay.

**21.8** Coming to be and passing away are not found without an existent; \
an existent is not found without coming to be and passing away.

**21.9** Coming to be and passing away are not possible of what is empty; \
coming to be and passing away are not possible of what is not empty.

**21.10** That coming to be and passing away are one is not possible; \
that coming to be and passing away are different is not possible.

**21.11** "Coming to be is seen, and passing away as well"—that might be yours; \
coming to be is seen, and passing away as well, from delusion.

**21.12** An existent is not born from an existent; an existent is not born from a non-existent; \
a non-existent is not born from a non-existent; a non-existent is not born from an existent.

**21.13** An existent is not born from itself, nor is it born from another; \
it is not born from itself and another: from where is it born?

**21.14** For one who has accepted an existent, the view of the eternal and of annihilation \
follows; for that existent would be either permanent or impermanent.[^186]

**21.15** For one who has accepted an existent,[^187] there is neither annihilation nor the eternal; \
for that `bhava` (existence)[^188] is the continuum of appearance and disappearance of fruit and primary cause.

**21.16** If that `bhava` is the continuum of appearance and disappearance of fruit and primary cause, \
since there is no arising again of what has disappeared,[^189] annihilation of the primary cause follows.

**21.17** And of what really exists by `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature), non-existence is not fitting; \
and at the time of `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) there is annihilation,[^190] from the quieting of the continuity of `bhava`.

**21.18** And when the last has ceased, the first `bhava` is not fitting; \
and when the last has not ceased, the first `bhava` is not fitting.

**21.19** If, while the last is ceasing, the first is born, \
the one ceasing would be one and the one being born would be another.

**21.20** If both the one ceasing and the one being born are not fitting \
together,[^191] then one is born in those `skandhas` (collections) in which one dies.

**21.21** Thus in all three times the continuity of `bhava` is not fitting; \
that which does not exist in the three times, how is it a continuity of `bhava`?

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**21.1** vinā vā saha vā nāsti vibhavaḥ sambhavena vai | \
vinā vā saha vā nāsti saṃbhavo vibhavena vai ||

**21.2** bhaviṣyati kathaṃ nāma vibhavaḥ saṃbhavaṃ vinā | \
vinaiva janma maraṇaṃ vibhavo nodbhavaṃ vinā ||

**21.3** saṃbhavenaiva vibhavaḥ kathaṃ saha bhaviṣyati | \
na janma maraṇaṃ caiva tulyakālaṃ hi vidyate ||

**21.4** bhaviṣyati kathaṃ nāma saṃbhavo vibhavaṃ vinā | \
anityatā hi bhāveṣu na kadācinna vidyate ||

**21.5** saṃbhavo vibhavenaiva kathaṃ saha bhaviṣyati | \
na janma maraṇaṃ caiva tulyakālaṃ hi vidyate ||

**21.6** sahānyonyena vā siddhirvinānyonyena vā yayoḥ | \
na vidyate tayoḥ siddhiḥ kathaṃ nu khalu vidyate ||

**21.7** kṣayasya saṃbhavo nāsti nākṣayasyāsti saṃbhavaḥ | \
kṣayasya vibhavo nāsti vibhavo nākṣayasya ca ||

**21.8** saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva vinā bhāvaṃ na vidyate | \
saṃbhavaṃ vibhavaṃ caiva vinā bhāvo na vidyate ||

**21.9** saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva na śūnyasyopapadyate | \
saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva nāśūnyasyopapadyate ||

**21.10** saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva naika ityupapadyate | \
saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva na nānetyupapadyate ||

**21.11** dṛśyate saṃbhavaścaiva vibhavaścaiva te bhavet | \
dṛśyate saṃbhavaścaiva mohād vibhava eva ca ||

**21.12** na bhāvājjāyate bhāvo bhāvo’bhāvānna jāyate | \
nābhāvājjāyate’bhāvo’bhāvo bhāvānna jāyate ||

**21.13** na svato jāyate bhāvaḥ parato naiva jāyate | \
na svataḥ parataścaiva jāyate jāyate kutaḥ ||

**21.14** bhāvamabhyupapannasya śāśvatocchedadarśanam | \
prasajyate sa bhāvo hi nityo’nityo’tha vā bhavet ||

**21.15** bhāvabhyupapannasya naivocchedo na śāśvatam | \
udayavyayasaṃtānaḥ phalahetvorbhavaḥ sa hi ||

**21.16** udayavyayasaṃtānaḥ phalahetvorbhavaḥ sa cet | \
vyayasyāpunarutpatterhetūcchedaḥ prasajyate ||

**21.17** sadbhāvasya svabhāvena nāsadbhāvaśca yujyate | \
nirvāṇakāle cocchedaḥ praśamād bhavasaṃtateḥ ||

**21.18** carame na niruddhe ca prathamo yujyate bhavaḥ | \
carame nāniruddhe ca prathamo yujyate bhavaḥ ||

**21.19** nirudhyamāne carame prathamo yadi jāyate | \
nirudhyamāna eka syājjāyamāno’paro bhavet ||

**21.20** na cennirudhyamānaśca jāyamānaśca yujyate | \
sārdhaṃ ca mriyate yeṣu teṣu skandheṣu jāyate ||

**21.21** evaṃ triṣvapi kāleṣu na yuktā bhavasaṃtatiḥ | \
triṣu kāleṣu yā nāsti sā kathaṃ bhavasaṃtatiḥ ||

21.7 kṣayasya saṃbhavo nāsti nākṣayasyāsti saṃbhavaḥ kṣayasya vibhavo nāsti vibhavo nākṣayasya ca  
🚹⨀⑥(kṣaya) 🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(na+kṣaya) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) 🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀⑥(kṣaya) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(na+kṣaya) ⏏️(ca)  
of decay | coming to be | there is not | not | of non-decay | there is | coming to be | of decay | passing away | there is not | passing away | not | of non-decay | and  
There is no coming to be of what decays; there is no coming to be of what does not decay;  
there is no passing away of what decays, and no passing away of what does not decay.

21.8 saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva vinā bhāvaṃ na vidyate saṃbhavaṃ vibhavaṃ caiva vinā bhāvo na vidyate  
🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(vinā) 🚹⨀②(bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚹⨀②(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀②(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(vinā) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
coming to be | passing away | and | indeed | without | an existent | not | is found | coming to be | passing away | and | indeed | without | an existent | not | is found  
Coming to be and passing away are not found without an existent;  
an existent is not found without coming to be and passing away.

21.9 saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva na śūnyasyopapadyate saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva nāśūnyasyopapadyate  
🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥(śūnya) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) 🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(na+śūnya) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
coming to be | passing away | and | indeed | not | of what is empty | is possible | coming to be | passing away | and | indeed | not | of what is not empty | is possible  
Coming to be and passing away are not possible of what is empty;  
coming to be and passing away are not possible of what is not empty.

21.10 saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva naika ityupapadyate saṃbhavo vibhavaścaiva na nānetyupapadyate  
🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(eka) ⏏️(iti) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) 🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(nānā) ⏏️(iti) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
coming to be | passing away | and | indeed | not | one | thus | is possible | coming to be | passing away | and | indeed | not | different | thus | is possible  
That coming to be and passing away are one is not possible;  
that coming to be and passing away are different is not possible.

21.11 dṛśyate saṃbhavaścaiva vibhavaścaiva te bhavet dṛśyate saṃbhavaścaiva mohād vibhava eva ca  
🔵⨀🤟▶️(dṛśyate) 🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀⑥(yuṣmad) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(dṛśyate) 🚹⨀①(saṃbhava) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀⑤(moha) 🚹⨀①(vibhava) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(ca)  
is seen | coming to be | and | indeed | passing away | and | indeed | yours | might be | is seen | coming to be | and | indeed | from delusion | passing away | indeed | and  
"Coming to be is seen, and passing away as well"—that might be yours;  
coming to be is seen, and passing away as well, from delusion.

21.12 na bhāvājjāyate bhāvo bhāvo’bhāvānna jāyate nābhāvājjāyate’bhāvo’bhāvo bhāvānna jāyate  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑤(bhāva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) 🚹⨀⑤(abhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑤(abhāva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) 🚹⨀①(abhāva) 🚹⨀①(abhāva) 🚹⨀⑤(bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate)  
not | from an existent | is born | an existent | an existent | from a non-existent | not | is born | not | from a non-existent | is born | a non-existent | a non-existent | from an existent | not | is born  
An existent is not born from an existent; an existent is not born from a non-existent;  
a non-existent is not born from a non-existent; a non-existent is not born from an existent.

21.13 na svato jāyate bhāvaḥ parato naiva jāyate na svataḥ parataścaiva jāyate jāyate kutaḥ  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(svatas) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(paratas) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(svatas) ⏏️(paratas) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) ⏏️(kutas)  
not | from itself | is born | an existent | from another | not | at all | is born | not | from itself | from another | and | at all | is born | is born | whence  
An existent is not born from itself, nor is it born from another;  
it is not born from itself and another: from where is it born?

21.14 bhāvamabhyupapannasya śāśvatocchedadarśanam prasajyate sa bhāvo hi nityo’nityo’tha vā bhavet  
🚹⨀②(bhāva) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(abhyupapanna) 🚻⨀①🆎(śāśvata+uccheda+darśana) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀①(nitya) 🚹⨀①(anitya) ⏏️(atha) ⏏️(vā) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati)  
an existent | of one who has accepted | the view of the eternal and of annihilation | follows | that | existent | for | permanent | impermanent | then | or | would be  
For one who has accepted an existent, the view of the eternal and of annihilation  
follows; for that existent would be either permanent or impermanent.

21.15 bhāvabhyupapannasya naivocchedo na śāśvatam udayavyayasaṃtānaḥ phalahetvorbhavaḥ sa hi  
🚹⨀②(bhāva) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(abhyupapanna) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀①(uccheda) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①(śāśvata) 🚹⨀①🆎(udaya+vyaya+saṃtāna) 🚹⨁⑥🆎(phala+hetu) 🚹⨀①(bhava) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(hi)  
an existent | of one who has accepted | not | at all | annihilation | not | the eternal | the continuum of appearance and disappearance | of fruit and primary cause | existence | that | for  
For one who has accepted an existent, there is neither annihilation nor the eternal;  
for that `bhava` (existence) is the continuum of appearance and disappearance of fruit and primary cause.

21.16 udayavyayasaṃtānaḥ phalahetvorbhavaḥ sa cet vyayasyāpunarutpatterhetūcchedaḥ prasajyate  
🚹⨀①🆎(udaya+vyaya+saṃtāna) 🚹⨁⑥🆎(phala+hetu) 🚹⨀①(bhava) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(cet) 🚹⨀⑥(vyaya) 🚺⨀⑤🆎(na+punar+utpatti) 🚹⨀①🆎(hetu+uccheda) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate)  
the continuum of appearance and disappearance | of fruit and primary cause | existence | that | if | of the disappearance | since there is no arising again | annihilation of the primary cause | follows  
If that `bhava` is the continuum of appearance and disappearance of fruit and primary cause,  
since there is no arising again of what has disappeared, annihilation of the primary cause follows.

21.17 sadbhāvasya svabhāvena nāsadbhāvaśca yujyate nirvāṇakāle cocchedaḥ praśamād bhavasaṃtateḥ  
🚹⨀⑥🆎(sad+bhāva) 🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+sad+bhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) 🚹⨀⑦🆎(nirvāṇa+kāla) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(uccheda) 🚹⨀⑤(praśama) 🚺⨀⑥🆎(bhava+saṃtati)  
of what really exists | by intrinsic nature | not | non-existence | and | is fitting | at the time of extinguishment | and | annihilation | from the quieting | of the continuity of existence  
And of what really exists by `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature), non-existence is not fitting;  
and at the time of `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) there is annihilation, from the quieting of the continuity of `bhava`.

21.18 carame na niruddhe ca prathamo yujyate bhavaḥ carame nāniruddhe ca prathamo yujyate bhavaḥ  
🚹⨀⑦(carama) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(niruddha) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(prathama) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) 🚹⨀①(bhava) 🚹⨀⑦(carama) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)🆎(na+niruddha) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(prathama) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) 🚹⨀①(bhava)  
when the last | not | has ceased | and | the first | is fitting | existence | when the last | not | has not ceased | and | the first | is fitting | existence  
And when the last has ceased, the first `bhava` is not fitting;  
and when the last has not ceased, the first `bhava` is not fitting.

21.19 nirudhyamāne carame prathamo yadi jāyate nirudhyamāna eka syājjāyamāno’paro bhavet  
🚹⨀⑦🔽(māna)(nirudhyamāna) 🚹⨀⑦(carama) 🚹⨀①(prathama) ⏏️(yadi) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(nirudhyamāna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(eka) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(jāyamāna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①🆎(na+para) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati)  
while ceasing | the last | the first | if | is born | the one ceasing | one | would be | the one being born | another | would be  
If, while the last is ceasing, the first is born,  
the one ceasing would be one and the one being born would be another.

21.20 na cennirudhyamānaśca jāyamānaśca yujyate sārdhaṃ ca mriyate yeṣu teṣu skandheṣu jāyate  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(cet) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(nirudhyamāna) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(jāyamāna) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) ⏏️(sārdham) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(mriyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑦(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑦(tad) 🚹⨂⑦(skandha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate)  
not | if | the one ceasing | and | the one being born | and | is fitting | together | and | dies | in which | in those | collections | is born  
If both the one ceasing and the one being born are not fitting  
together, then one is born in those `skandhas` (collections) in which one dies.

21.21 evaṃ triṣvapi kāleṣu na yuktā bhavasaṃtatiḥ triṣu kāleṣu yā nāsti sā kathaṃ bhavasaṃtatiḥ  
⏏️(evam) 🚹⨂⑦(tri) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨂⑦(kāla) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(yukta) 🚺⨀①🆎(bhava+saṃtati) 🚹⨂⑦(tri) 🚻⨂⑦(kāla) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(yad) ⏏️(nāsti) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(tad) ⏏️(katham) 🚺⨀①🆎(bhava+saṃtati)  
thus | in the three | even | times | not | fitting | continuity of existence | in the three | times | which | does not exist | that | how | continuity of existence  
Thus in all three times the continuity of `bhava` is not fitting;  
that which does not exist in the three times, how is it a continuity of `bhava`?

### §22 Tathāgataparīkṣā (Examination of the thus-gone one)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**22.1** Not the `skandhas` (collections), not other than the `skandhas`; the `skandhas` are not in him, he is not in them; \
the `tathāgata` (thus-gone one) does not possess the `skandhas`; which one here is the `tathāgata`?

**22.2** If the Buddha, taking up the `skandhas`[^192], does not exist by way of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature), \
and he who does not exist by way of `svabhāva`, whence is he by way of `parabhāva` (extrinsic nature)?

**22.3** Whoever is dependent on `parabhāva`, 'he is not a self' is possible[^193]; \
and whoever is not-self, how will he be the `tathāgata`?

**22.4** And if there is no `svabhāva`, how could there be `parabhāva`? \
Apart from `svabhāva` and `parabhāva`, who is that `tathāgata`?

**22.5** If, not taking up the `skandhas`[^194], there could be some `tathāgata`, \
he would now take them up; having taken them up, he would then exist.

**22.6** And also, not taking up the `skandhas`, there is not any `tathāgata`; \
and he who, not taking up, does not exist, how will he take up?[^195]

**22.7** And there is no `upādāna` (fuel)[^196] whatsoever that is not taken up; \
and there is in no way a `tathāgata` without `upādāna`[^197].

**22.8** He who, being sought in five ways, does not exist by way of identity or otherness[^198], \
how is that `tathāgata` designated by means of `upādāna`?

**22.9** Also whatever this `upādāna` is, that is not found by way of `svabhāva`; \
and what does not exist by way of `svabhāva`, whence is that by way of `parabhāva`?

**22.10** Thus the `upādāna` and the taker of fuel are empty in every way; \
and how is an empty `tathāgata` designated by what is empty?

**22.11** "Empty" is not to be said, nor would "not empty" be, \
nor "both", nor "not both"; but it is said for the sake of `prajñapti` (designation).

**22.12** Whence here, in one at peace, the tetrad eternal, not eternal and so on? \
And also whence here, in one at peace, the tetrad finite, infinite and so on?

**22.13** But he by whom a firm grasp has been grasped that the `tathāgata` exists, \
conceiving that he does not exist[^199], would imagine it even of the extinguished one.

**22.14** And with regard to this one, empty by way of `svabhāva`, the thought is not possible at all \
that after cessation the Buddha exists, or does not exist.

**22.15** Those who make `prapañca` (proliferation) of the Buddha, who is beyond `prapañca` and imperishable, \
all those, struck down by `prapañca`, do not see the `tathāgata`.

**22.16** Whatever `svabhāva` the `tathāgata` has, that `svabhāva` this world has; \
the `tathāgata` is without `svabhāva`; this world is without `svabhāva`.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**22.1** skandhā na nānyaḥ skandhebhyo nāsmin skandhā na teṣu saḥ | \
tathāgataḥ skandhavān na katamo’tra tathāgataḥ ||

**22.2** buddhaḥ skandhanupādāya yadi nāsti svabhāvataḥ | \
svabhāvataśca yo nāsti kutaḥ sa parabhāvataḥ ||

**22.3** pratītya parabhāvaṃ yaḥ sa nātmetyupapadyate | \
yaścānātmā sa ca kathaṃ bhaviṣyati tathāgataḥ ||

**22.4** yadi nāsti svabhāvaśca parabhāvaḥ kathaṃ bhavet | \
svabhāvaparabhāvābhyāmṛte kaḥ sa tathāgataḥ ||

**22.5** skandhān yadyanupādāya bhavet kaścit tathāgataḥ | \
sa idānīmupādadyādupādāya tato bhavet ||

**22.6** skandhāṃścāpyanupādāya nāsti kaścit tathāgataḥ | \
yaśca nāstyanupādāya sa upādāsyate katham ||

**22.7** na bhavatyanupādattamupādānaṃ ca kiṃcana | \
na cāsti nirupādānaḥ kathaṃcana tathāgataḥ ||

**22.8** tattvānyatvena yo nāsti mṛgyamāṇaśca pañcadhā | \
upādānena sa kathaṃ prajñapyate tathāgataḥ ||

**22.9** yadapīdamupādānaṃ tat svabhāvānna vidyate | \
svabhāvataśca yannāsti kutastat parabhāvataḥ ||

**22.10** evaṃ śūnyamupādānamupādātā ca sarvaśaḥ | \
prajñapyate ca śūnyena kathaṃ śūnyastathāgataḥ ||

**22.11** śūnyamiti na vaktavyamaśūnyamiti vā bhavet | \
ubhayaṃ nobhayaṃ ceti prajñaptyarthaṃ tu kathyate ||

**22.12** śāśvatāśāśvatādyatra kutaḥ śānte catuṣṭayam | \
antānantādi cāpyatra kutaḥ śānte catuṣṭayam ||

**22.13** ghanagrāho gṛhītastu yenāstīti tathāgataḥ | \
nāstīti sa vikalpayan nirvṛtasyāpi kalpayet ||

**22.14** svabhāvataśca śūnye’smiṃścintā naivopapadyate | \
paraṃ nirodhād bhavati buddho na bhavatīti vā ||

**22.15** prapañcayanti ye buddhaṃ prapañcātītamavyayam | \
te prapañcahatāḥ sarve na paśyanti tathāgatam ||

**22.16** tathāgato yatsvabhāvastatsvabhāvamidaṃ jagat | \
tathāgato niḥsvabhāvo niḥsvabhāvamidaṃ jagat ||

</TabItem>

22.1 skandhā na nānyaḥ skandhebhyo nāsmin skandhā na teṣu saḥ tathāgataḥ skandhavān na katamo’tra tathāgataḥ  
🚹⨂①(skandha) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(anya) 🚹⨂⑤(skandha) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(idam) 🚹⨂①(skandha) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑦(tad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①(skandhavat) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(katama) ⏏️(atra) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata)  
collections | not | not | other | than the collections | not | in him | collections | not | in them | he | thus-gone one | possessing collections | not | which one | here | thus-gone one  
Not the `skandhas` (collections), not other than the `skandhas`; the `skandhas` are not in him, he is not in them;  
the `tathāgata` (thus-gone one) does not possess the `skandhas`; which one here is the `tathāgata`?

22.2 buddhaḥ skandhanupādāya yadi nāsti svabhāvataḥ svabhāvataśca yo nāsti kutaḥ sa parabhāvataḥ  
🚹⨀①(buddha) 🚹⨂②(skandha) 🔽(ya)(upādadāti) ⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(svabhāvatas) ⏏️(svabhāvatas) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(kutas) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(parabhāvatas)  
the Buddha | the collections | taking up | if | does not exist | by way of intrinsic nature | by way of intrinsic nature | and | who | does not exist | whence | he | by way of extrinsic nature  
If the Buddha, taking up the `skandhas`, does not exist by way of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature),  
and he who does not exist by way of `svabhāva`, whence is he by way of `parabhāva` (extrinsic nature)?

22.3 pratītya parabhāvaṃ yaḥ sa nātmetyupapadyate yaścānātmā sa ca kathaṃ bhaviṣyati tathāgataḥ  
🔽(ya)(pratyeti) 🚹⨀②🆎(para+bhāva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(ātman) ⏏️(iti) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①(anātman) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(katham) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata)  
depending on | extrinsic nature | who | he | not | self | thus | is possible | who | and | not-self | he | and | how | will be | thus-gone one  
Whoever is dependent on `parabhāva`, 'he is not a self' is possible;  
and whoever is not-self, how will he be the `tathāgata`?

22.4 yadi nāsti svabhāvaśca parabhāvaḥ kathaṃ bhavet svabhāvaparabhāvābhyāmṛte kaḥ sa tathāgataḥ  
⏏️(yadi) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚹⨀①🆎(sva+bhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(para+bhāva) ⏏️(katham) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚹⨁⑤🆎(sva+bhāva+para+bhāva) ⏏️(ṛte) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kim) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata)  
if | is not | intrinsic nature | and | extrinsic nature | how | could be | intrinsic nature and extrinsic nature | apart from | who | that | thus-gone one  
And if there is no `svabhāva`, how could there be `parabhāva`?  
Apart from `svabhāva` and `parabhāva`, who is that `tathāgata`?

22.5 skandhān yadyanupādāya bhavet kaścit tathāgataḥ sa idānīmupādadyādupādāya tato bhavet  
🚹⨂②(skandha) ⏏️(yadi) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+upādadāti) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(idānīm) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(upādadāti) 🔽(ya)(upādadāti) ⏏️(tatas) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati)  
collections | if | not taking up | could be | some | thus-gone one | he | now | would take up | having taken up | then | would exist  
If, not taking up the `skandhas`, there could be some `tathāgata`,  
he would now take them up; having taken them up, he would then exist.

22.6 skandhāṃścāpyanupādāya nāsti kaścit tathāgataḥ yaśca nāstyanupādāya sa upādāsyate katham  
🚹⨂②(skandha) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(api) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+upādadāti) ⏏️(nāsti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(nāsti) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+upādadāti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(upādadāti) ⏏️(katham)  
collections | and | also | not taking up | there is not | any | thus-gone one | who | and | does not exist | not taking up | he | will take up | how  
And also, not taking up the `skandhas`, there is not any `tathāgata`;  
and he who, not taking up, does not exist, how will he take up?

22.7 na bhavatyanupādattamupādānaṃ ca kiṃcana na cāsti nirupādānaḥ kathaṃcana tathāgataḥ  
⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+upādatta) 🚻⨀①(upādāna) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kaścana) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) 🚹⨀①(nirupādāna) ⏏️(kathaṃcana) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata)  
not | there is | not taken up | fuel | and | whatsoever | not | and | is | without fuel | in any way | thus-gone one  
And there is no `upādāna` (fuel) whatsoever that is not taken up;  
and there is in no way a `tathāgata` without `upādāna`.

22.8 tattvānyatvena yo nāsti mṛgyamāṇaśca pañcadhā upādānena sa kathaṃ prajñapyate tathāgataḥ  
🚻⨀③🆎(tattva+anyatva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)(mṛgyamāṇa) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(pañcadhā) 🚻⨀③(upādāna) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(katham) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prajñapyate) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata)  
by identity or otherness | who | does not exist | being sought | and | in five ways | by means of fuel | that | how | is designated | thus-gone one  
He who, being sought in five ways, does not exist by way of identity or otherness,  
how is that `tathāgata` designated by means of `upādāna`?

22.9 yadapīdamupādānaṃ tat svabhāvānna vidyate svabhāvataśca yannāsti kutastat parabhāvataḥ  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(api) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) 🚻⨀①(upādāna) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀⑤🆎(sva+bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) ⏏️(svabhāvatas) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(kutas) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) ⏏️(parabhāvatas)  
whatever | also | this | fuel | that | by way of intrinsic nature | not | is found | by way of intrinsic nature | and | what | does not exist | whence | that | by way of extrinsic nature  
Also whatever this `upādāna` is, that is not found by way of `svabhāva`;  
and what does not exist by way of `svabhāva`, whence is that by way of `parabhāva`?

22.10 evaṃ śūnyamupādānamupādātā ca sarvaśaḥ prajñapyate ca śūnyena kathaṃ śūnyastathāgataḥ  
⏏️(evam) 🚻⨀①(śūnya) 🚻⨀①(upādāna) 🚹⨀①(upādātṛ) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(sarvaśas) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prajñapyate) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀③(śūnya) ⏏️(katham) 🚹⨀①(śūnya) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata)  
thus | empty | fuel | taker of fuel | and | in every way | is designated | and | by what is empty | how | empty | thus-gone one  
Thus the `upādāna` and the taker of fuel are empty in every way;  
and how is an empty `tathāgata` designated by what is empty?

22.11 śūnyamiti na vaktavyamaśūnyamiti vā bhavet ubhayaṃ nobhayaṃ ceti prajñaptyarthaṃ tu kathyate  
🚻⨀①(śūnya) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(vaktavya) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(vā) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ubhaya) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(ubhaya) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀②🆎(prajñapti+artha) ⏏️(tu) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(kathyate)  
empty | thus | not | to be said | not empty | thus | or | would be | both | not | both | and | thus | for the sake of designation | but | is said  
"Empty" is not to be said, nor would "not empty" be,  
nor "both", nor "not both"; but it is said for the sake of `prajñapti` (designation).

22.12 śāśvatāśāśvatādyatra kutaḥ śānte catuṣṭayam antānantādi cāpyatra kutaḥ śānte catuṣṭayam  
🚻⨀①🆎(śāśvata+na+śāśvata+ādi) ⏏️(atra) ⏏️(kutas) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(śānta) 🚻⨀①(catuṣṭaya) 🚻⨀①🆎(anta+na+anta+ādi) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(atra) ⏏️(kutas) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(śānta) 🚻⨀①(catuṣṭaya)  
eternal, not eternal and so on | here | whence | in one at peace | the tetrad | finite, infinite and so on | and | also | here | whence | in one at peace | the tetrad  
Whence here, in one at peace, the tetrad eternal, not eternal and so on?  
And also whence here, in one at peace, the tetrad finite, infinite and so on?

22.13 ghanagrāho gṛhītastu yenāstīti tathāgataḥ nāstīti sa vikalpayan nirvṛtasyāpi kalpayet  
🚹⨀①🆎(ghana+grāha) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(gṛhīta) ⏏️(tu) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(yad) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(iti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀①🔽(at)(vikalpayat) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(nirvṛta) ⏏️(api) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(kalpayati)  
a firm grasp | has been grasped | but | by whom | exists | thus | the thus-gone one | does not exist | thus | he | conceiving | of the extinguished one | even | would imagine  
But he by whom a firm grasp has been grasped that the `tathāgata` exists,  
conceiving that he does not exist, would imagine it even of the extinguished one.

22.14 svabhāvataśca śūnye’smiṃścintā naivopapadyate paraṃ nirodhād bhavati buddho na bhavatīti vā  
⏏️(svabhāvatas) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⑦(śūnya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑦(idam) 🚺⨀①(cintā) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) ⏏️(param) 🚹⨀⑤(nirodha) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati) 🚹⨀①(buddha) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(bhavati) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(vā)  
by way of intrinsic nature | and | empty | with regard to this one | the thought | not | at all | is possible | after | cessation | exists | the Buddha | not | exists | thus | or  
And with regard to this one, empty by way of `svabhāva`, the thought is not possible at all  
that after cessation the Buddha exists, or does not exist.

22.15 prapañcayanti ye buddhaṃ prapañcātītamavyayam te prapañcahatāḥ sarve na paśyanti tathāgatam  
🟢⨂🤟▶️(prapañcayati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) 🚹⨀②(buddha) 🚹⨀②🔽(ta)🆎(prapañca+atīta) 🚹⨀②🆎(na+vyaya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(prapañca+hata) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(sarva) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(paśyati) 🚹⨀②(tathāgata)  
make proliferation of | who | the Buddha | beyond proliferation | imperishable | those | struck down by proliferation | all | not | see | the thus-gone one  
Those who make `prapañca` (proliferation) of the Buddha, who is beyond `prapañca` and imperishable,  
all those, struck down by `prapañca`, do not see the `tathāgata`.

22.16 tathāgato yatsvabhāvastatsvabhāvamidaṃ jagat tathāgato niḥsvabhāvo niḥsvabhāvamidaṃ jagat  
🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🆎(yad+svabhāva) 🚻⨀①🆎(tad+svabhāva) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) 🚻⨀①(jagat) 🚹⨀①(tathāgata) 🚹⨀①🆎(niḥ+svabhāva) 🚻⨀①🆎(niḥ+svabhāva) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) 🚻⨀①(jagat)  
thus-gone one | having whatever intrinsic nature | having that intrinsic nature | this | world | thus-gone one | without intrinsic nature | without intrinsic nature | this | world  
Whatever `svabhāva` the `tathāgata` has, that `svabhāva` this world has;  
the `tathāgata` is without `svabhāva`; this world is without `svabhāva`.

### §23 Viparyāsaparīkṣā (Examination of the distortions)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**23.1** Passion, hatred and delusion are said to have conception as their source; \
for they come to be depending on the distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant[^200].

**23.2** Those which come to be depending on the distortions[^201] of the pleasant and the unpleasant, \
they are not found by way of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature)[^202]; therefore the `kleśas` (defilements) are not in `tattva` (reality).

**23.3** And the existence and non-existence of the self are not established in any way; \
without that, how are the existence and non-existence of the `kleśas` established?

**23.4** For these `kleśas` are someone's, and that one is not established; \
is there someone?[^203] Without anyone, do the `kleśas` exist, belonging to no one?

**23.5** Like the `dṛṣṭi` (view) of one's own body, the `kleśas` do not exist in the defiled one in five ways; \
like the `dṛṣṭi` of one's own body, the defiled one too does not exist in the `kleśas` in five ways.

**23.6** The distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant[^204] are not found by way of `svabhāva`; \
depending on which distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant are the `kleśas`?

**23.7** Forms, sounds, tastes, tangibles[^205], smells and `dharmas` (phenomena): the sixfold \
object of passion, of hatred[^206] and of delusion is conceived.

**23.8** Mere[^207] forms, sounds, tastes, tangibles, smells and `dharmas` \
have the appearance of a city of the gandharvas, resembling a mirage and a dream.

**23.9** Whence will either the unpleasant or even the pleasant be in those \
which resemble illusory persons[^208] and are equal to reflections?

**23.10** Independently of the pleasant[^209] there is no unpleasant; we would designate[^210] \
the pleasant depending on it; therefore the pleasant is not possible at all.

**23.11** Independently of the unpleasant there is no pleasant; we would designate \
the unpleasant depending on it; therefore the unpleasant is not found at all.

**23.12** And when the pleasant is not found, whence will passion be? \
And when the unpleasant is not found, whence will hatred be?

**23.13** If the grasp "permanent" in regard to the impermanent is thus a distortion, \
the impermanent is not found in what is empty; whence is the grasp a distortion?[^211]

**23.14** If the grasp "permanent" in regard to the impermanent is thus a distortion, \
why is the grasp "impermanent" in what is empty[^212] not a distortion too?

**23.15** That by which one grasps, the grasp, the grasper and what is grasped: \
all are calmed; therefore a grasp is not found.

**23.16** And when a grasp is not found, whether wrong or indeed right, \
whose would be the distortion, whose would be the non-distortion?

**23.17** And neither do distortions come to be for one who is distorted, \
nor do distortions come to be for one who is not distorted.

**23.18** Distortions do not come to be for one who is being distorted; \
consider for yourself: for whom do distortions come to be?

**23.19** How indeed will distortions that have not arisen come to be? \
When distortions are unborn, whence one gone into distortion?

**23.20** An existent is not born from itself, nor is it born from another, \
nor from itself and another; so whence one gone into distortion?[^213]

**23.21** If the self, and the pure, and the permanent, and happiness are found, \
the self, and the pure, and the permanent, and happiness are not distortions.

**23.22** If the self, and the pure, and the permanent, and happiness are not found, \
not-self, the impure and the impermanent, and `duḥkha` (suffering), are not found at all.

**23.23** Thus `avidyā` (ignorance) ceases through the cessation of distortion; \
when `avidyā` has ceased, what begins with the `saṃskāras` (mental constructions) ceases.

**23.24** For if some `kleśas` of someone were real by `svabhāva`[^214], \
how indeed could they be abandoned? Who will abandon a `svabhāva`?

**23.25** For if some `kleśas` of someone were unreal by `svabhāva`, \
how indeed could they be abandoned? Who will abandon a non-existence?

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**23.1** saṃkalpaprabhavo rāgo dveṣo mohaśca kathyate | \
śubhāśubhaviparyāsān saṃbhavanti pratītya hi ||

**23.2** śubhāśubhaviparyāsān saṃbhavanti pratītya ye | \
te svabhāvāanna vidyante tasmāt kleśā na tattvataḥ ||

**23.3** ātmano’stitvanāstitve na kathaṃcicca sidhyataḥ | \
taṃ vināstitvanāstitve kleśānāṃ sidhyataḥ katham ||

**23.4** kasyaciddhi bhavantīme kleśāḥ sa ca na sidhyati | \
kaścidāho vinā kaṃcit santi kleśā na kasyacit ||

**23.5** svakāyadṛṣṭivat kleśāḥ kliṣṭe santi na pañcadhā | \
svakāyadṛṣṭivat kliṣṭaṃ kleśeṣvapi na pañcadhā ||

**23.6** svabhāvato na vidyante śubhāśubhaviparyayāḥ | \
pratītya katamān kleśāḥ śubhāśubhaviparyayān ||

**23.7** rūpaśabdarasasparśā gandhā dharmāśca ṣaḍvidham | \
vastu rāgasya doṣasya mohasya ca vikalpyate ||

**23.8** rūpaśabdarasasparśā gandhā dharmāśca kevalāḥ | \
gandharvanagarākārā marīcisvapnasaṃnibhāḥ ||

**23.9** aśubhaṃ vā śubhaṃ vāpi kutasteṣu bhaviṣyati | \
māyāpuruṣakalpeṣu pratibimbasameṣu ca ||

**23.10** anapekṣya śubha nāstyaśubhaṃ prajñapayemahi | \
yat pratītya śubhaṃ tasmācchubhaṃ naivopapadyate ||

**23.11** anapekṣyāśubhaṃ nāsti śubhaṃ prajñapayemahi | \
yat pratītyāśubhaṃ tasmādaśubhaṃ naiva vidyate ||

**23.12** avidyamāne ca śubhe kuto rāgo bhaviṣyati | \
aśubhe’vidyamāne ca kuto dveṣo bhaviṣyati ||

**23.13** anitye nityamityevaṃ yadi grāho viparyayaḥ | \
nānityaṃ vidyate śūnye kuto grāho viparyayaḥ ||

**23.14** anitye nityamityevaṃ yadi grāho viparyayaḥ | \
anityamityapi grāhaḥ śūnye kiṃ na viparyayaḥ ||

**23.15** yena gṛhṇāti yo grāho grahītā yacca gṛhyate | \
upaśāntāni sarvāṇi tasmād grāho na vidyate ||

**23.16** avidyamāne grāhe ca mithyā vā samyageva vā | \
bhaved viparyayaḥ kasya bhavet kasyāviparyayaḥ ||

**23.17** na cāpi viparītasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ | \
na cāpyaviparītasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ ||

**23.18** na viparyasyamānasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ | \
vimṛśasva svayaṃ kasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ ||

**23.19** anutpannāḥ kathaṃ nāma bhaviṣyanti viparyayāḥ | \
viparyayeṣvajāteṣu viparyayagataḥ kutaḥ ||

**23.20** [ na svato jāyate bhāvaḥ parato naiva jāyate | \
na svataḥ parataśceti viparyayagataḥ kutaḥ ||

**23.21** ] ātmā ca śuci nityaṃ ca sukhaṃ ca yadi vidyate | \
ātmā ca śuci nityaṃ ca sukhaṃ ca na viparyayāḥ ||

**23.22** nātmā ca śuci nityaṃ ca sukhaṃ ca yadi vidyate | \
anātmāśucyanityaṃ ca naiva duḥkhaṃ ca vidyate ||

**23.23** evaṃ nirudhyate’vidyā viparyayanirodhanāt | \
avidyāyāṃ niruddhāyāṃ saṃskārādyaṃ nirudhyate ||

**23.24** yadi bhūtāḥ svabhāvena kleśāḥ keciddhi kasyacit | \
kathaṃ nāma prahīyeran kaḥ svabhāvaṃ prahāsyati ||

**23.25** yadyabhūtāḥ svabhāvena kleśāḥ keciddhi kasyacit | \
kathaṃ nāma prahīyeran ko’sadbhāvaṃ prahāsyati ||

🚹⨀①🆎(saṃkalpa+prabhava) 🚹⨀①(rāga) 🚹⨀①(dveṣa) 🚹⨀①(moha) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(kathyate) 🚹⨂②🆎(śubhāśubha+viparyāsa) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(saṃbhavati) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) ⏏️(hi)  
having conception as source | passion | hatred | delusion | and | is said | the distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant | come to be | depending on | for  
Passion, hatred and delusion are said to have conception as their source;  
for they come to be depending on the distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant.

23.2 śubhāśubhaviparyāsān saṃbhavanti pratītya ye te svabhāvāanna vidyante tasmāt kleśā na tattvataḥ  
🚹⨂②🆎(śubhāśubha+viparyāsa) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(saṃbhavati) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(yad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨀⑤🆎(sva+bhāva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(vidyate) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚹⨂①(kleśa) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(tattvatas)  
the distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant | come to be | depending on | which | they | by way of intrinsic nature | not | are found | therefore | defilements | not | in reality  
Those which come to be depending on the distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant,  
they are not found by way of `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature); therefore the `kleśas` (defilements) are not in `tattva` (reality).

23.3 ātmano’stitvanāstitve na kathaṃcicca sidhyataḥ taṃ vināstitvanāstitve kleśānāṃ sidhyataḥ katham  
🚹⨀⑥(ātman) 🚻⨁①🆎(astitva+nāstitva) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(kathaṃcit) ⏏️(ca) 🟢⨁🤟▶️(sidhyati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(tad) ⏏️(vinā) 🚻⨁①🆎(astitva+nāstitva) 🚹⨂⑥(kleśa) 🟢⨁🤟▶️(sidhyati) ⏏️(katham)  
of the self | existence and non-existence | not | in any way | and | are established | that | without | existence and non-existence | of the defilements | are established | how  
And the existence and non-existence of the self are not established in any way;  
without that, how are the existence and non-existence of the `kleśas` established?

23.4 kasyaciddhi bhavantīme kleśāḥ sa ca na sidhyati kaścidāho vinā kaṃcit santi kleśā na kasyacit  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kaścit) ⏏️(hi) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(idam) 🚹⨂①(kleśa) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(sidhyati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) ⏏️(āho) ⏏️(vinā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(kaścit) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(asti) 🚹⨂①(kleśa) ⏏️(na) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kaścit)  
someone's | for | are | these | defilements | that one | and | not | is established | someone | or | without | anyone | exist | defilements | not | of anyone  
For these `kleśas` are someone's, and that one is not established;  
is there someone? Without anyone, do the `kleśas` exist, belonging to no one?

23.5 svakāyadṛṣṭivat kleśāḥ kliṣṭe santi na pañcadhā svakāyadṛṣṭivat kliṣṭaṃ kleśeṣvapi na pañcadhā  
⏏️🔽(vat)🆎(sva+kāya+dṛṣṭi) 🚹⨂①(kleśa) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(ta)(kliṣṭa) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(asti) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(pañcadhā) ⏏️🔽(vat)🆎(sva+kāya+dṛṣṭi) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(kliṣṭa) 🚹⨂⑦(kleśa) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(pañcadhā)  
like the view of one's own body | defilements | in the defiled one | exist | not | in five ways | like the view of one's own body | the defiled one | in the defilements | too | not | in five ways  
Like the `dṛṣṭi` (view) of one's own body, the `kleśas` do not exist in the defiled one in five ways;  
like the `dṛṣṭi` of one's own body, the defiled one too does not exist in the `kleśas` in five ways.

23.6 svabhāvato na vidyante śubhāśubhaviparyayāḥ pratītya katamān kleśāḥ śubhāśubhaviparyayān  
⏏️(svabhāvatas) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚹⨂①🆎(śubhāśubha+viparyaya) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂②(katama) 🚹⨂①(kleśa) 🚹⨂②🆎(śubhāśubha+viparyaya)  
by way of intrinsic nature | not | are found | the distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant | depending on | which | defilements | the distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant  
The distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant are not found by way of `svabhāva`;  
depending on which distortions of the pleasant and the unpleasant are the `kleśas`?

23.7 rūpaśabdarasasparśā gandhā dharmāśca ṣaḍvidham vastu rāgasya doṣasya mohasya ca vikalpyate  
🚹⨂①🆎(rūpa+śabda+rasa+sparśa) 🚹⨂①(gandha) 🚹⨂①(dharma) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①🆎(ṣaḍ+vidha) 🚻⨀①(vastu) 🚹⨀⑥(rāga) 🚹⨀⑥(doṣa) 🚹⨀⑥(moha) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vikalpyate)  
forms, sounds, tastes, tangibles | smells | phenomena | and | sixfold | object | of passion | of hatred | of delusion | and | is conceived  
Forms, sounds, tastes, tangibles, smells and `dharmas` (phenomena): the sixfold  
object of passion, of hatred and of delusion is conceived.

23.8 rūpaśabdarasasparśā gandhā dharmāśca kevalāḥ gandharvanagarākārā marīcisvapnasaṃnibhāḥ  
🚹⨂①🆎(rūpa+śabda+rasa+sparśa) 🚹⨂①(gandha) 🚹⨂①(dharma) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂①(kevala) 🚹⨂①🆎(gandharva+nagara+ākāra) 🚹⨂①🆎(marīci+svapna+saṃnibha)  
forms, sounds, tastes, tangibles | smells | phenomena | and | mere | having the appearance of a city of the gandharvas | resembling a mirage and a dream  
Mere forms, sounds, tastes, tangibles, smells and `dharmas`  
have the appearance of a city of the gandharvas, resembling a mirage and a dream.

23.9 aśubhaṃ vā śubhaṃ vāpi kutasteṣu bhaviṣyati māyāpuruṣakalpeṣu pratibimbasameṣu ca  
🚻⨀①🆎(na+śubha) ⏏️(vā) 🚻⨀①(śubha) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(kutas) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂⑦(tad) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚹⨂⑦🆎(māyā+puruṣa+kalpa) 🚹⨂⑦🆎(pratibimba+sama) ⏏️(ca)  
the unpleasant | either | the pleasant | or | even | whence | in those | will be | which resemble illusory persons | equal to reflections | and  
Whence will either the unpleasant or even the pleasant be in those  
which resemble illusory persons and are equal to reflections?

23.10 anapekṣya śubha nāstyaśubhaṃ prajñapayemahi yat pratītya śubhaṃ tasmācchubhaṃ naivopapadyate  
🔽(ya)🆎(na+apekṣate) 🚻⨀②(śubha) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śubha) 🔵⨂👆⏯(prajñapayati) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(yad) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) 🚻⨀②(śubha) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚻⨀①(śubha) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
independently of | the pleasant | there is not | the unpleasant | we would designate | which | depending on | the pleasant | therefore | the pleasant | not | at all | is possible  
Independently of the pleasant there is no unpleasant; we would designate  
the pleasant depending on it; therefore the pleasant is not possible at all.

23.11 anapekṣyāśubhaṃ nāsti śubhaṃ prajñapayemahi yat pratītyāśubhaṃ tasmādaśubhaṃ naiva vidyate  
🔽(ya)🆎(na+apekṣate) 🚻⨀②🆎(na+śubha) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚻⨀①(śubha) 🔵⨂👆⏯(prajñapayati) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(yad) 🔽(ya)(pratyeti) 🚻⨀②🆎(na+śubha) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śubha) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
independently of | the unpleasant | there is not | the pleasant | we would designate | which | depending on | the unpleasant | therefore | the unpleasant | not | at all | is found  
Independently of the unpleasant there is no pleasant; we would designate  
the unpleasant depending on it; therefore the unpleasant is not found at all.

23.12 avidyamāne ca śubhe kuto rāgo bhaviṣyati aśubhe’vidyamāne ca kuto dveṣo bhaviṣyati  
🚻⨀⑦🔽(māna)🆎(na+vidyamāna) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀⑦(śubha) ⏏️(kutas) 🚹⨀①(rāga) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚻⨀⑦🆎(na+śubha) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(māna)🆎(na+vidyamāna) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(kutas) 🚹⨀①(dveṣa) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
when … is not found | and | the pleasant | whence | passion | will be | the unpleasant | when … is not found | and | whence | hatred | will be  
And when the pleasant is not found, whence will passion be?  
And when the unpleasant is not found, whence will hatred be?

23.13 anitye nityamityevaṃ yadi grāho viparyayaḥ nānityaṃ vidyate śūnye kuto grāho viparyayaḥ  
🚻⨀⑦(anitya) 🚻⨀①(nitya) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(evam) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀①(grāha) 🚹⨀①(viparyaya) ⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①(anitya) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚻⨀⑦(śūnya) ⏏️(kutas) 🚹⨀①(grāha) 🚹⨀①(viparyaya)  
in regard to the impermanent | permanent | thus | in this way | if | the grasp | a distortion | not | the impermanent | is found | in what is empty | whence | the grasp | a distortion  
If the grasp "permanent" in regard to the impermanent is thus a distortion,  
the impermanent is not found in what is empty; whence is the grasp a distortion?

23.14 anitye nityamityevaṃ yadi grāho viparyayaḥ anityamityapi grāhaḥ śūnye kiṃ na viparyayaḥ  
🚻⨀⑦(anitya) 🚻⨀①(nitya) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(evam) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨀①(grāha) 🚹⨀①(viparyaya) 🚻⨀①(anitya) ⏏️(iti) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨀①(grāha) 🚻⨀⑦(śūnya) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(viparyaya)  
in regard to the impermanent | permanent | thus | in this way | if | the grasp | a distortion | impermanent | thus | too | the grasp | in what is empty | why | not | a distortion  
If the grasp "permanent" in regard to the impermanent is thus a distortion,  
why is the grasp "impermanent" in what is empty not a distortion too?

23.15 yena gṛhṇāti yo grāho grahītā yacca gṛhyate upaśāntāni sarvāṇi tasmād grāho na vidyate  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀③(yad) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(gṛhṇāti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀①(grāha) 🚹⨀①(grahītṛ) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(gṛhyate) 🚻⨂①🔽(ta)(upaśānta) *️⃣🤟🚻⨂①(sarva) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚹⨀①(grāha) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
by which | one grasps | which | the grasp | the grasper | what | and | is grasped | calmed | all | therefore | a grasp | not | is found  
That by which one grasps, the grasp, the grasper and what is grasped:  
all are calmed; therefore a grasp is not found.

23.16 avidyamāne grāhe ca mithyā vā samyageva vā bhaved viparyayaḥ kasya bhavet kasyāviparyayaḥ  
🚹⨀⑦🔽(māna)🆎(na+vidyamāna) 🚹⨀⑦(grāha) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(mithyā) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(samyak) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(vā) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚹⨀①(viparyaya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+viparyaya)  
when … is not found | a grasp | and | wrong | whether | right | indeed | or | would be | the distortion | whose | would be | whose | the non-distortion  
And when a grasp is not found, whether wrong or indeed right,  
whose would be the distortion, whose would be the non-distortion?

23.17 na cāpi viparītasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ na cāpyaviparītasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)(viparīta) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(saṃbhavati) 🚹⨂①(viparyaya) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(ta)🆎(na+viparīta) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(saṃbhavati) 🚹⨂①(viparyaya)  
not | and | also | of one who is distorted | come to be | distortions | not | and | also | of one who is not distorted | come to be | distortions  
And neither do distortions come to be for one who is distorted,  
nor do distortions come to be for one who is not distorted.

23.18 na viparyasyamānasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ vimṛśasva svayaṃ kasya saṃbhavanti viparyayāḥ  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(māna)(viparyasyamāna) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(saṃbhavati) 🚹⨂①(viparyaya) 🔵⨀🤘⏹(vimṛśati) ⏏️(svayam) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kim) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(saṃbhavati) 🚹⨂①(viparyaya)  
not | of one who is being distorted | come to be | distortions | consider | for yourself | for whom | come to be | distortions  
Distortions do not come to be for one who is being distorted;  
consider for yourself: for whom do distortions come to be?

23.19 anutpannāḥ kathaṃ nāma bhaviṣyanti viparyayāḥ viparyayeṣvajāteṣu viparyayagataḥ kutaḥ  
🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(na+utpanna) ⏏️(katham) ⏏️(nāma) 🟢⨂🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚹⨂①(viparyaya) 🚹⨂⑦(viparyaya) 🚹⨂⑦🔽(ta)🆎(na+jāta) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(viparyaya+gata) ⏏️(kutas)  
that have not arisen | how | indeed | will be | distortions | when distortions | are unborn | one gone into distortion | whence  
How indeed will distortions that have not arisen come to be?  
When distortions are unborn, whence one gone into distortion?

23.20 [ na svato jāyate bhāvaḥ parato naiva jāyate na svataḥ parataśceti viparyayagataḥ kutaḥ  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(svatas) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) 🚹⨀①(bhāva) ⏏️(paratas) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(jāyate) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(svatas) ⏏️(paratas) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(iti) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(viparyaya+gata) ⏏️(kutas)  
not | from itself | is born | existent | from another | not | at all | is born | not | from itself | from another | and | so | one gone into distortion | whence  
An existent is not born from itself, nor is it born from another,  
nor from itself and another; so whence one gone into distortion?

23.21 ] ātmā ca śuci nityaṃ ca sukhaṃ ca yadi vidyate ātmā ca śuci nityaṃ ca sukhaṃ ca na viparyayāḥ  
🚹⨀①(ātman) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①(śuci) 🚻⨀①(nitya) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①(sukha) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(yadi) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚹⨀①(ātman) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①(śuci) 🚻⨀①(nitya) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①(sukha) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨂①(viparyaya)  
the self | and | the pure | the permanent | and | happiness | and | if | is found | the self | and | the pure | the permanent | and | happiness | and | not | distortions  
If the self, and the pure, and the permanent, and happiness are found,  
the self, and the pure, and the permanent, and happiness are not distortions.

23.22 nātmā ca śuci nityaṃ ca sukhaṃ ca yadi vidyate anātmāśucyanityaṃ ca naiva duḥkhaṃ ca vidyate  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(ātman) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①(śuci) 🚻⨀①(nitya) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①(sukha) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(yadi) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚹⨀①(anātman) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śuci+anitya) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(eva) 🚻⨀①(duḥkha) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
not | the self | and | the pure | the permanent | and | happiness | and | if | is found | not-self | the impure and the impermanent | and | not | at all | suffering | and | is found  
If the self, and the pure, and the permanent, and happiness are not found,  
not-self, the impure and the impermanent, and `duḥkha` (suffering), are not found at all.

23.23 evaṃ nirudhyate’vidyā viparyayanirodhanāt avidyāyāṃ niruddhāyāṃ saṃskārādyaṃ nirudhyate  
⏏️(evam) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate) 🚺⨀①(avidyā) 🚻⨀⑤🆎(viparyaya+nirodhana) 🚺⨀⑦(avidyā) 🚺⨀⑦🔽(ta)(niruddha) 🚻⨀①🆎(saṃskāra+ādya) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(nirudhyate)  
thus | ceases | ignorance | through the cessation of distortion | when ignorance | has ceased | what begins with the mental constructions | ceases  
Thus `avidyā` (ignorance) ceases through the cessation of distortion;  
when `avidyā` has ceased, what begins with the `saṃskāras` (mental constructions) ceases.

23.24 yadi bhūtāḥ svabhāvena kleśāḥ keciddhi kasyacit kathaṃ nāma prahīyeran kaḥ svabhāvaṃ prahāsyati  
⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)(bhūta) 🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨂①(kleśa) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(kaścit) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kaścit) ⏏️(katham) ⏏️(nāma) 🔵⨂🤟⏯(prahīyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kim) 🚹⨀②🆎(sva+bhāva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(prajahāti)  
if | real | by intrinsic nature | defilements | some | for | of someone | how | indeed | could be abandoned | who | intrinsic nature | will abandon  
For if some `kleśas` of someone were real by `svabhāva`,  
how indeed could they be abandoned? Who will abandon a `svabhāva`?

23.25 yadyabhūtāḥ svabhāvena kleśāḥ keciddhi kasyacit kathaṃ nāma prahīyeran ko’sadbhāvaṃ prahāsyati  
⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨂①🔽(ta)🆎(na+bhūta) 🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨂①(kleśa) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(kaścit) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(kaścit) ⏏️(katham) ⏏️(nāma) 🔵⨂🤟⏯(prahīyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kim) 🚹⨀②🆎(na+sad+bhāva) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(prajahāti)  
if | unreal | by intrinsic nature | defilements | some | for | of someone | how | indeed | could be abandoned | who | a non-existence | will abandon  
For if some `kleśas` of someone were unreal by `svabhāva`,  
how indeed could they be abandoned? Who will abandon a non-existence?

</TabItem>
</Tabs>

### §24 Āryasatyaparīkṣā (Examination of the realisations)

<Tabs syncKey="sanskrit">
<TabItem label="Translation">
**24.1** If all this is empty, there is no arising, no passing away; \
the non-existence of the four `āryasatya` (realisations) follows for you.

**24.2** Full understanding, giving up, development and personal realisation[^215], \
because of the non-existence of the four `āryasatya`, are not possible.

**24.3** Because of their non-existence, the four fruits too[^216] are not found; \
when there is no fruit, those abiding in the fruit do not exist, nor those who have entered upon the way.

**24.4** There is no Saṃgha if those eight individual persons do not exist[^217]; \
and because of the non-existence of the `āryasatya`, the true `dharma` (teaching) too is not found.

**24.5** And when the `dharma` and the Saṃgha do not exist, how will there be a Buddha? \
Speaking thus, you reject all three jewels[^218].

**24.6** Speaking of `śūnyatā` (emptiness)[^219], you reject the real existence of the fruit, `adharma` (demerit) and `dharma` (merit)[^220], \
and all worldly conventions.

**24.7** Here we say: you do not know the purpose of `śūnyatā`, \
`śūnyatā` and the meaning of `śūnyatā`[^221]; therefore you are thus thwarted.

**24.8** The teaching of the `dharma` by the Buddhas rests upon two truths: \
the truth of worldly `saṃvṛti` (convention), and the truth according to the `paramārtha` (ultimate).

**24.9** Those who do not understand the distinction between these two truths \
do not understand the `tattva` (reality) in the profound instruction of the Buddha[^222].

**24.10** Without relying on `vyavahāra` (convention), the `paramārtha` is not taught; \
without arriving at the `paramārtha`, `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) is not attained.

**24.11** `śūnyatā` wrongly seen destroys one of dull intellect[^223], \
like a snake wrongly grasped, or a spell wrongly mastered[^224].

**24.12** And hence the mind of the sage was turned back from teaching \
the `dharma`, having considered how hard this `dharma` is for the dull to fathom.

**24.13** Moreover, the objection[^225] which you, sir, make to `śūnyatā`: \
that consequence of fault is not ours; it is not possible in the empty.

**24.14** And everything is fitting for one for whom `śūnyatā` is fitting; \
nothing is fitting for one for whom the empty is not fitting.

**24.15** You, hurling your own faults upon us, \
have, while mounted on the very horse, forgotten the very horse[^226].

**24.16** If you perceive the real existence of existents as from `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature), \
then, that being so, you see existents as without primary cause and cause[^227].

**24.17** Effect and cause, agent, instrument[^228], action, \
arising and cessation and fruit, you reject.

**24.18** That which is `pratītyasamutpāda` (dependent origination), we declare that to be `śūnyatā`; \
that is a `prajñapti` (designation) in dependence[^229]; that itself is the `madhyamā pratipad` (middle way).

**24.19** No `dharma` (phenomenon) whatever is found that has not dependently arisen; \
for which reason, indeed, no `dharma` whatever is found that is not empty.

**24.20** If all this is not empty, there is no arising, no passing away; \
the non-existence of the four `āryasatya` follows for you.

**24.21** How will there be `duḥkha` (suffering)[^230] that has not dependently arisen? \
For `duḥkha` is said to be impermanent; that is not found in the state of `svabhāva`.

**24.22** What exists by `svabhāva`, why will that originate again? \
Therefore there is no origin for one who rejects `śūnyatā`.

**24.23** No cessation of `duḥkha` that exists by `svabhāva` is found; \
because of the fixation on `svabhāva`[^231], you reject cessation.

**24.24** When there is the state of `svabhāva`, the development of the path is not possible; \
but if that path is developed, the state of `svabhāva` is not found for you[^232].

**24.25** When `duḥkha`, origin and cessation are not found, \
which path, for the cessation of `duḥkha`[^233], will bring one to attainment?

**24.26** If by `svabhāva` there is no full understanding of it[^234], how could there later[^235] \
be full understanding? Does not `svabhāva` indeed stand firm?

**24.27** Giving up and personal realisation[^236], and development, in just this way for you, \
like full understanding, are not fitting, and the four fruits too.

**24.28** A fruit which by `svabhāva` is unattained[^237], how could that later \
be attainable, for one who holds to `svabhāva`?

**24.29** When there is no fruit, those abiding in the fruit do not exist, nor those who have entered upon the way; \
there is no Saṃgha if those eight individual persons do not exist.

**24.30** And because of the non-existence of the `āryasatya`, the true `dharma` too is not found; \
and when the `dharma` and the Saṃgha do not exist, how will there be a Buddha?

**24.31** It follows, for you, that there is a Buddha even without depending on awakening; \
it follows, for you, that there is awakening even without depending on a Buddha.

**24.32** And one who by `svabhāva` is not a Buddha, even striving for awakening, \
will not, for you, attain awakening in the conduct of a bodhisattva.

**24.33** And no one will ever do `dharma` or `adharma`; \
what is to be done with what is not empty?[^238] For `svabhāva` is not made.

**24.34** For a fruit is found for you without `dharma` and `adharma`; \
and a fruit occasioned by[^239] `dharma` and `adharma` is not found for you[^240].

**24.35** Or if a fruit occasioned by `dharma` and `adharma` is found for you, \
how is a fruit arisen from `dharma` and `adharma` not empty, for you?

**24.36** And you reject all worldly conventions[^241], \
you who reject[^242] the `śūnyatā` of `pratītyasamutpāda`.

**24.37** There would be nothing to be done, action would be unbegun, \
an agent would be one not acting, for one who rejects `śūnyatā`.

**24.38** Unborn and unceased and immovable will be \
the world, devoid of its diverse states, when there is `svabhāva`.

**24.39** And the attainment of what is unattained, and the act of ending `duḥkha`, \
and the giving up of all `kleśa` (defilements) are not found, if it is not empty[^243].

**24.40** One who sees `pratītyasamutpāda`, he sees this: \
`duḥkha` and origin and cessation and the path.

</TabItem>

<TabItem label="Sanskrit (Roman IAST)">
**24.1** yadi śūnyamidaṃ sarvamudayo nāsti na vyayaḥ | \
caturṇāmāryasatyānāmabhāvaste prasajyate ||

**24.2** parijñā ca prahāṇaṃ ca bhāvanā sākṣikarma ca | \
caturṇāmāryasatyānāmabhāvānnopapadyate ||

**24.3** tadabhāvānna vidyante catvāryapi phalāni ca | \
phalābhāve phalasthā no na santi pratipannakāḥ ||

**24.4** saṃgho nāsti na cet santi te’ṣṭau puruṣapudgalāḥ | \
abhāvāccāryasatyānāṃ saddharmo’pi na vidyate ||

**24.5** dharme cāsati saṃghe ca kathaṃ buddho bhaviṣyati | \
evaṃ trīṇyapi ratnāni bruvāṇaḥ pratibāghase ||

**24.6** śūnyatāṃ phalasadbhāvamadharmaṃ dharmameva ca | \
sarvasaṃvyavahārāṃśca laukikān pratibādhase ||

**24.7** atra brūmaḥ śūnyatāyāṃ na tvaṃ vetsi prayojanam | \
śūnyatāṃ śūnyatārthaṃ ca tata evaṃ vihanyase ||

**24.8** dve satye samupāśritya buddhānāṃ dharmadeśanā | \
lokasaṃvṛtisatyaṃ ca satyaṃ ca paramārthataḥ ||

**24.9** ye’nayorna vijānanti vibhāgaṃ satyayordvayoḥ | \
te tattvaṃ na vijānanti gambhīre buddhaśāsane ||

**24.10** vyavahāramanāśritya paramārtho na deśyate | \
paramārthamanāgamya nirvāṇaṃ nādhigamyate ||

**24.11** vināśayati durdṛṣṭā śūnyatā mandameghasam | \
sarpo yathā durgṛhīto vidyā vā duṣprasādhitā ||

**24.12** ataśca pratyudāvṛttaṃ cittaṃ deśayituṃ muneḥ | \
dharmaṃ matvāsya dharmasya mandairduravagāhatām ||

**24.13** śūnyatāyāmadhilayaṃ yaṃ punaḥ kurute bhavān | \
doṣaprasaṅgo nāsmākaṃ sa śūnye nopapadyate ||

**24.14** sarvaṃ ca yujyate tasya śūnyatā yasya yujyate | \
sarvaṃ na yujyate tasya śūnyaṃ yasya na yujyate ||

**24.15** sa tvaṃ doṣānātmanīyānasmāsu paripātayan | \
aśvamevābhirūḍhaḥ sannaśvamevāsi vismṛtaḥ ||

**24.16** svabhāvād yadi bhāvānāṃ sadbhāvamanupaśyasi | \
ahetupratyayān bhāvāṃstvamevaṃ sati paśyasi ||

**24.17** kāryaṃ ca kāraṇaṃ caiva kartāraṃ karaṇaṃ kriyām | \
utpādaṃ ca nirodhaṃ ca phalaṃ ca pratibādhase ||

**24.18** yaḥ pratītyasamutpādaḥ śūnyatāṃ tāṃ pracakṣmahe | \
sā prajñaptirupādāya pratipat saiva madhyamā ||

**24.19** apratītyasamutpanno dharmaḥ kaścinna vidyate | \
yasmāt tasmādaśūnyo hi dharmaḥ kaścinna vidyate ||

**24.20** yadyaśūnyamidaṃ sarvamudayo nāsti na vyayaḥ | \
caturṇāmāryasatyānāmabhāvaste prasajyate ||

**24.21** apratītyasamutpannaṃ kato duḥkhaṃ bhaviṣyati | \
anityamuktaṃ duḥkhaṃ hi tat svābhāvye na vidyate ||

**24.22** svabhāvato vidyamānaṃ kiṃ punaḥ samudeṣyate | \
tasmāt samudayo nāsti śūnyatāṃ pratibādhataḥ ||

**24.23** na nirodhaḥ svabhāvena sato duḥkhasya vidyate | \
svabhāvaparyavasthānānnirodhaṃ pratibādhase ||

**24.24** svābhāvye sati mārgasya bhāvanā nopapadyate | \
athāsau bhāvyate mārgaḥ svābhāvyaṃ te na vidyate ||

**24.25** yadā duḥkhaṃ samudayo nirodhaśca na vidyate | \
mārgo duḥkhanirodhatvāt katamaḥ prāpayiṣyati ||

**24.26** svabhāvenāparijñānaṃ yadi tasya punaḥ katham | \
parijñānaṃ nanu kila svabhāvaḥ samavasthitaḥ ||

**24.27** prahāṇasākṣātkaraṇe bhāvanā caivameva te | \
parijñāvanna yujyante catvāryapi phalāni ca ||

**24.28** svabhāvenānadhigataṃ yat phalaṃ tat punaḥ katham | \
śakyaṃ samadhigantuṃ syāt svabhāvaṃ parigṛhṇataḥ ||

**24.29** phalābhāve phalasthā no na santi pratipannakāḥ | \
saṃgho nāsti na cet santi te’ṣṭau puruṣapudgalāḥ ||

**24.30** abhāvāccāryasatyānāṃ saddharmo’pi na vidyate | \
dharme cāsati saṃghe ca kathaṃ buddho bhaviṣyati ||

**24.31** apratītyāpi bodhiṃ ca tava buddhaḥ prasajyate | \
apratītyāpi buddhaṃ ca tava bodhiḥ prasajyate ||

**24.32** yaścābuddhaḥ svabhāvena sa bodhāya ghaṭannapi | \
na bodhisattvacaryāyāṃ bodhiṃ te’dhigamiṣyati ||

**24.33** na ca dharmamadharmaṃ vā kaścijjātu kariṣyati | \
kimaśūnyasya kartavyaṃ svabhāvaḥ kriyate na hi ||

**24.34** vinā dharmamadharmaṃ ca phalaṃ hi tava vidyate | \
dharmādharmanimittaṃ ca phalaṃ tava na vidyate ||

**24.35** dharmādharmanimittaṃ vā yadi te vidyate phalam | \
dharmādharmasamutpannamaśūnyaṃ te kathaṃ phalam ||

**24.36** sarvasaṃvyavahārāṃśca lokikān pratibādhase | \
yaḥ pratītyasamutpādaśūnyatāṃ pratibādhase ||

**24.37** na kartavyaṃ bhavet kiṃcidanārabdhā bhavet kriyā | \
kārakaḥ syādakurvāṇaḥ śūnyatāṃ pratibādhataḥ ||

**24.38** ajātamaniruddhaṃ ca kūṭasthaṃ ca bhaviṣyati | \
vicitrābhiravasthābhiḥ svabhāve rahitaṃ jagat ||

**24.39** asaṃprāptasya ca prāptirduḥkhaparyantakarma ca | \
sarvakleśaprahāṇaṃ ca yadyaśūnyaṃ na vidyate ||

**24.40** yaḥ pratītyasamutpādaṃ paśyatīdaṃ sa paśyati | \
duḥkhaṃ samudayaṃ caiva nirodhaṃ mārgameva ca ||

Those who do not understand the distinction between these two truths  
do not understand the `tattva` (reality) in the profound instruction of the Buddha.

24.10 vyavahāramanāśritya paramārtho na deśyate paramārthamanāgamya nirvāṇaṃ nādhigamyate  
🚹⨀②(vyavahāra) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+āśrayati) 🚹⨀①(paramārtha) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(deśyate) 🚹⨀②(paramārtha) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+āgacchati) 🚻⨀①(nirvāṇa) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(adhigamyate)  
convention | without relying on | the ultimate | not | is taught | the ultimate | without arriving at | extinguishment | not | is attained  
Without relying on `vyavahāra` (convention), the `paramārtha` is not taught;  
without arriving at the `paramārtha`, `nirvāṇa` (extinguishment) is not attained.

24.11 vināśayati durdṛṣṭā śūnyatā mandameghasam sarpo yathā durgṛhīto vidyā vā duṣprasādhitā  
🟢⨀🤟▶️(vināśayati) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(durdṛṣṭa) 🚺⨀①(śūnyatā) 🚹⨀②🆎(manda+medhas) 🚹⨀①(sarpa) ⏏️(yathā) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(durgṛhīta) 🚺⨀①(vidyā) ⏏️(vā) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)(duṣprasādhita)  
destroys | wrongly seen | emptiness | one of dull intellect | a snake | just as | wrongly grasped | a spell | or | wrongly mastered  
`śūnyatā` wrongly seen destroys one of dull intellect,  
like a snake wrongly grasped, or a spell wrongly mastered.

24.12 ataśca pratyudāvṛttaṃ cittaṃ deśayituṃ muneḥ dharmaṃ matvāsya dharmasya mandairduravagāhatām  
⏏️(atas) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(pratyudāvṛtta) 🚻⨀①(citta) 🔽(tum)(deśayati) 🚹⨀⑥(muni) 🚹⨀②(dharma) 🔽(tvā)(manyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(idam) 🚹⨀⑥(dharma) 🚹⨂③(manda) 🚺⨀②🔽(tā)(duravagāhatā)  
hence | and | was turned back | the mind | from teaching | of the sage | the dharma | having considered | of this | dharma | by the dull | how hard to fathom  
And hence the mind of the sage was turned back from teaching  
the `dharma`, having considered how hard this `dharma` is for the dull to fathom.

24.13 śūnyatāyāmadhilayaṃ yaṃ punaḥ kurute bhavān doṣaprasaṅgo nāsmākaṃ sa śūnye nopapadyate  
🚺⨀⑦(śūnyatā) 🚹⨀②(adhilaya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀②(yad) ⏏️(punar) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(karoti) 🚹⨀①(bhavat) 🚹⨀①🆎(doṣa+prasaṅga) ⏏️(na) *️⃣👆⚧⨂⑥(asmad) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀⑦(śūnya) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate)  
to emptiness | objection | which | moreover | make | you, sir | the consequence of fault | not | ours | that | in the empty | not | is possible  
Moreover, the objection which you, sir, make to `śūnyatā`:  
that consequence of fault is not ours; it is not possible in the empty.

24.14 sarvaṃ ca yujyate tasya śūnyatā yasya yujyate sarvaṃ na yujyate tasya śūnyaṃ yasya na yujyate  
*️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(sarva) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(tad) 🚺⨀①(śūnyatā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(yad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(sarva) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(tad) 🚻⨀①(śūnya) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀⑥(yad) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(yujyate)  
everything | and | is fitting | for one | emptiness | for whom | is fitting | everything | not | is fitting | for one | the empty | for whom | not | is fitting  
And everything is fitting for one for whom `śūnyatā` is fitting;  
nothing is fitting for one for whom the empty is not fitting.

24.15 sa tvaṃ doṣānātmanīyānasmāsu paripātayan aśvamevābhirūḍhaḥ sannaśvamevāsi vismṛtaḥ  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(yuṣmad) 🚹⨂②(doṣa) 🚹⨂②(ātmanīya) *️⃣👆⚧⨂⑦(asmad) 🚹⨀①🔽(at)(paripātayat) 🚹⨀②(aśva) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(abhirūḍha) 🚹⨀①🔽(at)(sat) 🚹⨀②(aśva) ⏏️(eva) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(asti) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(vismṛta)  
that | you | faults | your own | upon us | hurling | the horse | very | mounted on | being | the horse | very | you have | forgotten  
You, hurling your own faults upon us,  
have, while mounted on the very horse, forgotten the very horse.

24.16 svabhāvād yadi bhāvānāṃ sadbhāvamanupaśyasi ahetupratyayān bhāvāṃstvamevaṃ sati paśyasi  
🚹⨀⑤🆎(sva+bhāva) ⏏️(yadi) 🚹⨂⑥(bhāva) 🚹⨀②🆎(sad+bhāva) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(anupaśyati) 🚹⨂②🆎(na+hetu+pratyaya) 🚹⨂②(bhāva) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀①(yuṣmad) ⏏️(evam) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat) 🟢⨀🤘▶️(paśyati)  
from intrinsic nature | if | of existents | the real existence | you perceive | without primary cause and cause | existents | you | thus | being so | you see  
If you perceive the real existence of existents as from `svabhāva` (intrinsic nature),  
then, that being so, you see existents as without primary cause and cause.

24.17 kāryaṃ ca kāraṇaṃ caiva kartāraṃ karaṇaṃ kriyām utpādaṃ ca nirodhaṃ ca phalaṃ ca pratibādhase  
🚻⨀②(kārya) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀②(kāraṇa) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀②(kartṛ) 🚻⨀②(karaṇa) 🚺⨀②(kriyā) 🚹⨀②(utpāda) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀②(nirodha) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀②(phala) ⏏️(ca) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(pratibādhate)  
the effect | and | the cause | and | indeed | the agent | the instrument | the action | arising | and | cessation | and | the fruit | and | you reject  
Effect and cause, agent, instrument, action,  
arising and cessation and fruit, you reject.

24.18 yaḥ pratītyasamutpādaḥ śūnyatāṃ tāṃ pracakṣmahe sā prajñaptirupādāya pratipat saiva madhyamā  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀①🆎(pratītya+samutpāda) 🚺⨀②(śūnyatā) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀②(tad) 🔵⨂👆▶️(pracaṣṭe) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(tad) 🚺⨀①(prajñapti) 🔽(ya)(upādadāti) 🚺⨀①(pratipad) *️⃣🤟🚺⨀①(tad) ⏏️(eva) 🚺⨀①(madhyama)  
which | dependent origination | emptiness | that | we declare | that | a designation | in dependence | the way | that | itself | middle  
That which is `pratītyasamutpāda` (dependent origination), we declare that to be `śūnyatā`;  
that is a `prajñapti` (designation) in dependence; that itself is the `madhyamā pratipad` (middle way).

24.19 apratītyasamutpanno dharmaḥ kaścinna vidyate yasmāt tasmādaśūnyo hi dharmaḥ kaścinna vidyate  
🚹⨀①🆎(na+pratītya+samutpanna) 🚹⨀①(dharma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) ⏏️(yasmāt) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) ⏏️(hi) 🚹⨀①(dharma) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
not dependently arisen | phenomenon | any | not | is found | for which reason | for that reason | not empty | indeed | phenomenon | any | not | is found  
No `dharma` (phenomenon) whatever is found that has not dependently arisen;  
for which reason, indeed, no `dharma` whatever is found that is not empty.

24.20 yadyaśūnyamidaṃ sarvamudayo nāsti na vyayaḥ caturṇāmāryasatyānāmabhāvaste prasajyate  
⏏️(yadi) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(idam) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(sarva) 🚹⨀①(udaya) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(vyaya) 🚻⨂⑥(catur) 🚻⨂⑥🆎(ārya+satya) 🚹⨀①(abhāva) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate)  
if | not empty | this | all | arising | is not | not | passing away | of the four | realisations | the non-existence | for you | follows  
If all this is not empty, there is no arising, no passing away;  
the non-existence of the four `āryasatya` follows for you.

24.21 apratītyasamutpannaṃ kato duḥkhaṃ bhaviṣyati anityamuktaṃ duḥkhaṃ hi tat svābhāvye na vidyate  
🚻⨀①🆎(na+pratītya+samutpanna) ⏏️(kutas) 🚻⨀①(duḥkha) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+nitya) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)(ukta) 🚻⨀①(duḥkha) ⏏️(hi) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) 🚻⨀⑦(svābhāvya) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
not dependently arisen | how | suffering | will there be | impermanent | is said | suffering | for | that | in the state of intrinsic nature | not | is found  
How will there be `duḥkha` (suffering) that has not dependently arisen?  
For `duḥkha` is said to be impermanent; that is not found in the state of `svabhāva`.

24.22 svabhāvato vidyamānaṃ kiṃ punaḥ samudeṣyate tasmāt samudayo nāsti śūnyatāṃ pratibādhataḥ  
⏏️(svabhāvatas) 🚻⨀①🔽(māna)(vidyamāna) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) ⏏️(punar) 🔵⨀🤟⏭(samudeti) ⏏️(tasmāt) 🚹⨀①(samudaya) ⏏️(nāsti) 🚺⨀②(śūnyatā) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)(pratibādhat)  
by intrinsic nature | existing | why | again | will originate | therefore | the origin | does not exist | emptiness | for one who rejects  
What exists by `svabhāva`, why will that originate again?  
Therefore there is no origin for one who rejects `śūnyatā`.

24.23 na nirodhaḥ svabhāvena sato duḥkhasya vidyate svabhāvaparyavasthānānnirodhaṃ pratibādhase  
⏏️(na) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) 🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚻⨀⑥🔽(at)(sat) 🚻⨀⑥(duḥkha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚻⨀⑤🆎(sva+bhāva+paryavasthāna) 🚹⨀②(nirodha) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(pratibādhate)  
not | cessation | by intrinsic nature | existing | of suffering | is found | because of the fixation on intrinsic nature | cessation | you reject  
No cessation of `duḥkha` that exists by `svabhāva` is found;  
because of the fixation on `svabhāva`, you reject cessation.

24.24 svābhāvye sati mārgasya bhāvanā nopapadyate athāsau bhāvyate mārgaḥ svābhāvyaṃ te na vidyate  
🚻⨀⑦(svābhāvya) 🚻⨀⑦🔽(at)(sat) 🚹⨀⑥(mārga) 🚺⨀①(bhāvanā) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(upapadyate) ⏏️(atha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(adas) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(bhāvyate) 🚹⨀①(mārga) 🚻⨀①(svābhāvya) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)  
the state of intrinsic nature | when there is | of the path | development | not | is possible | but | that | is developed | the path | the state of intrinsic nature | for you | not | is found  
When there is the state of `svabhāva`, the development of the path is not possible;  
but if that path is developed, the state of `svabhāva` is not found for you.

24.25 yadā duḥkhaṃ samudayo nirodhaśca na vidyate mārgo duḥkhanirodhatvāt katamaḥ prāpayiṣyati  
⏏️(yadā) 🚻⨀①(duḥkha) 🚹⨀①(samudaya) 🚹⨀①(nirodha) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚹⨀①(mārga) 🚻⨀⑤🔽(tva)🆎(duḥkha+nirodha) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(katama) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(prāpayati)  
when | suffering | origin | cessation | and | not | is found | path | for the cessation of suffering | which | will bring to attainment  
When `duḥkha`, origin and cessation are not found,  
which path, for the cessation of `duḥkha`, will bring one to attainment?

24.26 svabhāvenāparijñānaṃ yadi tasya punaḥ katham parijñānaṃ nanu kila svabhāvaḥ samavasthitaḥ  
🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+parijñāna) ⏏️(yadi) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀⑥(tad) ⏏️(punar) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①(parijñāna) ⏏️(nanu) ⏏️(kila) 🚹⨀①🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀①🔽(ta)(samavasthita)  
by intrinsic nature | no full understanding | if | of it | later | how | full understanding | does not | indeed | intrinsic nature | stand firm  
If by `svabhāva` there is no full understanding of it, how could there later  
be full understanding? Does not `svabhāva` indeed stand firm?

24.27 prahāṇasākṣātkaraṇe bhāvanā caivameva te parijñāvanna yujyante catvāryapi phalāni ca  
🚻⨁①🆎(prahāṇa+sākṣātkaraṇa) 🚺⨀①(bhāvanā) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(evam) ⏏️(eva) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) 🔽(vat)(parijñā) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨂🤟▶️(yujyate) 🚻⨂①(catur) ⏏️(api) 🚻⨂①(phala) ⏏️(ca)  
giving up and personal realisation | development | and | in this way | just | for you | like full understanding | not | are fitting | the four | too | fruits | and  
Giving up and personal realisation, and development, in just this way for you,  
like full understanding, are not fitting, and the four fruits too.

24.28 svabhāvenānadhigataṃ yat phalaṃ tat punaḥ katham śakyaṃ samadhigantuṃ syāt svabhāvaṃ parigṛhṇataḥ  
🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚻⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+adhigata) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(yad) 🚻⨀①(phala) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(tad) ⏏️(punar) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①🔽(ya)(śakya) 🔽(tum)(samadhigacchati) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀②🆎(sva+bhāva) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)(parigṛhṇat)  
by intrinsic nature | unattained | which | a fruit | that | later | how | possible | to attain | could be | intrinsic nature | for one who holds to  
A fruit which by `svabhāva` is unattained, how could that later  
be attainable, for one who holds to `svabhāva`?

24.29 phalābhāve phalasthā no na santi pratipannakāḥ saṃgho nāsti na cet santi te’ṣṭau puruṣapudgalāḥ  
🚹⨀⑦🆎(phala+abhāva) 🚹⨂①🆎(phala+stha) ⏏️(no) ⏏️(na) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(asti) 🚹⨂①(pratipannaka) 🚹⨀①(saṃgha) ⏏️(nāsti) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(cet) 🟢⨂🤟▶️(asti) *️⃣🤟🚹⨂①(tad) 🚹⨂①(aṣṭan) 🚹⨂①🆎(puruṣa+pudgala)  
when there is no fruit | those abiding in the fruit | not | nor | exist | those who have entered upon the way | the Saṃgha | does not exist | not | if | exist | those | eight | individual persons  
When there is no fruit, those abiding in the fruit do not exist, nor those who have entered upon the way;  
there is no Saṃgha if those eight individual persons do not exist.

24.30 abhāvāccāryasatyānāṃ saddharmo’pi na vidyate dharme cāsati saṃghe ca kathaṃ buddho bhaviṣyati  
🚹⨀⑤(abhāva) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨂⑥🆎(ārya+satya) 🚹⨀①🆎(sad+dharma) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚹⨀⑦(dharma) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀⑦🔽(at)🆎(na+sat) 🚹⨀⑦(saṃgha) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(katham) 🚹⨀①(buddha) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(bhavati)  
because of the non-existence | and | of the realisations | the true dharma | too | not | is found | the dharma | and | when … does not exist | the Saṃgha | and | how | a Buddha | will there be  
And because of the non-existence of the `āryasatya`, the true `dharma` too is not found;  
and when the `dharma` and the Saṃgha do not exist, how will there be a Buddha?

24.31 apratītyāpi bodhiṃ ca tava buddhaḥ prasajyate apratītyāpi buddhaṃ ca tava bodhiḥ prasajyate  
🔽(ya)🆎(na+pratyeti) ⏏️(api) 🚺⨀②(bodhi) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀⑥(yuṣmad) 🚹⨀①(buddha) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate) 🔽(ya)🆎(na+pratyeti) ⏏️(api) 🚹⨀②(buddha) ⏏️(ca) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀⑥(yuṣmad) 🚺⨀①(bodhi) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(prasajyate)  
without depending on | even | awakening | and | for you | a Buddha | follows | without depending on | even | a Buddha | and | for you | awakening | follows  
It follows, for you, that there is a Buddha even without depending on awakening;  
it follows, for you, that there is awakening even without depending on a Buddha.

24.32 yaścābuddhaḥ svabhāvena sa bodhāya ghaṭannapi na bodhisattvacaryāyāṃ bodhiṃ te’dhigamiṣyati  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀①🆎(na+buddha) 🚹⨀③🆎(sva+bhāva) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🚹⨀④(bodha) 🚹⨀①🔽(at)(ghaṭat) ⏏️(api) ⏏️(na) 🚺⨀⑦🆎(bodhi+sattva+caryā) 🚺⨀②(bodhi) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(adhigacchati)  
one who | and | not a Buddha | by intrinsic nature | he | for awakening | striving | even | not | in the conduct of a bodhisattva | awakening | for you | will attain  
And one who by `svabhāva` is not a Buddha, even striving for awakening,  
will not, for you, attain awakening in the conduct of a bodhisattva.

24.33 na ca dharmamadharmaṃ vā kaścijjātu kariṣyati kimaśūnyasya kartavyaṃ svabhāvaḥ kriyate na hi  
⏏️(na) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨀②(dharma) 🚹⨀②🆎(na+dharma) ⏏️(vā) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(kaścit) ⏏️(jātu) 🟢⨀🤟⏭(karoti) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kim) 🚹⨀⑥🆎(na+śūnya) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(kartavya) 🚹⨀①🆎(sva+bhāva) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(kriyate) ⏏️(na) ⏏️(hi)  
not | and | dharma | adharma | or | anyone | ever | will do | what | of what is not empty | to be done | intrinsic nature | is made | not | for  
And no one will ever do `dharma` or `adharma`;  
what is to be done with what is not empty? For `svabhāva` is not made.

without | dharma | adharma | and | a fruit | for | for you | is found | occasioned by dharma and adharma | and | a fruit | for you | not | is found  
For a fruit is found for you without `dharma` and `adharma`;  
and a fruit occasioned by `dharma` and `adharma` is not found for you.

24.35 dharmādharmanimittaṃ vā yadi te vidyate phalam dharmādharmasamutpannamaśūnyaṃ te kathaṃ phalam  
🚻⨀①🆎(dharma+adharma+nimitta) ⏏️(vā) ⏏️(yadi) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate) 🚻⨀①(phala) 🚻⨀①🆎(dharma+adharma+samutpanna) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) *️⃣🤘⚧⨀④(yuṣmad) ⏏️(katham) 🚻⨀①(phala)  
occasioned by dharma and adharma | or | if | for you | is found | a fruit | arisen from dharma and adharma | not empty | for you | how | a fruit  
Or if a fruit occasioned by `dharma` and `adharma` is found for you,  
how is a fruit arisen from `dharma` and `adharma` not empty, for you?

24.36 sarvasaṃvyavahārāṃśca lokikān pratibādhase yaḥ pratītyasamutpādaśūnyatāṃ pratibādhase  
🚹⨂②🆎(sarva+saṃvyavahāra) ⏏️(ca) 🚹⨂②(lokika) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(pratibādhate) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚺⨀②🆎(pratītya+samutpāda+śūnyatā) 🔵⨀🤘▶️(pratibādhate)  
all conventions | and | worldly | you reject | who | the emptiness of dependent origination | you reject  
And you reject all worldly conventions,  
you who reject the `śūnyatā` of `pratītyasamutpāda`.

24.37 na kartavyaṃ bhavet kiṃcidanārabdhā bhavet kriyā kārakaḥ syādakurvāṇaḥ śūnyatāṃ pratibādhataḥ  
⏏️(na) 🚻⨀①🔽(tavya)(kartavya) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀①(kaścit) 🚺⨀①🔽(ta)🆎(na+ārabdha) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(bhavati) 🚺⨀①(kriyā) 🚹⨀①(kāraka) 🟢⨀🤟⏯(asti) 🚹⨀①🔽(māna)🆎(na+kurvāṇa) 🚺⨀②(śūnyatā) 🚹⨀⑥🔽(at)(pratibādhat)

There would be nothing to be done, action would be unbegun,  
an agent would be one not acting, for one who rejects `śūnyatā`.

24.38 ajātamaniruddhaṃ ca kūṭasthaṃ ca bhaviṣyati vicitrābhiravasthābhiḥ svabhāve rahitaṃ jagat

unborn | unceased | and | immovable | and | will be | of diverse | states | when there is intrinsic nature | devoid | the world  
Unborn and unceased and immovable will be  
the world, devoid of its diverse states, when there is `svabhāva`.

24.39 asaṃprāptasya ca prāptirduḥkhaparyantakarma ca sarvakleśaprahāṇaṃ ca yadyaśūnyaṃ na vidyate  
🚻⨀⑥🔽(ta)🆎(na+saṃprāpta) ⏏️(ca) 🚺⨀①(prāpti) 🚻⨀①🆎(duḥkha+paryanta+karman) ⏏️(ca) 🚻⨀①🆎(sarva+kleśa+prahāṇa) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(yadi) 🚻⨀①🆎(na+śūnya) ⏏️(na) 🔵⨀🤟▶️(vidyate)

And the attainment of what is unattained, and the act of ending `duḥkha`,  
and the giving up of all `kleśa` (defilements) are not found, if it is not empty.

24.40 yaḥ pratītyasamutpādaṃ paśyatīdaṃ sa paśyati duḥkhaṃ samudayaṃ caiva nirodhaṃ mārgameva ca  
*️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(yad) 🚹⨀②🆎(pratītya+samutpāda) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(paśyati) *️⃣🤟🚻⨀②(idam) *️⃣🤟🚹⨀①(tad) 🟢⨀🤟▶️(paśyati) 🚻⨀②(duḥkha) 🚹⨀②(samudaya) ⏏️(ca) ⏏️(eva) 🚹⨀②(nirodha) 🚹⨀②(mārga) ⏏️(eva) ⏏️(ca)  
one who | dependent origination | sees | this | he | sees | suffering | origin | and | indeed | cessation | the path | indeed | and  
One who sees `pratītyasamutpāda`, he sees this:  
`duḥkha` and origin and cessation and the path.
